# isibi-app

> **Read `docs/owner-notes.md` at the start of every session** — it's the owner's
> running bug log + how-they-like-things-done preferences. Keep it updated.

Zephyr — an AI image/video/voice generator at https://isibi.ai — **high-contrast LIGHT: white surfaces, near-black ink, black accents** (`--split` is a near-black gradient on buttons/active states), Space Grotesk wordmark. Black-and-white platform since 2026-07-23 (owner's call — see the comment at the top of `styles.css`, which is the authority). The old dark studio look and the pink → amber accents are gone; that brand gradient survives ONLY on the landing chatbox, as `--halo`. (This paragraph described the dark theme until 2026-07-28 — five days stale, and it misrendered a screenshot before anyone noticed.) **The SIGN-IN GATE and the auth-code EMAIL were still on the dark theme until 2026-07-30**, which is the same failure one layer down: the gate's scrim was `rgba(8,7,12,.68)` over a white page and its inputs were a 30%-black fill carrying near-black text, so it was a legibility bug rather than a stale preference — and none of it is visible from reading the CSS, only from rendering it. The email is worse to leave stale because it is the first thing a new customer sees and it is NOT token-driven: mail clients strip `<style>` blocks and several ignore custom properties, so every colour in `supabase/functions/send-email/index.ts` is inline and literal and has to be changed BY HAND whenever the brand does. (The old LaserFlow beam on the home thread was removed 2026-07-05.) Root (`/`) opens straight into the Zephyr chatbox behind a Supabase login gate; the workspace is a single page, view-switched. **The old Home landing was removed 2026-07-15 — the Builder chatbox is now the home screen** (`viewHome`). The sidebar is kept slim (chats only). The other views — Gallery/Avatar/Media Agent — are **top-bar tabs** (`data-act="view"`), with Integrations + Settings (both undocumented before) reachable from the top-right **profile-pop menu** (`toggleProfileMenu`). (**the Products feature was removed entirely 2026-07-16, owner's call** — page, scanner, presets, picker source; the `.pr-head`/`.pr-or`/`.pr-manual` header CSS pattern survives on the Avatar page.) (The old floating-logo menu — `#floatNav`/`toggleFloatMenu` — is gone; any lingering refs are dead.) `showView('landing')` now redirects to `'home'`; `renderLanding` is dead code. **Studio (the iMovie-style video editor) was removed 2026-07-12** — the platform is AI-native, so all video editing is moving into the Builder chatbox (attach a clip → say the change → an AI edit model does it). The on-device ffmpeg engine (`public/ffmpeg-edit.js`) was KEPT — the QR-burn feature runs on it.

## Structure

- Static frontend in `public/` (plain HTML/CSS/JS, no framework): `index.html` (the Zephyr chatbox — the only page), shared `styles.css` + `chat.js`, `auth.js` (Supabase email/password + email-code sign-in via GoTrue fetch, session in localStorage)
- `worker.js` — Cloudflare Worker: serves assets, `/api/video` + `/api/image` + `/api/audio` (fal.ai queue, per-kind model allowlists), `/api/direct` (director via ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, tool-use for structured output — Sonnet 5 rejects prefill; effort-routed: Haiku 4.5 writes Low/Medium compose/revise prompts AND the ask/error steps, Sonnet 5 handles High/Ultra/Max compose/revise (the `studio` step is dead code — Studio was removed, no client sends it); the ask step also returns `needsWeb`, and when true the frontend runs a `research` step — Sonnet 5 + Anthropic `web_search_20250305`, `max_uses:4`, ~$0.01/search — that returns a factual brief + sources folded into compose via `webFacts`, so "the newest X" gets depicted as the real current thing; gated behind that judgment so most generations never search), `/api/import/fetch` (gallery import-from-link: server-side fetch of a user-pasted URL behind auth + the SSRF guard/safeFetch, Chrome UA; direct image/video/audio → base64+kind back to the client; an HTML page runs `pageImageCandidates` — JSON-LD → og/twitter → microdata → link image_src → lazy-load/srcset `<img>` scan, candidates tried in order with retry — and when the free path comes up dry (bot wall or no image) it AUTO-FALLS-BACK to the paid AI lookup: Sonnet 5 + web_search finds direct image links / alternate open pages, **✦3 charged up front, refunded on any failure**, `scanai` quota 20/day, response carries `balance` for the pill; the client then pipes the base64 through the normal `/api/save` gates), `/api/video/poll` (proxies fal status/result so FAL_KEY stays server-side), `/api/save` (copies finished fal outputs into Supabase Storage `media` bucket using the caller's JWT). All `/api/*` require a Supabase-authenticated user (verified via GoTrue `/auth/v1/user`).
- Supabase project: fifa-tournament-hub (`ujrqdmmtcptvimazlhom`) — auth + public `media` storage bucket (INSERT-only RLS for authenticated users). Settings account controls: "Sign out on all devices" (GoTrue `logout?scope=global`) and "Delete account" (`delete_account()` RPC, SECURITY DEFINER auth-only — deletes chats/credits/usage_log/storage rows (via the `storage.allow_delete_query` GUC)/GoTrue child rows/auth user; **`purchases` are deleted too** — full wipe on account deletion is the owner's call (2026-07-15), so `purchases.user_id` cascades with the auth user by design; client wipes its storage files via the Storage API first, then every `zephyr_*` localStorage key)
- Chats persist in localStorage (`zephyr_chats_v1`, 30 chats × 80 msgs) AND sync cross-device to a Supabase `chats` table (pushChats/pullChats); media messages store the permanent Supabase Storage URL. **Avatars sync cross-device too (2026-07-16)**: one `user_assets` row per user (avatars jsonb, RLS own-row, FK-cascades on account deletion; the table's `products` column is orphaned since the Products removal — harmless, ignore it), whole-object last-writer-wins like `user_memory` (pushAssets debounced from saveAvatars, pullAssets at boot); images are compacted to ≤800px JPEG before push (hosted URLs pass through), so the originating device keeps full-res locally until another device edits
- **Media Agent** — Instagram/YouTube manager (Composio, `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` server-side). Analytics/Posts/Comments (read) + **comment auto-reply** (prompt-driven, cron every 2 min, Haiku drafts, `INSTAGRAM_POST_IG_COMMENT_REPLIES`) are live; DM auto-reply is blocked (Instagram `instagram_manage_messages` needs Meta App Review). **A "Schedule post" tab (Instagram only) was added 2026-07-17 — FRONTEND ONLY: compose media+caption+datetime → a local queue in `localStorage` (`zephyr_ig_scheduled_v1`), nothing publishes yet. Media is previewed locally (no upload). Backend wiring (Composio create-post at the scheduled time + persistence) is the pending next step — the composer carries a "Preview · not published yet" flag.** Config in `user_autoreply`, dedup in `autoreply_log`, cron gated by `AUTOREPLY_ALLOW`. **For tool permissions, rate limits, engine caps, cost, and the scaling concern → see `docs/media-agent.md` (pull it up when the user asks any of that).**
- **Universal memory** — a **backend/system feature with NO front-end** (user's call): auto-learned creative taste, applied to EVERY generation across all chats (not per-chat). It learns from BOTH generations and plain conversation: the **ask** step (runs on every chat message) returns an evolved `memory` list that commits immediately (no approval gate — chat itself is the signal), and the **compose/revise** `write_prompt` tool also returns one that commits on approval (`pendingMemory`→`commitMemory`, mirroring the brief). Both feed compose/revise/ask via `directorContext().memory` → `memoryLine` in the worker (≤12 short durable phrases, project-specifics excluded). Stored in `zephyr_memory_v1` + a per-user `user_memory` Supabase row (items jsonb, enabled bool, RLS own-row; pushMemory/pullMemory last-writer-wins; in `delete_account` + the local wipes). Gated off for audio. **No UI at all** — the Memory floating button and its `viewMemory` "space" page were removed; the feature is entirely invisible/backend.

## Deploy

Push to `main` → GitHub Actions → Wrangler → Cloudflare Workers → isibi.ai (+ www). Secrets in GitHub Actions: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `FAL_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (uploaded to the Worker each deploy). Work happens on a feature branch, merged to `main` via PR; deploys land in ~30-40s.

## Working rules

- **Always show UI changes as screenshots in the chat** (render the page headless and send the image) — the user reviews everything visually here.
- The user directs design; don't restyle beyond what's asked.
- Don't spend fal credits or Workers AI calls on tests without asking first.
- **THE WEBSITE BUILDER WAS DELETED 2026-07-27 (owner's call). It is being rebuilt from zero — do not restore any of it, and do not do builder work uninvited.** What went: all 368 files of the generation engine (the 258-component kit, the 5 starters, the capability registry, page recipes, both pipelines, the Lovable-clone pipeline, the eval harnesses, both build containers), the `/api/site/react-build` · `/api/site/react-revise` · `/api/site` POST handlers in `worker.js` (~1,340 lines), 17 test files and 13 CI workflows. **The only survivor is `builder/lovable/template/` — the shadcn UI, kept whole** (46 `src/components/ui/*.tsx` in new-york style, the shadcn theme in `styles.css`, `components.json`, plus the React 19 + Tailwind v4 + TanStack Router scaffold around it). The rebuild starts there and is **live end to end as of 2026-07-28** — brief → schema → Neon database → data API → generated pages → published React app (see the two bullets below).
- **Per-site data moved from Cloudflare D1 to Neon Postgres 2026-07-27 — D1 is gone from the repo entirely.** **ONE NEON PROJECT PER SITE as of 2026-07-29 (owner's call, was one per user)**, with the site's database inside it. Chosen over schema-per-tenant in the existing Supabase project because a Supabase project is one database, and over a Supabase-project-per-user because that bills per signup; Neon has no per-project fee and idle projects scale to zero. `site-db.mjs` = provisioning + the query layer (`sqlQuery`/`sqlExec`); `site-schema.mjs` = the schema engine. Both are plain modules, importable and tested outside the Worker. **`site_backends` now holds `neon_db` (a database name); the connection string lives in `user_site_project`, RLS on with NO policies, service-key only — it carries a PASSWORD, and `site_backends` has an "own read" policy that would have leaked it to any signed-in user.**
- **The old D1 site runtime was deleted 2026-07-27 (owner's call, data was demo-only).** All 37 `/api/site/*` and `/api/db/*` route handlers (20,569 lines) plus 626 orphaned helpers (4,577 lines) — `worker.js` went 31,585 → ~6,100 lines. Also gone: `test/backend` (386 files). What survived: the schema engine, `/s/<slug>/` serving from R2, and every core platform route. **R2 stays** — it holds published HTML served to the public and has no egress fees; Neon storage is ~23× the price and serving statics from it would keep computes awake and destroy the scale-to-zero economics.
- **`POST /api/site/react-build`** (also answers `/api/site/build` and `/api/site/react-revise`) — auth-gated, the builder's whole send path, and what `public/chat.js` already posts to. Idempotent (revise re-runs it); a slug is claimed by whoever built it first (409). The pipeline: brief → `designSiteSchema` (Sonnet 5, tool-use) → Neon database + `applySiteSchema` → `generateSitePages` (Sonnet 5, tool-use, same shape) → `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` in the **`SiteBuildContainer`** (`builder/Dockerfile`, binding `SITE_BUILD_CONTAINER`) → dist published to R2 at `/s/<slug>/`. Responds `{ok, slug, url, backend, brand, tables, schema, page, files, notes, problems, cost, buildMs}` — **`page` is `"app"` or `"placeholder"`**, so a fallback is never mistaken for a built site. **Page generation is best-effort by design**: it runs after the database is live, so a generator or compile failure leaves the caller a working backend plus the placeholder page rather than losing the build (and a *revise* whose pages fail leaves the already-published site alone — the placeholder is only written when nothing is published at that slug yet).
- **`DELETE /api/site/<slug>`** — owner-only; drops the site's Neon database, removes its published R2 objects, then deletes the `site_backends` row, **in that order** (the row is both the ownership record it authorises against and the last thing keeping a failed delete retryable; the DB drop is best-effort so a failure there can't leave the files up). Before this a site could only be REPLACED, never removed, so a slug whose row had gone kept serving a React shell whose every data call 404s. **An unknown slug is 404, not a permitted delete** — files can't be deleted by guessing slugs, which is asserted in the smoke test along with 401 for unauthenticated. `deleteSitePrefix` follows R2's list cursor and stops when the cursor is absent as well as when the page isn't truncated, so it can't spin; `writeSiteDistToR2` wipes through the same helper. **Consequence of that 404 rule:** an already-orphaned prefix has no row to authorise against, so it needs the operator sweeper — `.github/workflows/sweep-orphan-site.yml` (manual, takes a slug), which re-attaches the orphan to a throwaway account and deletes it through the same route rather than growing a second, untested deletion path. It refuses any slug that still has an owner.
- **`publicView` was declarable, enforced — and never STATED to the page generator (2026-07-29).** Rule 9 tells the model not to call `usePublicRows` on a table without one, but `schemaDigest` — "what the schema permits, stated so the model cannot get it wrong" — never said which tables had one, so the rule was conditioned on a fact the model was never given. Measured live: the generator correctly deduced `bookings` had none, could not build the taken-slots hint, and **the whole site came out as the placeholder over one optional enhancement**. Now: the digest prints `usePublicRows: YES/NO` for **every** non-`display` table (an absent line reads as an omission, not an answer) and names exactly the published columns; rule 9 says a NO means ship the ordinary form; and `lintPages` flags the call, the same class as its other schema-mismatch checks. `hasPublicView` lives in `site-access.mjs` so the lint and the data path ask ONE question — two copies drift into a page the lint passes and the API 404s, guarded by a test that reads all three files.
- **`usePublicRows` could not be called correctly at all (2026-07-29).** It was typed `<T extends Row>`, and `Row` requires `id: number` — the one field a `publicView` can NEVER carry, because `parseSchemaSpec` strips `id` and `owner_id` from a projection and the API selects only the declared columns. So an honest type was a compile error (`TS2344`, measured live) and the only type that compiled was a lie leaving `row.id` undefined at runtime — a React `key` of `undefined` on every row. Now `export type PublicRow = Record<string, unknown>` and `<T = PublicRow>`; the generator is told these rows have no `id` and to key on a published column. Guarded both ways: a test asserts the signature no longer demands `Row`, and another asserts `site-schema.mjs` still strips `id`, since `PublicRow` is typed on that premise.
- **The page generator** — `builder/GENERATOR.md` is the contract, `builder/lovable/template/src/routes/index.tsx` is the reference page it is derived from (**when the two disagree, the file wins**), and `builder/page-gen.mjs` is the model-facing half: the rules, the `write_pages` tool, `schemaDigest` (what the schema permits, stated so the model cannot get it wrong), `validatePages` (paths, dedup, an index is required) and `lintPages`. **The lint is the important one** — it catches, without running anything, the class of page that typechecks, bundles and then 403s: listing a `collect` table, submitting to a `display` one, naming a table the schema never declared, calling `fetch`, importing a ui component that does not exist, or reaching for `useUpdateRow`/`useDeleteRow` (the API refuses PATCH and DELETE). **There is NO repair pass — one model call a build (removed 2026-08-04, owner's call).** A compile error or a lint problem used to buy a second whole generation; output is 80% of what a build costs, so a failing build cost ~2x a working one, and a working one is already about break-even against the 22 credits charged. A compile failure now falls back to the placeholder and a lint problem is reported on a site that still publishes. **Billed only when a real app is published** (owner's call 2026-08-05) — metered on real token usage (~26 credits warm, 38 cold), gated by a floor (`MIN_CREDITS`) and not by the worst case, since a new account is granted 20. **A placeholder costs the customer nothing**, by all three routes to one (no pages, no home page, a compile failure): measured live, a build spent 68s, returned `pages: []` and took 23 credits off an account granted 20 — the same price as a working site, for nothing. The charge now runs AFTER `publish`, so a publish that throws cannot bill either; `usage` is still returned on every path, so what WE spent is visible even when nothing is billed. The schema call's flat 2 credits still applies — the database really is live and a revise reuses it. **The four token kinds are priced APART** — fresh input, output, cache read (0.1x) and cache write (1.25x) — from ONE table in `publish-pages.mjs` that the eval imports rather than restates. Summing them into one `usedIn` billed a cache read at the fresh rate, ten times over on the largest input component, and overcharged a warm build by 35%. `page-gen.mjs` is a plain module, so all of it is tested outside the Worker (`test/page-gen.test.mjs`, 35 tests) — including drift guards asserting its copies of the reference page and the ui-component list still match the files on disk.
- **Every `display` table is SEEDED at build time** (2026-07-28) — `seed` is a required field on the `design_schema` tool (3-6 realistic rows per display table), inserted by `seedSiteRows` in `site-schema.mjs` right after `applySiteSchema`. **This is not decoration:** nothing can write to a `display` table after a build — not a visitor, not the owner, there is no route — so an unseeded table is an empty list forever, and a form whose required Select reads that table renders with **zero options**, meaning nobody can submit it. Measured live 2026-07-28: every site the builder produced was a brochure with a dead form. Seeding is `display`-only (fabricating `collect` rows would put fake customers in the owner's booking list), skips any table that already has rows (so a revise cannot duplicate the menu), drops undeclared and managed columns the way the data API does, survives one bad row without losing the rest, and is capped at `MAX_SEED_ROWS` 12. Non-fatal — the build already succeeded by then. The response carries `seeded: {table: count}`. `test/seed-rows.test.mjs` (15 tests, all mutation-checked).
- **`builder/publish-pages.mjs`** is the other half — the orchestration that spends the money and picks what gets served: the credit floor, charge-after-the-call, and publish-or-placeholder. It lived inline in `worker.js` (which cannot be imported — believed unimportable at the time; CORRECTED 2026-08-13, it CAN be, though extracting it was still right for the injection it bought) and so ran untested; every side effect is now injected (`generate`/`compile`/`publish`/`readCredits`/`useCredits`) the way `site-data.mjs` takes its database functions, and `worker.js` keeps only the wiring that supplies the real ones. `test/publish-pages.test.mjs` (22 tests) drives the decisions against fakes — no model call, no container, no R2 — and each one was mutation-checked. The repair-pass tests were replaced by their inverse: that a SECOND call never happens, on every route that used to trigger one, asserted at the dep boundary as well as the branch. **A site with no `index.tsx` is refused rather than published** — `validatePages` only flags it (it cannot know which of five pages is home), the repair used to paper over it, and publishing anyway ships a site whose root URL renders nothing. `stage`/`error` are returned rather than only logged. The credit floor **fails closed** — an unreadable ledger means no paid call.
- **The build smoke test is 45 checks and names its own site.** It proves the whole path in one pass against the deployed Worker — brief → schema → Neon database → seeded rows → generated pages → compiled bundle → published site, then a real browser loads it, submits its form and the row lands in Postgres, then the owner deletes the site and the files are gone. **A green run is the strongest signal in the repo**; a `page=placeholder` result is generator variance (one call, then fallback by design) and the response now carries `stage`/`error` so which is which does not need a second run to find. A slug is claimed by whoever built it first, across every account, so letting the designer name the site from a fixed brief meant it kept proposing the same good name — and the moment any real user (or an earlier manual test) owned it, the build correctly answered 409 and the whole smoke run failed on something that was not a bug. It failed exactly that way 2026-07-28 against `sharp-fade-barbershop`. It now sends an explicit `smoke-<random>` slug: a test must not depend on a global namespace it does not control.
- **`test/integration/site-build.mjs` + CI workflow `site-build`** — GENERATOR.md's definition of done made repeatable, and $0: runs `builder/build-server.mjs` against a sandbox copy of the template and asserts a generated-shaped two-page site typechecks and builds, that a page with a type error is **refused** (`stage:"typecheck"`) rather than bundled, and that `src/routes` is reset between builds so one site's pages can never leak into another's. No model call, no container, no Neon project. **Note for deploys:** the image bakes the template's dependencies, so a deploy now builds two container images instead of one.
- **Postgres dialect notes (things that bit, or would have).** Triggers: SQLite inlines the body, Postgres needs a function per trigger — `pgTrigger()` in `site-schema.mjs` emits both. Postgres `WHEN` clauses cannot contain a subquery, so the `maxRows` count and the `enforceRefs` `NOT EXISTS` live in the trigger body as `IF … RAISE`. SQLite's `IS` is null-safe → `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM`. Timestamp columns are TEXT and app code parses SQLite's `"YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS"`, so defaults use `to_char(now() AT TIME ZONE 'UTC', …)` — bare `now()` is `timestamptz` and will not implicitly cast into TEXT at all. **Provisioning is async**: create-project returns when the work is SCHEDULED, and Neon rejects further calls while operations are in flight, so `waitForProject()` polls `/projects/{id}/operations` after creating a project AND after creating a database.
- **FTS is now a generated `tsvector` column + GIN index**, replacing the FTS5 virtual table and its three sync triggers — the database maintains it, so it cannot drift from the row. `to_tsvector` needs an explicit `'english'` regconfig to be IMMUTABLE enough for a generated column.
- **`noOverlap` IS a real `EXCLUDE USING gist` constraint** (`int4range(start,end) WITH &&`, plus `btree_gist` so the scope columns can use `=`) — the CLAUDE.md line claiming it was "still the hand-rolled INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS" and that a gist exclusion was "not yet done" was stale, corrected 2026-07-28. It **requires start/end to be INTEGER columns** and is SILENTLY SKIPPED otherwise (`console.error("noOverlap skipped")`), which is why the designer is told to use plain `unique` unless it has actually declared the integers. Partial UNIQUE indexes work too, and the partial form is what stops a CANCELLED booking holding a slot forever.
- **A whole tier of schema features is parsed, validated, persisted to `_meta` — and acted on by NOTHING** (surveyed 2026-07-28): `publicView`, `teamRead`, `transitions`, `sla`, `mask`, `fieldRoles`, `webhooks`, `geo`, `currency`, `formulas`, `roundRobin`, `assignBy`, `searchWeights`, `jsonShapes`, `checks`, `computed`, `defaultSort`. Their only appearance in `applySiteSchema` is the `norm.push` that copies them into `_meta`. `maskFields()` is even written and never called. The module's own header comment claims this is "where unique / noOverlap / publicView / teamRead / transitions / sequence / sla / trash actually live" — true for four of those, false for the other four. **Nothing can declare them** (the designer's tool never offered them), so no site is silently broken today — but do not assume a declared feature does anything without checking for its DDL.
- **What IS enforced**, verified by reading the DDL: `unique`, `uniqueCI`, `oncePerUser`, `maxRows`, `enforceRefs`, `noOverlap`, `sequence`, `trash`, `ordered`, `expires`, `pinnable`, `scheduled`, `version`, `sync`, `audit`, `history`, `archivable`, `fts`, `slug`.
- **The designer could not declare a single constraint until 2026-07-28.** `design_schema` offered exactly `name`, `access`, `columns`, `timestamps`, `fts` — so `unique`, `uniqueCI`, `maxRows` and `noOverlap` were fully implemented, tested, and unreachable on every site the builder has ever made. **Measured live: two customers booked the same 14:00 slot on a generated barber shop and both were accepted.** The tool now offers those four, with the booking case named explicitly (`unique` on a booking table is the headline; `noOverlap` only when integer start/end columns were actually declared, since it is silently skipped otherwise). Proven live the same day: second booking for a taken slot → **409 `duplicate`**, a different slot → 201.
- **`isibi.schema.json` is still the table-declaration format** — `parseSchemaSpec` + `applySiteSchema` live in `site-schema.mjs`, so a rebuilt generator that emits one gets tables, scoping, `unique`/`noOverlap`/`teamRead`/`transitions`/`sequence`/`sla`/`trash` for free. Proven against a real database by `test/integration/neon-e2e.mjs` (CI workflow `neon-e2e`, needs the `NEON_API_KEY` GitHub secret): it provisions a throwaway project, applies a schema, checks the triggers/FTS/guards actually fire, and deletes the project in a `finally`.

## A BAD EVAL RUN ATE THE CORPUS FOUR CHECKS MEASURE AGAINST (2026-08-20)

**Found while merging main: four floor guards were RED on main, for a reason unrelated to any change.** `page gen eval` `git add`s `docs/auth-audit/pages` wholesale every run, and a run whose menu and tool scenarios both failed committed the one site that worked and **DELETED the other two** — 8 pages across 3 sites became 4 across 1.

- **FOUR CHECKS READ THAT DIRECTORY AS A FIXED CORPUS while the eval owned it as per-run OUTPUT**, which is the coupling rather than the symptom. The nav slot-finder, the anchor rule and two order-lane checks measure their false-alarm rate against **the only pages in this repo the GENERATOR wrote** — as opposed to the 324 exemplars, which are pages WE wrote in the house style, so a rule tuned only on those is tuned to us rather than to the thing being linted.
- **THE FLOORS DID THEIR JOB, AND GOING RED EVERY UNLUCKY RUN IS STILL NOT A SIGNAL.** Most of those checks assert an ABSENCE, and an absence over a shrunken corpus is a weaker claim wearing the same green tick — so without the floors they would have gone quietly VACUOUS, which is worse. But a check that fails whenever the generator has a bad day is one everybody learns to ignore, which this file already records as its own failure mode.
- **THE FIX IS THE ONE ALREADY APPLIED TO THE 324 EXEMPLARS: the corpus is not the producer's output directory.** Pinned at `test/fixtures/generated/`, reached through one path (`test/fixtures/generated.mjs`), and refreshed by a deliberate copy from a good run rather than by whichever run happened to go last. `docs/auth-audit/pages/` is untouched and still the eval's rolling latest.
- **GUARDED BOTH WAYS, and the second half is what stops the coupling coming back**: the corpus must still hold 3+ sites, AND no test file may read the eval's output directory — a restored read is an innocent-looking path change that brings the whole thing back.
- **THE GUARD FLAGGED ITSELF ON ITS FIRST RUN**, because the literal it searches for necessarily appears in its own search line. Built at runtime from parts instead of self-exempting, the trick `test/source-bytes.test.mjs` already uses — a self-exemption is a hole somebody later widens. Ninth recorded instance of prose-or-code containing the thing it forbids.
- 3535 tests.

## THE PLATFORM COULD MAKE A SITE LOOK LIKE MARBLE AND NOT LIKE IT ANSWERS (2026-08-20)

**Counted across the whole theme engine: ONE keyframe — `isibi-ambient`, the background drift — and ZERO rules for `transition`, `hover`, `:focus`, `scroll-behavior` or `view-transition`.** So eighteen axes could give a site glass panels, an aurora wash, twenty textures and a drifting light, and none of them could make it feel quick, calm or alive, because nothing a visitor's POINTER or KEYBOARD touches was in a stylesheet the theme wrote.

- **IT WAS HARDCODED PER COMPONENT INSTEAD — 632 hover utilities baked into the kit**, `hover:bg-muted` 288 times and `hover:text-foreground` 165. Identical on a funeral director and a skate shop. Not a missing feature so much as a decision nobody was allowed to make.
- **FOUR AXES, AND THEY ARE THE SAFE HALF OF SEVEN CANDIDATES.** `motion` (none/calm/brisk/springy), `hover` (none/tint/lift/edge), `focus` (ring/bold/inset), `reveal` (none/fade/rise). Page transitions and scroll-snap are deliberately left out: the first needs router cooperation and uneven browser support, the second can trap a visitor mid-page.
- **A PSEUDO-CLASS ON A COMMA LIST ATTACHES TO THE LAST SELECTOR ONLY, and the first draft shipped exactly that.** `${BUTTON_SEL}:hover` emitted `[data-slot="button"], button.justify-center, a.x:hover` — so **every button on the site would have been permanently raised and shadowed**, and `focus: bold` a permanent 3px outline round every card and input. Valid CSS, compiles, bundles, renders. Found by READING the emitted rules, not by any test. `each(list, pseudo)` maps over the members, and the property is asserted on the module's output AND on the compiled bundle, since a minifier may merge selector lists.
- **`@media (hover: hover)` IS THE HALF THAT IS EASY TO SKIP.** On a touch screen `:hover` STICKS after a tap, so a lift stays on the last card somebody pressed until they press another — and most visitors to these sites are on a phone.
- **`reveal` CANNOT TRAP CONTENT INVISIBLE, and that is the whole design rather than caution.** The opacity-0 from-state exists ONLY inside `@supports (animation-timeline: view())` AND `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`, so no support or less motion means not one rule is emitted and the section is simply visible — the state it was in before the axis existed. `animation-range: entry` covers the load case: anything already on screen is past its entry range and renders at the `to` state.
- **`focus` HAS NO `none`**, because it is an accessibility decision wearing a taste one. Every option is a visible indicator; only its weight and offset move. `:focus-visible` and never `:focus`, or a mouse click leaves a ring behind and the site reads as broken to somebody who never used the keyboard.
- **REDUCED MOTION ZEROES THE DURATION rather than dropping the rule**, so the end state is identical and only the travel goes — a visitor who asked for less motion still sees the hover answer, instantly. And the transition list is EXPLICIT, never `transition: all`, which would animate layout and make every resize a slideshow.
- **SCOPED TO THE STABLE HOOKS AND NOTHING WIDER.** These are unlayered rules and Tailwind's utilities are in `@layer utilities`, so they win — which is the `display` axis's own mechanism and exactly why the selector must be narrow. Widened to "everything", the hover tint would override the one a dropdown uses to show which row is under the cursor.
- **TWO EXISTING GUARDS CAUGHT MY OWN WORK, which is them earning their keep**: a `said` name over 20 characters, and the derived options map that requires every axis to be the engine's own list in both directions.
- 22 axes now. **PROVEN IN A REAL BROWSER**: at rest `transform: none`, on hover `matrix(1,0,0,1,0,-2)`, transition `0.12s`, focus outline `3px` — and photographed, with only the pointed-at card moving.

## THE TAB ICON WAS A COLOUR NOBODY CHOSE (2026-08-20)

**The designer writes three colours, the engine derives thirty-one from them, and the favicon — the most-repeated mark of the brand, in the tab, the bookmark bar and the phone home screen — was a hue hashed from the business NAME.** Measured: *"Fade & Co Barbershop"* hashes to 273, a **purple** tab icon on a warm-brick site whose own accent is hue 36. Every site on the platform, matching nothing on it.

- **`brandHue` WAS THE WHOLE ANSWER AND STOPPED BEING ONE.** It is stable per business and distinct between two of them, which is the job of a tab icon — and it was written when nothing else about a site was authored. The seeds gave the platform a palette and nobody went back. Same shape as the `style` clause in the entry below: a derivation that made sense until the layer under it moved.
- **OKLCH RATHER THAN THE OLD `hsl()`, and that is a legibility improvement rather than a tidy-up.** The mark pins its lightness so white initials always clear contrast, and how much contrast a fixed lightness BUYS depends on the hue. Swept over all 360 hues: `hsl(h 62% 34%)` bottoms out at **3.55:1**, the OKLCH form at **6.95:1** across every hue AND every chroma up to the cap. Strictly better everywhere, and the mark is now in the same colour space as the palette it comes from.
- **I NEARLY FILED A FALSE BUG AND THE INSTRUMENT IS WHY.** The first sweep said the old form was illegible at a third of all hues; `contrast()` in `site-theme.mjs` takes **OKLCH arrays**, not 0-255 RGB, so every number was garbage. Caught by asking it for a known answer — `contrast(white, black)` came back **6.24** where it must be 21. **A harness that cannot fail honestly is worse than none**, and the check is now the first assertion in the test. The old form's own claim held: 3.55 worst, nothing below 3.0, which is the bar a 32px mark in bold caps has to clear.
- **A CEILING, NOT A FIXED VALUE, and only a render found it.** Pinning outright made a deliberately near-black brand (`Noir`, accent L 0.14) come out **MID-GREY** — lighter than the identity it is meant to be. Darker only ever increases contrast against white, so `min(accentL, MARK_L)` is safe by construction and truer to the brand: measured, Noir goes to **17.9:1** and keeps its own weight. The whole set now runs 7.5–17.9:1.
- **THE ACCENT'S OWN CHROMA IS KEPT, capped rather than replaced**, so a neutral brand gets a near-grey mark — that is their identity, not a failure to pick a colour, and the sweep covers chroma 0.
- **HEX, NOT `oklch()`, because a favicon is not only rendered by a browser.** Bookmark managers, OS home-screen shortcuts and link unfurlers read this document too, and the older notation is the one that cannot be got wrong.
- **`site-identity.mjs` STOPS BEING A LEAF, and that is the cost, stated.** It imports `site-seeds.mjs` and `site-theme.mjs`; neither imports it, so there is no cycle, and both are plain modules so nothing here becomes harder to drive.
- **THE WIRING IS THE HALF THAT KILLS THIS**, so it is guarded at both ends: `writeSiteBrand` must take the seeds and the call site must pass `payload.seeds`. `markGround` can be perfectly correct and never reached, and the only symptom is a tab icon in a colour nobody chose — which is the bug, restored silently.
- **AN EXISTING GUARD WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE, and its own comment warns about exactly that, twice.** It pinned `initialsMark(title)` verbatim AND windowed the branch in `{0,200}` bytes, which my comment outran. Re-anchored on the branch's own close and on the ARGUMENTS. This repo's most-repeated own-goal, third instance this session.
- **TWO SWEEP SURVIVORS, AND MEASURING SEPARATED THEM.** The chroma cap is **REACHABLE** — 38 of the 500 pinned palettes carry an accent above it, up to 0.220 — and is **NOT** a safety bound, since white clears 5.36:1 even at chroma 0.37; what it bounds is how saturated a 32px square gets, which no contrast test can see, so it is asserted directly along with its own reachability (or the check goes vacuous the day the palette engine starts clamping below it). The finite-accent check **CANNOT FIRE** — `normalizeSeeds` refuses a bad accent before this reads one, measured — so it is kept and **said so in the source**, because that guarantee lives in another module and what it would otherwise produce is `NaN` in a customer's tab icon.
- 3557 tests, **8/8 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against it.

## THE MODEL AUTHORS THE PALETTE AND NOT THE LOOK — A CLAUSE THAT OUTLIVED ITS PREMISE BY HOURS (2026-08-20)

**The designer writes three colours per site and the other EIGHTEEN decisions a design makes were the template's plain defaults on every site since the registry went, because one sentence in a tool description told the model they were already decided.** *"Omit it entirely otherwise — on a first build the theme already decides all of these."*

- **IT WAS TRUE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN AND THE THING THAT MADE IT TRUE WAS DELETED THE SAME DAY.** Every one of the 500 registry rows carried all eighteen axes (`corner`, `scale`, `tracking`, `leading`, `weight`, `density`, `width`, `border`, `icon`, `shadow`, `buttons`, `inputs`, `display`, `surface`, `backdrop`, `decor`, `ambient`, `skin`), so a designer NAMING a theme really did pick them. An authored theme is `{label, light, dark}` — measured: **`applyStyle(theme, undefined)` attaches 0 of 18.** Same shape as the `#/` href rule and the `fonts` field one section up: **a rule true because of something one layer down expires when that layer moves, and nothing announces it.**
- **IT COSTS NO NEW TOKENS TO ASK FOR, which is what makes this a description change rather than a feature.** ONE tool serves the build and the revise — line 4335 swaps only `required` — so the eighteen axes and their 66 options (**~1,921 characters of enums**) were already in front of the model on every build. It read them and was told not to use them. The description itself grew 486 → 1,028 characters, **~199 tokens on the cached block**, and the credit floor does not move: the schema call prices at **12 credits either way**, `buildFloor(sonnet)` stays 20.
- **AND THE CAP WAS THE HALF THAT WOULD HAVE MADE THE FIX INERT.** `MAX_STYLE` is 6, and it keeps whichever six `AXES` happens to declare FIRST — measured, an eighteen-axis answer came back as `corner, scale, tracking, leading, weight, density` with the buttons, the inputs, the shadows, the icon weight and the whole world layer silently dropped. Worse than silent: `styleNote` would have told the customer we refused twelve axes on a build where nothing was refused for any reason they could act on.
- **THE CAP'S OWN PARAGRAPH SAYS IT IS A REVISE RULE.** *"A customer moving half the look is not asking for an adjustment to the theme they have, they are telling us the theme is wrong."* On a first build there is no theme they are on — the designer is stating what the look IS, so there is nothing to be moving half of and nothing to hide. `MAX_STYLE_BUILD` is **derived from `ASKABLE.length`**, so it is a no-op by construction rather than a generous number that goes stale the day a nineteenth axis lands, and it is written as a cap so both call sites read the same way.
- **THE BOUND HAD TO BE THREADED INTO BOTH INNER `parseStyle` CALLS, and a mutant proves it.** `mergeStyle` calls `parseStyle` on each side and `parseStyle` applies its OWN cap — so a build passing the wide bound to the merge alone would have had the patch cut to six before the merge ever saw it, and the widening would read as done while doing nothing. Same for the route's own `parseStyle(designed.style)`, or the note and the stored patch disagree about what was kept.
- **`existing` IS THE FREE SIGNAL**, already read for the ownership check three statements above — so a first build authors and a revise still patches, with no new lookup and no new field on the wire. **The look edit lane is asserted NOT to take the build cap, separately**, because one `mergeStyle` call site carrying it satisfies a check written for the other.
- **AND `applyStyle` WAS THE THIRD PLACE THE CAP LIVED — the one NOTHING but the container could see.** It runs in the build service, downstream of the Worker's decision, and re-parsed at the revise cap: so the Worker would have stored eighteen, sent eighteen, and had twelve thrown away there. Measured against a real compiled stylesheet: `stroke-width: 2` (the theme's own value, not the `icon: heavy` asked for) and **no world layer at all**. `explicitRadiusCss` had it worse — `AXES` declares `buttons` eleventh and `inputs` twelfth, so the re-cap cut BOTH before the function looked for them and it returned the empty string, silently un-fixing the corner collision on exactly the widest patches. **3552 unit tests were green with the whole feature dead in production**; only running the container found it, for the thirteenth recorded time at that layer.
- **CAPPING THE MERGED TOTAL WAS A WORSE BUG THAN THE ONE BEING FIXED, and the sweep is what surfaced it.** `mergeStyle` bounded the whole accumulated look, which was indistinguishable from bounding the patch for as long as nothing could author more than six. Measured: **a site built with all eighteen and then asked for round buttons came back with SIX** — density, width, border, icon, shadow, inputs, display, surface, backdrop, decor, ambient and skin gone, the whole visual identity stripped to the template's defaults on the customer's first edit, silently, reported as a success. `MAX_STYLE`'s own paragraph says what it bounds: *"a customer MOVING half the look"* — a fact about ONE INSTRUCTION. The prior is parsed whole and only `next` is capped; growth is bounded anyway, because there are only eighteen axes.
  - **THE DEDUP-BEFORE-THE-CAP MACHINERY WENT WITH IT**, and that is the better fix for the failure it was written for: it existed only to make a total cap correct, and with nothing to make room for the question disappears.
  - **THREE TESTS PINNED THE OLD RULE and each was asserting an earlier instruction being dropped.** Rewritten rather than deleted, because the rule they held was right on its own premise.
- **A MUTANT SURVIVED BECAUSE MY FIXTURE WAS TOO SMALL TO REACH ITS OWN CASE.** The kept-whole test used a prior of exactly `MAX_STYLE`, so capping the prior at the revise cap was a no-op and the mutation reintroducing the shredding bug passed. Built from every axis the site could have now, with the size asserted first.
- **EVERY ONE OF THE EIGHTEEN MOVES THE STYLESHEET, verified rather than assumed** — driven axis by axis against a bare seeds theme, all 18 change `themeCss` on at least one option, and 30 lines differ between a bare build and a fully authored one. A first draft's "40 characters differ" was misleading: **the axes REPLACE values rather than adding lines**, so a length delta understates them and a line diff does not.
- **DRIVEN THROUGH THE REAL CONTAINER**, because the widest patch it had ever received was seven axes and the count is exactly what changed. One assertion per LAYER — a token axis, a rule axis, the corner shape, and the world layer — since a single check on the stylesheet's length passes with any one of them silently dropped. **My own first draft of that control was wrong**: it compared lengths against a build that carries a style patch AND a token override while the message called it "the same theme with no patch". Re-anchored on `isibi-ambient`, whose control provably has none.
- **NO NEW LEGIBILITY RISK, measured over the 500 pinned themes rather than reasoned.** The only axis that moves a colour is `display`, and `displayColor` already drags the accent to 4.5:1 against the paper. `surface: glass` on a plain page is the one coupled option and `applyStyle` has satisfied it automatically since the world axes landed.
- 3554 tests, **`site build` 185/185 against the real container** — the largest count that harness has run, and the five eighteen-axis checks were every one of them red or vacuous before the `applyStyle` fix. **18/18 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against it, so nothing has yet been authored by a real designer.

## THE 500 THEMES ARE DELETED — THE MODEL AUTHORS THE PALETTE (2026-08-20, owner's call)

**"Get rid of the 500 themes and have the model make its own theme while building."** The designer used to name one of a 100-strong shortlist out of a 500-row registry; it writes **three anchor colours** per site now and the engine derives the other 28 tokens exactly as it always did. Same act as the families one section down, one layer over.

- **THE SEAM WAS ALREADY EXACTLY THERE, which is why this is small.** Every one of the 500 declares only `paper`/`ink`/`accent` per mode and lets `paletteFor` derive the rest — so the registry was never a store of palettes, it was a store of nine numbers and a name. `themeCss` has taken a theme OBJECT since it was written. **Measured: a bare seeds-only theme produces a complete 4,518-character stylesheet with 96 token declarations and a real `.dark` block**, and `applyStyle` composes the 17 style axes on top of it unchanged.
- **THREE COLOURS, NOT THIRTY-ONE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** The 28 derived tokens are where every legibility guarantee lives — `foregroundFor` picks a readable text colour per surface, `fitState` drags a state colour to 4.5:1, `separateFromAccent` stops a brand red reading as the destructive red, `worldBorderColor` keeps a divider darker than its ground. Letting a model write those directly throws all of it away for nothing anybody can see, so the field's description tells it not to try.
- **HEX, NOT OKLCH, AND THAT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN PREFERRED.** Round-tripping all 3,000 seeds of the 500 through hex moves L or C by at most **0.058** and the resulting body contrast by at most **0.117**, on ratios that run 10 to 20 — and every shipped colour is clamped into sRGB by `oklchToRgb` regardless, so nothing that reaches a screen is lost. What it buys: a model writes `#b44a2e` fluently and `[0.48, 0.14, 28]` badly, where a transposed pair is a silent in-range mistake. It is also the notation the rest of the platform already speaks — `site-tokens.mjs`'s customer-facing hint is `#rrggbb`, and two colour notations in one design call is a way to mix them up.
- **THE FLOORS ARE CHECKED ON THE DERIVED PALETTE, NEVER ON THE SEEDS**, because that is what ships. Each is set below the worst of the 500, so it refuses nothing anybody designed: body text **7** (hand-written min 10.67), text on cards **7** (10.43), quiet text **3** (3.55), button labels **3** (3.47). **3.0 and not 4.5 for the muted pair is deliberate and consistent** — `site-render.mjs` uses 3.0 for the same reason, since every theme's caption and placeholder sits in the 3-4.5 band on purpose.
- **A FIFTH FLOOR EXISTS BECAUSE THE FIRST DRAFT LET AN INVISIBLE BUTTON THROUGH.** A near-white accent gives a button whose LABEL is perfectly legible — `foregroundFor` picks the dark ink and reports 15.4:1 — while the button is the same colour as the page. Measured at **1.10:1 against the paper**. No label-contrast check can see it, so `primary` vs `background` is its own floor at **1.5**, which is very low on purpose: a button carries a border and a shadow, a quiet ghost button on near-white paper is real design, and the lowest of the 500 is 1.98.
- **THE ONE FAILURE CONTRAST CANNOT SEE AT ALL: swapped modes.** A dark light-palette passes every floor and renders dark when the visitor asked for light. Checked separately, in both directions.
- **`dark` IS OPTIONAL AND THE MIRROR IS EMPIRICAL, not invented.** 391 of 500 are exact hue mirrors, and the offsets are the medians of what those authors did: `dark.paper` = `light.ink` at L−0.07, `dark.ink` = `light.paper` at L−0.03, `dark.accent` = `light.accent` at L+0.17 with chroma unchanged (median ratio 1.000). **Verified rather than assumed: applied to all 500 light halves the derived dark mode's worst body text is 12.27:1, worst quiet text 4.46:1, worst button label 3.77:1.**
- **A NEUTRAL ACCENT FLIPS INSTEAD OF LIFTING, and every author agreed.** A brutalist theme's brand colour IS black; +0.17 gives a near-black button on a near-black page (1.4:1, correctly refused). All **11** themes with a near-colourless accent put it on the dark side in light mode, all 11 moved it 0.45-0.87 in dark, and all 11 land within 0.13 L of their own dark ink — so a colourless accent is a value on the ink-to-paper scale and flips when the scale does. **The derivation lands within 0.093 of what eleven people independently chose, median 0.04, one exactly on it.**
- **THE FALSE-ALARM RATE HAD TO BE ZERO BEFORE THE VALIDATOR COULD EXIST**, the `lintPages` bar. All 500 re-expressed as hex seeds and driven through `normalizeSeeds`: **0 refused with an authored dark half, 0 refused with the mirror.**
- **THE NAME CAN CLOSE THE CSS COMMENT IT IS WRITTEN INTO.** `themeCss` emits `/* theme: <name> …` as the first line, so a terminator in a model-written name makes everything after it live CSS on a customer's site. An ALLOW-LIST, never an escape — and **Unicode letters, not `A-Za-z`**, or "Café Noir" becomes "Caf Noir" and a Greek or Cyrillic name becomes nothing: the exact bug the favicon initials shipped once. `\p{L}\p{M}\p{N}` admits every script and no format character, so the bidi overrides stay out with the asterisk. The em-dash is excluded because `themeCss` splits a label on one.
- **IT REFUSES RATHER THAN REPAIRS, and the reason travels.** Nudging a bad seed until it clears a floor produces a palette nobody chose on a site that reports the theme it asked for. Four failures need four responses — nothing sent, an unreadable colour, swapped modes, an illegible palette.
- **`fonts` BECAME REQUIRED IN THE SAME CHANGE, and that assertion has now been inverted twice.** `themeFontPair` read a curated pairing off whichever registry theme was named, which is why the field's description said to leave it out. With no registry there is nothing to read, so "usually omit" would put every such site on the template's default face. Each position was right on its own premise; the premise is what moved.
- **THE REGISTRY SURVIVES AS `test/fixtures/themes/`, OUT OF THE PRODUCT** — the same call as the 324 corpus pages, for the same reason. It reaches no prompt, no site can name one, the Dockerfile no longer copies it, and it ships in nothing; what it still is, is the only corpus of hand-designed palettes there is, and **every number above is calibrated against it.** Deleting it would not advance the goal, it would make the floors unre-verifiable. `THEME_SHORTLIST` and `themeFontPair` went with the feature rather than staying as dead exports. One path, `test/fixtures/themes.mjs`.
- **`+42 TOKENS` on the cached block**, measured — the field replaced a 100-name enum of almost the same size, and it is a cache read at 0.1x on every build after the first.
- **THREE REAL BUGS CAUGHT BY DERIVED GUARDS ON THE WAY IN, none by reading.** `site-seeds.mjs` was not COPYd into the build image, so the service could not have started. `buildAndPublishPages` was handed `seeds` and still destructured `theme`, so every build would have shipped the default look silently. And `currentStateNote` never showed the designer the site's CURRENT palette — the widest possible door for the re-roll anchoring the look exists to stop, since a fresh answer there changes every colour on every page at once.
- **AND A FOURTH THE API-SCHEMA CHECK CAUGHT, whose own comment says "third time this stub has been too simple".** This was the fourth: the stub cannot stand in for a schema FRAGMENT, so `design_schema.seeds` read as a Proxy with no `type` and would have been checked by nothing — precisely the gap that let `unique`'s empty `items` through and took the builder down for three merges. Named fragments are bound for real now, which silently un-stubbed `PLAN_FIELDS` too.
- **TWO OF MY OWN GUARDS HAD RECORDED TRAPS.** A scope scanner matched only `key: value` and not ES6 shorthand, so it accused correct code of a missing binding — a false alarm, which this repo rates worse than a miss. And an absence check for `themeFontPair` matched the comment in `worker.js` recording that it had been removed: **prose explaining a deletion spells the deleted thing**, for at least the seventh time here.
- **ONE MUTATION SURVIVOR WAS REAL AND ONE WAS MY OWN INERT MUTANT.** `"quiet text"` was a prefix of `"quiet text on the page"`, so the derived floor check was satisfied by the wrong entry and one floor was covered by nothing — the substring-versus-token bug again, and a reporting bug in its own right, since two floors sharing a name give the customer two sentences they cannot tell apart. The other replaced the floor list with `[].concat([]) && [...]`, which returns the original array because an empty array is truthy: **the fifteenth recorded inert mutant.**
- **AND ONE HAZARD THE AUTHORED PALETTE INTRODUCED, found by asking what a bad answer does.** A theme was a NAME from an enum, so an invalid one was impossible; three hex colours can be perfectly well-formed and illegible. Counting that as an answer means it REPLACES the stored palette, the container refuses it on every publish thereafter, and the site is stuck on the default look until somebody happens to ask for a different colour — silent, and persistent. `hasValue` asks whether a palette is USABLE rather than merely complete, through the same `normalizeSeeds` (one validator asked a different question at a different moment, never a rival ruling). A first build with a bad palette stores nothing rather than storing a fault.
- **THE CONTAINER RUN'S THREE FAILURES WERE THE TEST'S FAULT, NOT THE CODE'S, and the distinction is the lesson.** Four checks assert an override REPLACES *the site's own answer* — its corner rules give way to a radius, its icon weight to a heavier one — and the first draft of the fixture helper stopped sending the fixture theme's style axes, so there was no answer to replace and the checks reported a working mechanism as broken. The palette and the 17 axes are two separate authored things in the product; sending both from one fixture row ALSO meant a check that then set `style: {width:"wide"}` replaced the axes wholesale, so its two builds differed in more than the axis under test. `HOUSE_STYLE` is the site's own baseline, named once.
- **AND THE CONTAINER RUN FOUND A MECHANISM THAT QUIETLY WENT INERT — reported, not deleted.** `stripThemeRadius` exists because "280 of the 500 themes hard-set `border-radius` on buttons and inputs as real rules", so a corner override moved the cards and left every button square. That was true of the REGISTRY. Measured since: **a seeds-only theme emits ZERO border-radius rules**, so with no style patch there is nothing to strip — and `RADIUS_AXES` is exactly `["buttons","inputs"]`, exactly what `explicitRadiusCss` re-emits, so with one the strip removes precisely what is put straight back. **A wash either way, and the check that told us was a silent 33-vs-33.** The mechanism is left in place (provably harmless, and folding an unrelated removal into the palette work makes both harder to review) and its COMMENT is corrected, because a false comment is what gets believed. On the list as a decision rather than an oversight.
- **AND `asHex` WAS DELETED WITH THE COMMENT BLOCK AROUND IT** while editing that helper — a `ReferenceError` on every call that `node --check` passes happily. The `vidRefN` class, in a test file, caught by looking rather than by the parser.
- 3540 tests, **27/27 mutations as expected** across three sweeps from a verified-green baseline, with a comment-only control that had to survive. Every layer below the model call was driven against the real derivation and the real container — **`theme seam` 11/11 and `site build` 180/180**, the latter printing `corner rules: 33 with a radius, 33 without` as the standing measurement of the inert strip.
- **PROVEN LIVE 2026-08-20, run `32350969314`: a model authored its own palette and the site published and served.** *Fade & Co Barbershop* — three anchor colours written by the designer, the other 28 tokens derived, and a real Chromium loaded the result with no console errors. **`site_look` is 311 bytes against 276 the run before**, which is the seeds being stored rather than a theme name. **38 credits** (13 schema + 25 pages), both calls cold; the run before was 33 with the pages call warm, so the **+42 tokens** measured on the tool schema is not what moved it.
  - **AND THE `[skip smoke]` MARKER IS PROVEN TOO, in both directions.** The merge carried it and all four paid workflows concluded `skipped`; the commit after it did not and `build smoke` alone fired. Worth knowing because a marker that silently stops working looks exactly like a marker that works. **`workflow_dispatch` answers 403 to a session, so a push is the only way to fire one** — which means a build cannot be spent on nothing: the commit that triggers it has to carry real work.
  - **THEN 105/0 ON RUN `32359957809`, the first fully green run since the seeds landed and the largest check count this file has ever run.** Same path, plus: `/book` had its own form and its own card, seven API calls including the `POST` that landed a row, and the new per-route error check passed. So the previous run's `/book` failures were GENERATOR VARIANCE and not a broken pipeline — measured rather than assumed, which is what the whole round of fixes was for.
  - **THE REVISE WENT LIVE IN 244ms**, worker confirmed in 542, against `5818ms` the run before and `NEVER, budget spent` before that. Third data point, and it settles it: those failures were propagation.

## RULE 19 WAS IN THE PROMPT FOR TWO DAYS AND OBEYED BY NOTHING (2026-08-20)

**Every page of every site shared one link preview and one search result.** `/book` and the home page came back with byte-identical titles AND og:descriptions on a site that published perfectly — so a booking page pasted into WhatsApp previewed as the front page, and a search for "book a table" showed the site's blurb.

- **THE RULE EXISTS, IS EMPHATIC, AND CARRIES A WORKED EXAMPLE.** It landed 2026-08-18, and three of the four reference pages in the same prompt demonstrate it. **Measured over the pinned generator corpus — written by a run on 2026-08-20, so with the rule in front of it — 5 of 5 non-home pages have no `head`.** Not one unlucky run: the rule had never been obeyed.
- **A LINT AND NOT A SIXTH PARAGRAPH.** This file already records five prompt rewrites spent on a symptom, and the answer there was to check the mechanism. `__root.tsx` supplies the site-wide title as a DEFAULT and a route's own `head()` overrides it — so a missing `head` is a syntactic fact about the page, invisible to `tsc`, to vite and to every other check, and exactly what `lintPages` is for.
- **ANCHORED AFTER `createFileRoute(` AND DELIBERATELY LOOSE**: a `head:` anywhere after it counts, so a page whose COMPONENT happens to contain the word passes when it should not. The safe direction on purpose — a false alarm teaches the model away from a rule it is following, which this repo rates worse than the miss. **Its first live outing was a clean pass**, which is the number that had to come back zero before it could exist.
- **THE 324 EXEMPLARS CANNOT CALIBRATE THIS ONE, and saying so is the point.** They flag 222 of 224 — they predate the rule by months and reach no prompt now, so that number is evidence about the corpus's age and not a false-alarm rate. The 4 REFERENCE pages are the corpus that matters here (they are what the model is shown) and they are clean.
- **TWO CHECKS THAT ONLY EVER WATCHED THE HOME PAGE.** `pageErrors`/`consoleErrors` fill for the life of the page and keep filling as the form walk navigates — and both assertions run ABOVE the walk, so everything `/book` threw was captured and never looked at. "The page is thin" and "the page is erroring" produced one line. Attributed per route now, because a count says a page broke and a name says which.
- **AND THE SCREENSHOT WAS CAPTIONED AS A PAGE IT WAS NOT.** The go-home before `smoke-site.png` hung off `formRoute` being set, so on the one run where no form was found the walk's last page — `/manage`, reading *"This page needs the link from your confirmation"* — was shot and labelled *"live at &lt;site&gt;/"*. The comment above it already called that class of thing a small lie; it was gated on the wrong condition.
- **THE REVISE'S OWN DIAGNOSTIC REPORTED A CONSTANT.** `Object.keys(tokenAsk)` where `tokenAsk` is the `{tokens, dropped}` WRAPPER — the line below it proves that, calling `tokenNote(tokenAsk.tokens, tokenAsk.dropped)` — so the field was `["tokens","dropped"]` on every build ever made. Worse than wrong: it exists so *"the model never asked"* and *"it asked and we lost it"* stop being one observation, and both keys are always present, so it was never empty and could never say the first. **`askedNames` takes the SAME ARGUMENTS as `tokenNote`**, one line apart, so a refactor cannot reintroduce the wrapper. Proven live the same day: `tokens=["background","radius"] style=[]`, where the empty list is the field finally able to say nothing was asked.
- **THE BLOCK-SCOPE SCANNER FALSE-ALARMED ON A REGEX**, fifth time from that scan and the first where its quote-counting could not help: `/<body[^>]*>/` read a tag name as a variable and blamed a `const body` far above. Narrowed to `<` against the name — the tag shape in a regex, a string and JSX alike — and driven both ways.
- **THREE OWN-GOALS IN ONE SITTING, all recorded shapes.** A test case assuming `index/index.tsx` is the home page (it routes at `/index`, like `menu/index.tsx` at `/menu` — the code was right); a guard windowed in BYTES that my own comment outran; and an absence guard matching the comment EXPLAINING the deletion, because prose describing a removal spells the removed thing.
- 3546 tests, **19/19 mutations as expected** across four sweeps from verified-green baselines, each with a comment-only control that had to survive.
  - **THE TWO REVISE FAILURES OF 2026-08-19 WERE PROPAGATION, NOT A BROKEN FEATURE.** The colour and the corner both reached the published stylesheet this time (`went live after 5818ms`, hash `DocgFpyw → oajjw1hc`) where the previous run reported `NEVER, budget spent` with the hash unmoved. So an identical stylesheet hash there was the republish not having landed, and reading it as "the revise produced identical CSS" would have sent somebody into `mergeTokens` looking for a bug that is not there.

## THE FAMILIES, THE LAYOUT TABLE AND THE 100 REFERENCE APPS ARE DELETED (2026-08-20, owner's call)

**"The point of making this builds is so that I want to eliminate the families and reference app and layouts so model can make it itself."** The designer used to pick one of 100 pre-written trades; now it AUTHORS a plan per site — purpose, skeleton, shape, page list, primary verb, component manifest — and the directive is composed from what it wrote instead of looked up. **4,208 lines deleted against 706 added.**

- **THE `family` FIELD WAS A 100-OPTION ENUM AND EVERY OTHER PIECE HUNG OFF IT.** `FAMILIES` (2,479 lines of `site-layouts.mjs`), `familiesForPrompt()`, `layoutDirective`, `imageBudget`/`imagesForPage`/`picturesAreContent`, `familyExemplar`, `builder/family-exemplars.mjs` (853 KB of baked home pages), `gen-family-exemplars.mjs`, `family-apps.yml`, two test files. What survives in `site-layouts.mjs` is `STRUCTURES` and `STRUCTURE_NAMES` — eight page SKELETONS, which are a real vocabulary rather than a list of trades, and which the plan still names.
- **THE ORDERING IN THE TOOL SCHEMA IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, and it was the owner's own correction.** I proposed sending more component signatures to cover the blind spot; they said *"I mean that you said the designer picks the list before the pages exist, thats the fix"* — the root cause, not the symptom. **A tool's property order is its generation order**, so `components` is LAST in `PLAN_KEYS`: the manifest is chosen after the page list is written, by a model that has just written it. Pinned by a test, because a reordering that reads as tidying silently puts the guess back.
- **THE PROMPT GOT CHEAPER, MEASURED: 52,769 → 45,256 tokens** on the cached system block. Bigger than the −2,385 estimate because the old block carried 292 component signatures WITH their type shapes; the per-site block now carries only what this site's manifest names, out of a 32-component cached core. Cheaper cold (~3.5 credits) and warm (~0.3).
- **THE DIRECTIVE FORMAT IS UNCHANGED AND THAT WAS PROVEN, NOT ASSUMED.** Every family row was fed through `directiveFromPlan` and compared with `layoutDirective`: **98 of 100 byte-identical**, the two differences being `MAX_ACTION` = 3 biting on families that declared four verbs. The page prompt, the four reference pages and every rule mentioning the layout block were written against that shape.
- **THE FAMILY LISTS WERE WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is why replacing them is not merely equivalent.** Measured over the corpus: **42% of every component reach lay outside the family's declared list**, and **31 of 282 declared names were imported by no page at all.** A hand-maintained table describing what a trade needs was describing neither what the pages used nor what existed.
- **THE PER-TRADE EXEMPLAR WENT DELIBERATELY, and the obvious rescue was refused.** Re-keying eight of them on `structure` is the thing being eliminated wearing a different costume — the owner said so directly: *"we were gonna eliminate that, remember, the model will decide."* It also inverts what made an exemplar safe: matched by TRADE a salon brief got the salon page and copying its content was right; matched by SKELETON a dentist is shown a restaurant, and the one thing a model reliably does with a worked example is copy it.
- **THE ATTACHMENT NOTE LOST ITS SECOND CLAUSE, AND THAT WAS FORCED.** It read *"where they and the trade example below disagree, the attachment wins"* — it arbitrated between two references, which was the whole reason it earned its place, and one of them no longer exists. **Deliberately NOT re-pointed at the plan**: the plan is a DECISION and the image budget is derived from its page list before the model is called, so "the attachment wins" there invites a build costed against pages it did not write.
- **THE 324 REFERENCE PAGES SURVIVE AS `test/fixtures/corpus/`, OUT OF THE TEMPLATE.** They reach no prompt and ship in nothing; what they are now is the only corpus of complete, house-style pages this repo has, and about a dozen checks measure their FALSE-ALARM RATE against it — the prop lint, the colour lint, the import/export lint, the link lane, the nav slot-finder, the tweak lane, the rename cap and hold, the download sweep, the RTL sweep. Deleting them would not advance the goal; it would remove the evidence that our checks do not cry wolf.
  - **THEY HAD TO MOVE, THOUGH, AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHY.** `builder/Dockerfile` does `COPY lovable/template/ ./`, so at `template/src/family-pages` they shipped **3.4 MB into every build container image** — excluded from `tsconfig.json`, imported by no page, and typechecked by NOTHING once their own harness was deleted. That also made `tsconfig-coverage.test.mjs` correct to go red: a directory excluded from the build check and covered by no harness is exactly what it exists to catch. **Answered by moving them rather than by widening the exemption** — the `HARNESSED` escape hatch is gone, and adding a per-directory one back is how a real gap gets waved through.
  - **ONE PATH, in `test/fixtures/corpus.mjs`**, because seven test files each spelling `../builder/lovable/template/src/family-pages` is seven things to keep in step, and the day one drifts it walks an empty directory and reports a clean sweep over nothing. A test asserts the corpus is out of the template AND still there — each half without the other is a different silent failure.
- **THE DEAD PARAMETERS WENT WITH THEM.** `family` was threaded through `briefWithLayout`, `pagesRequest`, `pagesPrompt`, `generateSitePages`, `buildAndPublishPages` and `budgetFor` and read by NONE of them — kept for a while "so every caller keeps compiling", which is how a dead argument reads as a live one. `noFamily` went too: it was the experiment switch that suppressed the layout family, and there are none, so it could only ever have suppressed the PLAN — the opposite of what its name says.
- **AND IT FOUND A REAL GAP THE PLAN WORK HAD OPENED.** The look lane escalates to a page rewrite when a layout field moves, because the container never sees one — and the filter was `k === "family" || k === "structure"`, correct while those were the only two. The plan brought five more (`purpose`, `shape`, `pages`, `action`, `components`), every one a page-generation input and every one on `EDIT_FIELDS`, so a look edit moving one would have been **stored and silently never drawn**. Derived from `PLAN_KEYS` now.
- **THE EVAL SENDS A PLAN PER SCENARIO, hand-written as the designer would answer.** Without one it composes the BARE BRIEF — no page list, no shape, no manifest — which is precisely the ~287-token gap `briefWithLayout` was extracted to close, reopened one field along. Its `validFamily` check became `validPlan`, driven through the real `normalizePlan` rather than restating its rules.
- **TWO SWEEP SURVIVORS WERE REAL GAPS OF MINE, both mine and both the recorded shapes.** `plan: null` at the worker call site passed the ENTIRE suite — the wiring layer, for the umpteenth time. And the rule-3 check sampled `UI_COMPONENTS[0]` and the last entry, **both of which happen to be in the 279-name palette**, so swapping the whole-kit list for the palette passed; it asserts every module is named now. A third own-goal: my own absence guard matched the COMMENT recording the deletion, because prose explaining a removal contains the removed name — the eighth recorded instance, and the first inside a guard written the same day as the entry warning about it.
- 3511 tests, template typechecks, **36/36 mutations as expected** across two sweeps from verified-green baselines. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against it.

## Everything shadcn publishes is now installed (2026-07-30, owner's call)

**"Install every package from shad, all of it."** The kit is **61 components + 70 charts + 27 layout blocks**. The section below this one records the state before it and the reasoning that was overturned — kept because two of its refusals were reversed by ONE additive change, and that is the useful lesson.

- **The registry is 471 items and only 158 are installable things.** `ui` 61 · `block` 97 · **`example` 239** (doc demos — `accordion-demo` and friends) · `font` 52 · `internal`/`lib`/`hook`/`style`/`theme` 22. The examples are documentation, not product code, and putting them in the template would ship 239 files the generator could import. `npx shadcn list @shadcn --offset N` is the authoritative enumeration; the docs sidebar and `/r/index.json` each gave a different, wrong answer earlier.
- **`combobox` was refused twice and both reasons dissolved.** It failed `TS2322` because it wants v4's button sizes — so `button.tsx` now carries `xs`/`icon-xs`/`icon-sm`/`icon-lg` **alongside** its own four, additively. The existing four are untouched, so nothing that renders today can change; taking v4's button wholesale would have rewritten all of them AND reverted this file's local edits, which is exactly what `--overwrite` did here once. That one change also unblocked `attachment` and `message-scroller`, which failed identically. **The generalisation: a v4 component's incompatibility is usually a missing variant, not a fork in the design system.** `@base-ui/react` did come with it, which was the second objection — accepted on the owner's call, and it is now a second primitive library alongside Radix.
- **THE 27 BLOCKS WERE DELETED 2026-07-31 (owner's call), the same day as the examples and for the same reason.** What went: `src/blocks/` (93 files), the `tsconfig.json` exclusion and its `CATALOGUE` entry. **They were rendered live before the call** — all 27 compile and display — so this is not a claim they were broken. **`builder/install-blocks.sh` was deliberately KEPT** (owner narrowed the scope to the blocks themselves): it is the record of how they were installed and the only way to get them back, and it is inert until somebody runs it.
  - **Every one was inert markup, which is what decided it.** `login-01`'s form had no `onSubmit`, its links were `href="#"`, its "Login with Google" button was wired to nothing, and **not one block imported `@/lib/rows`**. So using one meant rewriting every line that does something, at which point it is a reference and not a component — and the model was never told the folder existed (no mention in `PAGE_RULES` or `GENERATOR.md`, and no lint rule in either direction, unlike the examples). Typechecked by nothing, reachable by nothing.
  - **The fit was wrong independently.** 16 of the 27 were admin sidebars and 1 an analytics dashboard, on a platform that makes barber shops and cafés. The 10 that could have mattered — login and signup — were exactly the inert ones, and the 500-component kit already covers their parts: that login card is `Card` + `Label` + `Input` + `Button`, whose APIs we own and describe accurately.
  - **The install reasoning, kept because it was right about the mechanics.** Blocks are NOT additive: `page.tsx` was claimed by ALL 22 · `app-sidebar.tsx` by 15 · `nav-user.tsx` and `nav-main.tsx` by 7 · `login-form.tsx` by all five login blocks — alternative designs of one thing, so a flat install silently lost 21 of 22 and each needed its own folder. A block's `page.tsx` is a Next.js App Router file (`registry:page`, `target: "app/login/page.tsx"`) which the CLI skips on a Vite + TanStack template, so what landed was the block's COMPONENTS, the portable half.
  - **The `react-day-picker` pin OUTLIVES them and must not be "cleaned up".** A block install silently upgraded it 9 → 10 and broke `calendar.tsx` (`TS2353`, its `ClassNames` type changed); it is pinned to `^9.14.0` in `package.json`. **`calendar.tsx` is still in the kit and still needs v9** — the pin is about that component, not about the blocks that caused it. That was the `--overwrite` damage class arriving through npm instead of through files, caught only because the typecheck runs on the whole template.
- **The build-time cost of a catalogue, measured when blocks + charts were both present:** `tsc` 4.97s → 8.02s, `vite` 5.00s → 5.17s, dist unchanged. Vite tree-shakes what nothing imports, so a visitor pays nothing; `tsc` checks every file in `include` whether imported or not. `src/components/charts` is **excluded** from tsconfig for that reason and `src/components/ui` joined it when the kit reached 500.
  - **That is only safe because `exclude` means "not in the initial file list", not "never checked"** — TypeScript still follows an import into an excluded file. Asserted in `test/integration/site-build.mjs` (25 checks now) by breaking a chart in memory, importing it, and requiring the build to fail AND to name the chart. **The first draft passed for the wrong reason**: posting `index.tsx` alone leaves its `<Link to="/menu">` pointing at a route that does not exist, which fails the build by itself — so the assertion held with no chart error at all. Found by mutation, fixed by posting both routes. Both assertions flip now.
- **All 70 charts rendered GREY, and it typechecked perfectly.** They came from the v3 style and say `hsl(var(--chart-1))` — the Tailwind v3 convention, where the token holds bare HSL channels (`12 76% 61%`). This template is Tailwind **v4**, where `--chart-1: oklch(0.646 0.222 41.116)` is a complete colour, so `hsl(oklch(…))` is invalid CSS and every series fell back to the default. v4's own source says `var(--chart-1)`, so all 185 references were rewritten to that form (2 of them `--background`, and one was inside `dashboard-01`). **A CSS-variable mismatch is invisible to `tsc`, to `vite`, to the lint and to every test in the repo** — it was found by LOOKING at a render, which is the whole reason the working rule says to screenshot UI changes.
- **THE 196 `example` ITEMS WERE DELETED 2026-07-31 (owner's call).** They were installed the day before on the reasoning below, which is kept because the deletion reverses it and the reversal is the useful part. What went: `src/examples/` (196 files), its `tsconfig.json` exclusion, the `@/examples/*` lint rule and its test.
  - **They were documentation, and the generator could never use them as such.** The stated purpose was "read one to learn the pattern, then write the real thing" — but the model has no file access, it can only IMPORT, and importing was the one thing the lint refused. So the whole tier served a human browsing the repo, not the builder. Verified before deleting: neither `PAGE_RULES` nor `GENERATOR.md` ever told the model the folder existed.
  - **The lint rule went WITH the files, deliberately, and this is the part to get right if they ever come back.** Its own comment said why it existed: every file in that folder COMPILED, so `tsc`, `vite` and every other check passed while the site published showing a real customer *"Our flagship product combines cutting-edge technology with sleek design."* Delete the folder and that import is a missing module — caught by `tsc` like any other, no longer silent. The justification evaporated with the files. **The cost of removing it: a page that imports one from training-data habit is refused by `tsc` instead of being caught for free — and since 2026-08-04 there is no repair pass to absorb that, so it is a placeholder.** `test/page-gen.test.mjs` asserts the folder is ABSENT rather than asserting the rule, because the deletion is only safe while that premise holds — restore the files without restoring the rule and the silent failure comes straight back.
  - **The install reasoning, kept because it was right about the mechanics.** They ship importing `@/registry/new-york-v4/ui/accordion`, a path this template does not have — but the CLI REWRITES that to `@/components/ui/` on install, so they compiled here. 43 of the 239 were dropped for needing packages we do not have (`@formisch/react` 18 · `valibot` 9 · `@tabler/icons-react` 9 · `next/link` 8), on the rule that **an example that cannot compile is worse than no example**. Fetched directly rather than through the CLI for two measured reasons: `button-group-demo` does not exist in the v3 style and **one missing item aborts the entire batch**, so 239-at-once fails and even a batch of 20 loses the other 19; and 239 sequential invocations is ~40 minutes.
