So switching `:not(.bg-primary *)` ((0,1,1), since `:not()` carries its argument's specificity) to `:not(:where(…))` ((0,0,1)) changed **nothing**, measured. Every `h1`–`h4` takes the colour, including one carrying `text-muted-foreground`. Kept, because rendered side by side that eyebrow going brand-coloured beside its own h3 reads as a hierarchy rather than a clash, and body copy is untouched because a `<p>` is not a heading. `:where()` stays as the correct form the day this is ever layered, and the source says it is not doing the work today.
  - **`site-build` NOW CHECKS IT AGAINST A COMPILED STYLESHEET, which is the check that was missing.** A unit test reads the string the module returns and not what the compiler did with it; only a real build can tell "emitted a rule" from "emitted a rule for a selector that will never exist".
- 3514 tests, `site build` against the real container, **9/9 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer has asked for either; every layer below the model call was driven through the real container and a real browser.

## Stable hooks, so "every button" can mean something (2026-08-19)

**Groundwork for a site-wide restyle lane, and it turned up the same dead-selector shape a third time.** A customer saying *"make every button rounder"* needs a selector that means "a button"; the kit had **15 of 2,112 files stamping a `data-slot`**, the button axis targeting a guess at utility classes, and the heading axis targeting a class nothing carries.

- **THE HOOK IS PER-TARGET, decided by what the customer MEANS, and I got it wrong twice before measuring.** A HEADING is an ELEMENT — every `h1` is one, and using a class is exactly how the `display` axis died. A BUTTON is the PRIMITIVE, not the element: **450 kit files write a raw `<button>` against 220 that use `Button`**, and those 450 are accordion triggers, sort toggles, close crosses and hamburgers — pill-shaping an accordion trigger is not what "rounder buttons" means. A CARD has no element at all, so it can only ever be stamped.
- **`STYLE_TARGETS` IS ONE LIST**, so an axis and the restyle lane can never disagree about what "every button" is. `input` deliberately covers `textarea`: somebody saying "the input boxes" means both, and two names for one visible thing is a distinction they did not make.
- **THE CLASS HALF OF THE BUTTON SELECTOR IS KEPT, AND ONE COMPONENT IS WHY.** `calendar.tsx` hands `buttonVariants()` to react-day-picker as a `classNames` map — the LIBRARY renders those elements, so there is nowhere to stamp an attribute and only a class can reach them. `alert-dialog` and `pagination` had the same shape and were stampable, so they are stamped. The selector is a UNION and strictly a superset of what it was: nothing that changed shape before stops changing shape now.
- **THE OLD COMMENT ON THAT SELECTOR WAS WRONG ABOUT ITS OWN PURPOSE.** It said the second half caught *"hand-written link-buttons on generated pages"*. Measured over the 324-page corpus: **ZERO** pages write one, use `<Button asChild>` or call `buttonVariants()`. What it really caught was three KIT components.
- **THE HOOKS ARE READ OFF A SERVED DOCUMENT, not off the source** — a hook that does not reach the DOM is the dead-selector failure one layer down. Through the real container and the real packaged worker: `button` ×2 (the primitive AND the `asChild` anchor, since `Slot` merges it onto the child), `card`, `badge`, `input` ×2, and **the raw `<button>` is NOT stamped**, which is the narrowing that was the point. The pill rule reaches the compiled stylesheet with the hook leading.
- **THREE OF FOUR SWEEP SURVIVORS WERE MINE, and two are traps recorded earlier the same day.** `button.tsx` explains its hook at length and that prose contains the string `data-slot="button"`, so **both** source-reads matched the EXPLANATION and stayed green with the attribute deleted — the sixth recorded instance, and the second time in one session. And `all.includes('data-slot="input"')` matched **`data-slot="input-group-control"`**, because the one is a prefix of the other: the same substring-versus-token mistake the RTL sweep's first grep made with `items-center` containing `ms-`. Comments are blanked before scanning and the attribute is matched whole.
- **THE FOURTH SURVIVOR WAS A REAL COVERAGE GAP RATHER THAN A FALSE ALARM.** Deleting the hook from `input.tsx` left the kit-wide scan green, because `textarea.tsx` stamps the same one — so every text field on every site could stop following the lane while the guard reported a healthy kit. Each primitive is now asserted to carry its own.
- 3516 tests, template typecheck clean, **9/9 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit; every layer below the model call was driven through the real container.

## A site in two languages, and the mechanism was forced (2026-08-19)

**A published site could be in ONE language.** `lang` picked which, RTL made either direction work, and there was no way to offer a site in English AND Spanish — the ask a shop in a bilingual town makes first.

- **THE MECHANISM WAS DECIDED BY A MEASUREMENT, NOT A PREFERENCE.** A published site is ONE bundle in ONE Worker script — the dispatch namespace keys a script per slug and a Worker cannot load code at runtime — so a bilingual site cannot be two bundles. That left exactly two designs: rewrite every literal string in model-written page code into a runtime lookup, or make the second language **ORDINARY ROUTES in the same bundle**. Driven through the real container and the real packaged worker BEFORE a line of the feature was written: `es/index.tsx` and `es/services.tsx` build, `/` and `/services` answer in English, `/es` and `/es/services` in Spanish, and `/fr` gets the site's own branded 404. **`routeOf` already maps a nested directory to its address, so the addressing layer needed no change at all.**
- **THE HARD HALF WAS ALREADY BUILT, which is why `site-translate.mjs` is small.** Telling prose from code in a TSX file is the whole difficulty, and `site-text.mjs` solved it for the free text editor: it offers only strings it is confident about — never a `className`, a route id, a column name or a bare identifier — and applies a change at the exact offset it offered it, back to front. Translation is that machinery pointed at a different source of replacements, so it inherits every guard that one is mutation-checked for.
- **ONE CALL PER LANGUAGE, NOT PER PAGE.** Measured over the 324 exemplar pages: 19,579 strings, median 61 a page (~764 tokens), so a whole site fits in one request — and a page-at-a-time loop translates "Book" on the home page and "Reserve" on the booking page, which reads as two businesses. Haiku, because this is the cheapest thing a model can be asked to do and the expensive ones are no better at it.
- **THE CACHE IS WHAT MAKES A SECOND LANGUAGE AFFORDABLE TO KEEP, and it is keyed by the ENGLISH STRING.** Every cheap edit republishes through the shared spine — a typo fix, a colour change, a swapped picture — so without it each one would re-translate the whole site to keep the two halves in step. Keyed on the string rather than on a page or an offset: a paragraph that moves to another page is still translated, and a colour change moves no words at all, so it costs **nothing** — proven end to end, 7 strings on the first publish and **0 misses on the second**. Rebuilt from the current strings on every publish, which is also the pruning, so it cannot grow without bound over years of edits.
- **A WRONG-LENGTH ANSWER IS REFUSED WHOLESALE.** The mapping is positional, so an answer one item short does not translate n-1 strings — it puts every translation after the gap on the WRONG string, and the result compiles perfectly and is nonsense. An ARRAY rather than a map keyed by the English, because a map needs the model to reproduce every source string exactly and one stray character silently loses that string.
- **A FAILED TRANSLATION IS NOT A FAILED PUBLISH.** It runs on the spine every cheap edit goes through, so it falls back to whatever is cached and, for anything new, to the primary language: the second language is merely behind, which is strictly better than losing the edit the customer actually asked for. A string with no translation is CACHED AS ITSELF rather than dropped, or it is asked about again on every publish for ever.
- **EACH REFUSAL IN `resolveLangs` IS A REAL FAILURE IT PREVENTS**, and one of them was found by mutation: a duplicate base (`pt-BR` and `pt-PT` both want `/pt`), a reserved segment (`/api`, `/assets`), **a prefix that shadows a page the site already has** (`/cy` as an ordinary page on an English site), and **the PRIMARY's own base** — `en-US` beside an `en-GB` site is a second copy of the whole site at a second set of addresses in the same language, which nothing else here would have refused.
- **THE SWITCHER IS RENDERED BY `SiteHeader` ITSELF, never passed in.** A switcher the generator must add is one it eventually forgets, and forgetting leaves a bilingual site whose second half is reachable only by typing a URL — the `safe-image` and `spam-guard` rule. It renders **nothing** when the site has one language, which is every site published before this, so no existing header changes. **In the sheet as well as the nav**, because the nav is `hidden md:flex` and most visitors to these sites are on a phone — and the sheet's gate had to widen from `links.length > 0`, or a one-page bilingual site had no way at all to switch on a phone.
- **IT LINKS TO THE SAME PAGE, not to the other language's front door.** Somebody reading the Spanish booking page who presses "English" wants the English booking page. Measured off the served document: on `/es/services` the switcher offers `/services` and `/es/services`.
- **THE LABEL IS BAKED AT BUILD TIME, and that is an ICU decision rather than a convenience.** `Intl.DisplayNames` is the obvious way to render "Español" in the page, and this repo has already been bitten by two ICU VERSIONS disagreeing about one locale between the server render and the browser hydration. Resolved once, in one process, shipped as a string: there is no second opinion to diverge.
  - **THE BASE SUBTAG, NOT THE FULL TAG — found by LOOKING at a render.** `en-GB` resolves to **"British English"**, which is regional noise in a two-item nav; the reader is choosing a language, not a dialect. Safe because two variants of one language can never both be offered. Capitalised because Intl lowercases in several languages ("español", "français") and a nav item is a label rather than a sentence.
  - **AND IT LIVED IN THE CONTAINER, WHERE NOTHING COULD DRIVE IT.** `build-server.mjs` starts an HTTP server, so a unit test cannot import it and every rule in `langLabel` was held by a source-read at best. Moved into `site-langs.mjs`, where it is arithmetic about a language tag like everything else there — and the three mutants that survived against it before the move all die now.
- **EACH LANGUAGE CARRIES ITS OWN DIRECTION, not the site's.** An English site with an Arabic second language needs `/ar` to read right to left while `/` reads left to right — only expressible at all because the kit went logical the day before.
- **THE DOCUMENT READS ITS LANGUAGE OFF THE ROUTE**, from ONE reading shared by `lang` and `dir`, matched on a WHOLE SEGMENT: `/eshop` is not Spanish, which is the anchoring mistake the hostname rewrite already recorded once. Asserted in three places, because the switcher hand-rolls the same question.
- **THE BUILD PATH DELIBERATELY SENDS NO LANGUAGES, and that is not an omission.** `SITE_LANGS` non-empty with no translated routes is a switcher pointing at a **404**: the build publishes only the pages the generator wrote, so baking the list there would put a link to `/es` in the header of a site that has no `/es`. A second language is added by an EDIT, which goes through the spine and produces the pages in the same publish. Asserted, so nobody "completes" it into exactly that broken state.
- **DATABASE ROWS ARE NOT TRANSLATED, and it is stated rather than discovered.** A café's menu items live in Postgres, not in the page source, so the dishes stay in the owner's own words. Translating rows means a column per language, a Data panel that edits both, and a rule for what a half-translated row shows — a separate feature, and pretending otherwise ships a site bilingual in its headings and monolingual in its content without saying so.
- **THE SWEEP FOUND A REAL BUG AND THE FIXTURE IS WHY IT SURVIVED.** `pageForLang` read `page.route || "/"` and a stored page HAS NO `route` FIELD — `loadSiteSource` hands back `{path, source}` — so `bare` was `"/"` for every page of every site and `es/services.tsx` shipped still declaring `createFileRoute("/services")`. The test passed `route: "/book"` explicitly: **a fixture more capable than reality, the `setTotp` lesson in a route id.** Derived from the path through `routeOf` now, which is the one reading of file→address this repo already has.
  - **AND THE COMMENT ABOVE IT WAS WRONG ABOUT THE CONSEQUENCE.** It claimed the generator "refuses it rather than picking one". Measured on two real builds differing only in that string: **both build and both serve `/es/services` with the Spanish page**, because `tsr generate` writes the tree from the FILE PATH and corrects the call. So the bug was latent rather than fatal. The rewrite is kept — the source should say what the file is, and `route` on the returned object is read as truth — and the claim is corrected, because a false one in a comment is what gets believed.
- **THREE GUARDS PROVED INERT AND ARE SAID SO IN THE SOURCE rather than deleted or falsely asserted.** `relinkForLang`'s `if (!prefix) return src` cannot change the answer, because `prefixRoute` returns the route untouched with no prefix; the already-in-this-language check cannot fire, because `known` only ever holds the PRIMARY's routes and a site with a real page at `/es` cannot exist (`resolveLangs` refuses it); and **our own `aria-current` is set by `Link` itself** — removing it leaves the served HTML byte-identical, measured on a real render in both languages. All three are kept because each rests on a property in another function that is one edit away from changing, and the last is written as `page` rather than `true` so the source does not claim a value the DOM never shows.
- **`collectStrings` RETURNED A `where` MAP THAT NOTHING READ.** Every occurrence's page and offset, computed on every publish, consumed by nobody — `translatePages` re-scans the pages itself because the offsets it needs are the ones in the source it is about to edit. Deleted rather than kept in case: the on-disk-and-reachable-by-nothing shape this repo has removed 289 files over.
- **IT REPLACED A DIFFERENT COMPONENT OF THE SAME NAME, and that was only safe because the old one was reachable by nothing.** `lang-switch.tsx` has been in the kit since the fifth hundred as a `value`/`onChange` form-control picker — used by **0 of the 328 corpus pages**, named in no rule and in no exemplar. The same on-disk-and-reachable-by-nothing shape the 27 blocks and 196 examples were deleted for, checked before overwriting rather than after. `COMPONENT_API` now reads `LangSwitch()`, which is honest: it takes nothing, because the site's own language list is what it renders.
- **AND MY OWN `aria-current` GUARD MATCHED THE COMMENT EXPLAINING IT.** The source-read scanned the raw file, and the prose arguing that the attribute is belt-and-braces contains the string `aria-current="page"` — so deleting the attribute left the assertion perfectly green. **Prose describing a thing contains that thing's spelling: the fifth time this repo has recorded that trap, and the first time inside a guard written the same day as the entry warning about it.** Comments are blanked, length-preserving, before anything in that file is asserted. Found by mutation, not by reading.
- 3512 tests, template typecheck clean, **45/45 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer edit has run through the lane; every link below the model call was driven through the real container and the real packaged worker, with the Haiku call faked.

## The cheap rung: change how a page looks without rewriting it (2026-08-19)

**Option B of the deferred #129, and it closes six gaps rather than one.** "Make that heading bigger", "too much space above the prices", "show the gallery four across", "centre that", "put a line under it", "move the opening hours above the map" — every one regenerated the WHOLE page, and a regeneration rewords the copy on the way past.

- **MEASURED ON A REAL 12,044-CHARACTER PAGE: 3 credits against 10, and a REFUSAL costs 1.** That last number is what makes it unconditional: the cheap attempt is tried on every page edit with no new routing decision, because there is no case where asking first is the expensive move. A refusal is a few words of output; the miss costs ~1 credit on top of the rewrite that was going to happen anyway.
- **MOST OF THE SAVING IS NOT THE MODEL, it is the ~36,000-token generator prompt this call does not send.** Both halves are pinned by a test — Haiku, and `TWEAK_RULES` asserted to stay under 2,000 characters and never to grow `PAGE_RULES`/`COMPONENT_API`/`CHART_COMPONENTS`.
- **THE PROMISE IS CHECKED, NOT ASKED FOR.** The model is told not to touch the words, and then `sameProse` throws the answer away if they moved — because a promise a prompt makes is one a model eventually breaks quietly. **Calibrated over the corpus: 1,640 real tweaks across 329 pages moved the prose 0 times, and a deliberate rewording was caught on 329 of 329.** That is the guarantee the `page` layer cannot make and the reason this rung is worth having beyond the price.
- **THE COMPARISON IS A MULTISET, and that is what allows the feature.** "Move the opening hours above the map" changes every offset after the block it moved, so anything positional would refuse the change the rung exists for. Sorted texts: a reordered page compares equal, a reworded one does not. `extractText` is REUSED rather than reimplemented — it already knows a sentence from an identifier, and a second reading of "what counts as words" would drift toward refusing correct tweaks.
- **EVERY REFUSAL ESCALATES; none of them is ever shown to a customer.** Six named reasons (`cannot` · `reworded` · `moved-route` · `no-change` · `truncated` · `unreadable`) that all fall through to the rewrite. They are named separately because a log of them is the only way to tell "the model keeps refusing" from "the model keeps rewording", which want opposite fixes.
- **A FAILED COMPILE FALLS THROUGH TOO, which is the one place this differs from every other lane.** The rewrite below is a genuinely different attempt by a stronger model, so answering *"that didn't compile"* here would refuse the customer a path that still works. Their live site is untouched either way.
- **A PAGE MAY NOT MOVE ITS OWN ADDRESS.** A route id is typed against the tree `tsr generate` emits, so a page that renames itself turns every `<Link>` to it into a compile error. `renameRoute` does that properly with the redirect; this rung has no business near it.
- **THE CHEAP ATTEMPT IS BILLED WITH THE REWRITE, ONCE.** `eCharge` is variadic precisely so two calls on one path round up once — the `pageCredits` lesson. A refusal is still billed (the call happened); a provider that was never reached bills nothing.
- **A NUL BYTE SHIPPED IN THE NEW MODULE AND EVERY TEST WAS GREEN.** a raw NUL inside a `.join()` where a space was meant — a perfectly good separator, so nothing behavioural could see it. The only symptom was `grep` answering *"binary file matches"*, which means **every source-reading guard in this repo silently stops covering that file while it looks exactly like one with nothing to report.** Fourth recorded instance (three in the kit on 2026-07-31) and the first that anything was watching for: `test/source-bytes.test.mjs` now sweeps the tree, with a floor on the file count and a negative case built at runtime so the guard needs no self-exemption. It found four more on its first run — `public/mkt/demo*/index.html`, captured prerendered demo pages whose NUL is upstream's own serialisation, scoped out as build output rather than source.
- **TWO OF MY OWN GUARDS WERE THE RECORDED TRAPS.** An existing rename check sliced to a landmark far below and swallowed the new block, going red about a model call in a different branch — the overlapping-window own-goal, now bounded by the branch's own closing brace. And my own fall-through assertion used `indexOf(a) < b`, which passes vacuously when `a` is deleted: a mutant replacing the success gate with `if (false)` — paying for the cheap call and discarding its answer — survived it. Both anchors are proved to exist first now.
- 3425 tests, **25/25 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that survived. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer edit has run through it.

## One page's own typeface (2026-08-19)

**The look lane could change the site's fonts, and the router said in as many words that one page's could not be changed: *"a request to change one page's TYPEFACE or corners is not this"*.** So "the menu page should be in something handwritten" escalated to a ~27-credit page rewrite that could not do it either — the container writes ONE font pair for the whole site.

- **WHY IT IS POSSIBLE AT ALL WAS MEASURED IN A REAL BROWSER BEFORE A LINE WAS WRITTEN.** The template applies fonts as plain `var()` reads — `body { font-family: var(--font-sans) }` and `h1,h2,h3,h4 { font-family: var(--font-heading) }` — so overriding those two on `body[data-page="/menu"]` reaches both: `PageSans` on the body, `PageHead` on the heading.
- **AND THE SAME MEASUREMENT REFUSES CORNERS.** The kit derives `--radius-sm`/`--radius-xl` with `calc(var(--radius) ± Npx)` **at `:root`**, and a custom property is computed where it is DECLARED — so a page-scoped `--radius` moved the cards to 24px and left every chip at 6px and every panel at 18px. **A page rounded in some places and not others reads as a badly-made site**, which is worse than the honest limit. (Independently: 280 of the 500 themes hard-set `border-radius` as real RULES, which no token scope can reach.) The refusal is in the model's own instructions, not just in code.
- **IT REUSES `tokensPage` RATHER THAN ADDING A SECOND PAGE FIELD.** Two fields could name two different pages in one answer, which is two changes wearing one.
- **NAMING A PAGE MUST NOT RE-FONT THE WHOLE SITE, and the fix is an ORDERING.** `designed.fonts` reaching `mergeLook` changes the site's typeface as well as the page's — so a request to give the menu page a script font would re-set every heading on every page, reported as a success. The fonts are stripped from the INPUT rather than patched out of the result, so `merged`, `moved` and the `site_look` write are all correct from ONE expression instead of three that can disagree; `forPage` is therefore computed BEFORE the merge.
- **REPLACED, NOT ACCUMULATED, unlike the per-page colours.** A typeface is a PAIR and `resolvePair` fills in whichever half was not asked for, so merging a new answer over an old one keeps a half nobody asked to keep. **Both halves empty is the removal verb**, so a page put back to the site's fonts keeps no entry at all.
- **EVERY SCOPE'S FILE IS FETCHED AND EVERY SCOPE'S PACKAGE IS IMPORTED.** A page whose family was never fetched — or whose npm font nothing imports — renders the FALLBACK while the build reports the font it asked for, which is the exact failure shape `site-fonts.mjs` is written around. `@font-face` is emitted once per family however many scopes reference it.
- **`MAX_PAGE_FONTS` IS 2**, because every extra pair is up to two more woff2 files a VISITOR downloads, on a platform whose customers' customers are mostly on phones.
- **OUTSIDE `@theme`, WHICH MAY NOT CONTAIN A SCOPED RULE — and it does not need to be inside one.** `body[data-page="/x"]` is (0,1,1) against `:root`'s (0,1,0), so it wins on SPECIFICITY rather than source order. That is what makes it safe here where a second `:root` was not: the minifier proved that one dead and the build shipped the default font while reporting the chosen one.
- **PROVED AGAINST THE REAL CONTAINER, and proved to FAIL.** `site-build` builds a byte-identical payload twice, differing only in `pageFonts`, and reads the COMPILED stylesheet: the scoped rule is in it, the page's family is in it, the site's own font survives beside it, and a site not using the feature has no `data-page` at all. Dropping `decls.scoped` from the write fails exactly the two positive assertions — 153/2 against 155/0.
- **FIVE SWEEP SURVIVORS, ALL GAPS IN MY OWN TESTS, and two are recorded classes.** The dedup test used ONE family for both scopes, so dropping the page loop still declared it via the site's pair. Two fetch assertions were SPELLINGS — the signature and the argument name — both satisfied by a body that ignores `pages` and a `pageFontScopes` that is `[]`. Putting a page back was asserted only in the tool's description. And `/TYPEFACE/i` matched a sentence a mutant left behind.
- **THREE OWN-GOALS ON THE WAY, all recorded:** a `#5` guard sized in BYTES went red an hour after shipping when this change inserted a key above the one it asserted (bounded by the loop now); a phrase-pinned router guard went red on a clause that honestly grew (asserts the property now); and a 12-space anchor matched an 18-space line, because 18 spaces CONTAINS 12.
- 3468 tests, `site build` **155/155** against the real container, **24/24 mutations as expected** across two sweeps from verified-green baselines, with a comment-only control that survived. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer edit has run through it.

## A site could not be proved to Google (2026-08-19)

**Search Console, Bing, Pinterest and Facebook all ask for the same thing — a `<meta>` tag with a token they generated, in the head of the site — and there was no slot for one.** Every tag in a published head is composed by `__root.tsx` out of a sidecar the platform writes, and an owner could not add a character to either. So a business could not get itself indexed, could not see what it ranked for, and could not run an ad against its own domain. Nothing was broken; there was no way in.

- **AN ALLOW-LIST OF NAMES, NOT A SNIPPET, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DESIGN.** Every CMS offers "paste some HTML into your head" and every one of them is a stored-XSS hole on the customer's own domain. `builder/site-verify.mjs` takes a PROVIDER and a TOKEN: the tag's name comes from a fixed table and the token is checked against the shape these providers actually issue (`[A-Za-z0-9_.=-]`, 8-128). **There is therefore no escaping question to get wrong** — the rule `site-tokens.mjs` already lives under for colours in a stylesheet.
- **SCRIPTS ARE DELIBERATELY OUT, and that is a decision rather than a gap.** Analytics is the other thing people put in a head and it is a completely different question: a `script-src` the published-site CSP does not grant, a third party on every visitor's page, and consent obligations we would be handing a small business silently. It gets built as its own thing with its own answer to all three, or not at all.
- **THE FIRST IMPLEMENTATION WENT INTO `metaTags` AND WOULD HAVE REACHED NOTHING.** That function patches a fenced block into a built shell — which was the whole truth until Start, and **under Start there is no shell**: the document is `__root.tsx` rendered per request, and `worker.js` imports only `siteMetaKey` and `SITE_LIVE_FILE` from `site-meta.mjs`. Caught by asking who calls it before wiring further, which is the ten-minutes-of-driving rule applied to one's own change.
- **PAIRS ON THE SIDECAR, NOT HTML, and the split falls exactly where it should.** The platform resolves `{google: "TOK"}` into `[{name, content}]` and the template renders what it is handed — so **a site cannot name its own tag**, and a provider added to the table reaches every published site with no template change and no site rebuilt. Checked at BOTH ends anyway: the value comes off an R2 read, and a malformed row would otherwise render `name="undefined"` on every page.
- **ON EVERY PAGE, not only the home one.** A provider reads the tag off whichever URL it was pointed at, so a site verified at its root and not at `/book` is one failed check from being unverified.
- **IT TAKES EFFECT WITHOUT A REPUBLISH.** The site's own Worker reads its head out of the sidecar, so patching that one key IS the deployment — which matters because the alternative is a 40-second container run to change a string, and because somebody pasting a token has the provider's Verify button open in the next tab. `_meta` is the source of truth and both writers derive from it, so they can differ in timing and never in value. The store is read on BOTH publish paths, or an owner's tag would vanish the next time they fixed a typo.
- **A PANEL FIELD RATHER THAN A CHAT LANE, deliberately.** The token is a 43-character opaque string nobody types, so extracting it from prose is a model call that fails silently when it gets it wrong — and this is a set-once action on the same shelf as Secrets and Domains. **No model call anywhere on the path**, asserted by name. It lives in the Domains panel because somebody who has just pointed a domain at their site is exactly the person about to ask why Google cannot find it.
- **PEOPLE PASTE THE WHOLE TAG, because that is what the provider's page gives them a copy button for.** `readPastedTag` lifts the `content` attribute out; refusing it and saying "just the token please" is a support question created on purpose. It is a convenience on the way IN, not a parser — anything it cannot read falls through to the token check, which refuses it **by name and with a reason**, because "that didn't work" over a 43-character opaque string leaves somebody re-pasting the same thing.
- **THE PANEL ASKS THE SERVER WHICH PROVIDERS EXIST.** A hand-written list in the client is a second opinion, and the day they disagree the panel offers a field whose value the server silently drops — which reads to the owner as the save not working. Asserted by requiring no provider name to appear in the client at all.
- **PROVED BY EXECUTING THE REAL SITE WORKER**, which is the only thing that could: `site-build` puts a sidecar carrying two pairs into its fake R2 and asserts both tags reach the rendered document, on the home page AND on `/menu`. **And proved to FAIL**, twice — the first mutant (`for (const v of [])`) broke the build and took 65 checks down with it, which discriminates nothing; a mutant that still compiles (`content + "X"`) failed **exactly one** assertion. **The per-page check passed against it** — a substring match survives a changed content — so both ends now share one matcher.
- **FOUR SWEEP SURVIVORS, ALL REAL, and two are recorded classes.** `VERIFIERS["constructor"]` is truthy, so a truthiness check stores a junk provider (the Stripe-plan bug, third instance). A source-read for `siteMetaKey(vslug)` is satisfied by a dead `if (0) await ...get(...)`. And **asserting the publish CALL says `verify` passes on a spine that publishes `undefined`** — the read that fills it has to be asserted separately, derived over every payload read of `_meta`.
- 3467 tests, template typecheck clean, `site build` **149/149** against the real container, **25/25 mutations as expected** across four sweeps from verified-green baselines, including a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no owner has pasted a token and no provider has checked one.

## A site could not offer a document, and the download button was already there (2026-08-19)

**`download-card` has been in the generator's shortlist since the kit was written and the corpus reaches for it 24 times — a parish council's annual return, a franchise prospectus, a food hygiene certificate, a photographer's press pack — and BOTH upload gates sniffed images and nothing else.** So the platform put a download button in front of the model and had nowhere for the file to go. The component was reachable and the bytes were not: the on-disk-and-reachable-by-nothing property the 27 blocks and the 196 examples were deleted for, arriving from the other side.

- **EVERY ONE OF THE 34 EXEMPLAR DOWNLOADS WAS BROKEN, and they are what the model copies.** `href="/papers"`, `href="/report"`, `href="#dl"` — a route or a fragment, never a file, because there were no files. Clicking one saved a page's HTML or reloaded the current page. Fixing the component without sweeping the corpus would have been undone by the examples, which is the `#/` href lesson verbatim.
- **`DownloadCard` REQUIRED ITS `href`, so the honest value was `""` — and `<a href="" download>` RELOADS THE CURRENT PAGE.** A Download button that appears to work, does nothing, and is invisible to `tsc`, to vite, to the lint and to every test here. It is optional now and renders "Not available yet" instead of an anchor, which is the `safe-image` rule from the other end: the owner uploads the file AFTER the build, so a fresh site has cards with nothing behind them and that is the ORDINARY state rather than an edge case.
  - **The kit-wide version of that check now runs on every pass of `kit-render`** — no component may paint `href=""`/`src=""` — because it is a class rather than one component, and the `empty` pass (every optional omitted) is exactly the state of a site whose owner has uploaded nothing. Clean across all 2,071 components in all four passes, and **proved to fire** by putting an empty anchor back and watching it name the component.
- **PDF IS THE FEATURE, measured rather than assumed: 21 of the corpus's 24 named files are `.pdf`.** The zip family is the tail and is in because the next two — `.xlsx`, `.docx` — are what a business hands out when it is not a PDF.
- **WITHIN THE ZIP FAMILY THE BYTES DECIDE THE FAMILY AND THE NAME PICKS THE MEMBER, and that gives up nothing.** Office Open XML is a zip of XML parts, so no magic number separates `.xlsx` from `.docx` from a plain `.zip` without unpacking the archive and reading `[Content_Types].xml`, which may be deflated. The extension can only ever pick among types that are already equivalent: same bytes, all served `attachment`, none rendered. **`.xlsm`/`.docm` are deliberately absent** — they are the macro-carrying ones, they still upload, and they come back down named `.zip`, so we never hand somebody a file their own operating system is right to warn them about.
  - **`Object.hasOwn`, not truthiness, and a mutant proved it was covered by nothing.** `ZIP_MEMBERS["constructor"]` is truthy — it is `Object.prototype.constructor` — so a plain lookup names a file `.constructor` and hands the browser a FUNCTION as its content type. **That exact bug shipped here once already, in the Stripe plan lookup.**
- **THE DISPOSITION IS DECIDED FROM THE STORED TYPE**, which is what the leading bytes said and the one thing about a file a caller cannot lie about. A picture stays `inline` (an `<img>` pointing at an attachment renders nothing); a document is `attachment`, which is both what a download button means and what stops a PDF being opened by the browser's own viewer on this origin.
- **BOTH FILENAME FORMS, or a business whose language is not written in Latin letters gets a download called `a1b2c3d4….pdf`.** The bare `filename=` is ASCII by spec and carries the stripped fallback; `filename*=UTF-8''…` carries the real one. `encodeURIComponent` escapes the quote and every control character, and the plain half is an allow-list, so **neither can inject a header** — and the starred form is only sent when it says something the plain one could not, so an ordinary English filename is unchanged.
- **THE DOWNLOAD NAME'S EXTENSION IS OURS AND ITS STEM IS THEIRS.** `invoice.exe` whose bytes are a PDF comes back down as `invoice.pdf` — the two can never disagree, because only one half comes from the caller. A path becomes its BASENAME the way a browser does it (`../../etc/passwd` → `passwd.pdf`), not every directory flattened into a name that is one `..` from being a path again; `\p{Cf}` goes first, because that is where the bidi overrides live — the character that makes `holiday<RLO>fdp.exe` DISPLAY as `holidayexe.pdf`, the oldest filename spoof there is and invisible in every log. A leading dot is never eaten as an extension, or `.hidden` yields nothing at all.
- **A VISITOR STILL CANNOT SEND A DOCUMENT, and that is the line rather than an omission.** `handleVisitorUpload` is unauthenticated by design — a customer attaching a photo to a booking has no account — so anyone who knows a slug can call it, and its purpose is "send a photo of the job". A stranger putting a PDF or a zip on a business's own domain is a malware-hosting hole. Asserted, with the picture it exists for still working, or the refusals pass on an endpoint that refuses everything.
- **THE DELETE GATE IS DERIVED FROM THE SNIFFERS**, never a second list of extensions: a name that route refuses is a file the owner can upload and can never remove, and two lists drifting apart is exactly how that happens. Driven over every extension either sniffer can mint.
- **THE PRE-CHECK BOUNDS ON THE LARGER CAP AND THE MODULE DECIDES THE REST.** Which cap applies depends on what the bytes turn out to be — a 7 MB brochure is fine and a 7 MB photograph is not — and `content-length` is read before a byte has been buffered, so it refuses what could not be either and `handleUpload` sniffs first and caps second.
- **THE PANEL'S DOCUMENT TILE WAS BROKEN AND ONLY LOOKING COULD SAY SO.** An `<a>` is inline, so the format badge inside it had no definite width to derive its height from: `aspect-ratio:1` lost, the badge came out 180px tall in a 153px square, and `overflow:hidden` **cut the filename and the size off entirely** — a tile that said nothing but "PDF". The badge was also browser-blue and underlined, because nothing had ever put text inside that anchor before. Every test passed. `test/site-documents.test.mjs` is 28 tests and none of them could see either.
- **MY OWN VACUOUS ASSERTION, found by the sweep:** `/href\s*\n?\s*\?/` was meant to prove the anchor is gated on there being an href and was satisfied by `href?: string` in the type two lines above it, so replacing the whole condition with `{true` SURVIVED. Anchored on `{href` now.
- **AND THE CONTROL-BYTE GUARD CAUGHT ITS AUTHOR TWICE IN ONE SITTING.** `\u0000-\u001f` written into an Edit tool argument arrived in `site-uploads.mjs` as TWO RAW BYTES, and then again in the test file — so the escape-sequence rule is not enough on its own: **the file has to be verified afterwards**, which is exactly what `test/source-bytes.test.mjs` does. Both are expressed without a character range now (`\p{Cc}\p{Cf}` in the source, a char-code predicate in the test). Fifth and sixth recorded instances.
- 3453 tests, kit typecheck clean, template typecheck clean, `kit render` 2,071 components clean on the new check across all four passes, **24/24 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no build has written a `DownloadCard` and no file has been uploaded to a real site.

## Every theme had a dark half drawn for it and nothing ever applied it (2026-08-18)

**500 themes, every one shipping a `.dark` block of 31 solved colour properties into every site's stylesheet, applied by no site ever.** `themeCss` has emitted it since themes existed, `worldCss` emits `.dark body` washes beside it, `worldMutedCss` re-derives the muted ink for it — the whole world layer was WRITTEN for dark and switched on by nothing. So *"make my site dark"* was answered with a token patch, which is a different feature wearing the same words: it moves `--background` and leaves the buttons, borders, badges and highlights on colours picked for white paper.

- **THE FIX IS ONE CLASS ON `<html>`, AND THE STYLESHEET DOES NOT MOVE.** Measured through the real container on two builds of a byte-identical payload differing only in `mode`: the CSS asset came out with the **same content hash**, and the document came out with `class="dark"`. That pair is the assertion in `site build`, and it needs both halves — a build that ignored `mode` gives identical CSS, and one that regenerated a second palette gives a different document.
- **ON `<html>`, NOT `<body>`, and that is forced rather than chosen.** `styles.css` declares `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))` — a DESCENDANT of `.dark` — so the class has to sit above everything it reaches, and `<body>` is already carrying the per-page colour scope, which would then be inside the dark scope instead of above it.
- **THE PREMISE IS ASSERTED, because the whole feature is cheap only while two things one layer down stay true.** If that variant ever becomes `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` the site follows the VISITOR rather than the owner and every dark site renders light for anyone whose laptop is set to light — silently, past `tsc`, past vite, past the lint. And if a theme ever emits the same colours under both selectors the class is inert the other way. Both are checked, the second **across the whole shortlist** with a floor on the count, because a loop that ran over nothing passes every assertion inside it.
- **BAKED, NOT DETECTED (owner's call by implication of the whole product).** A site does not follow the visitor's own setting: the owner picked a look, and half their customers seeing a different one is not something they asked for.
- **THE MEASUREMENT THAT SHOWS WHY THE TOKEN PATCH WAS NOT THIS.** On `noir`, `--primary` goes `oklch(30% 0 0)` light → `oklch(82% .005 85)` dark. A patch that darkened the ground alone would have left every button at 30% lightness on a 12% ground — a near-invisible control on the one element a visitor has to press. Rendered and looked at, both modes, plus a backdrop theme (`glass`, whose wash follows through `.dark body`) and a dark site carrying a brand-colour override, which still wins: `--primary: #f59e0b` with `withContrast` deriving the dark text on it.
- **`normalizeMode` LIVES IN `site-identity.mjs` BESIDE `normalizeLang`, and for the OPPOSITE reason.** That one refuses rather than defaulting, so a caller can keep whatever the site had. This one cannot — `<html>` either carries the class or it does not — so everything unreadable is light, which is what **every site published before today** already is, and one deploy therefore re-draws nothing. It is there rather than inline in the container because `writeSiteBrand` writes files: nothing in that function can be driven by a unit test, and every shape the value can arrive in has to be answerable without one. **A non-string is not coerced** — `String(["dark"])` is `"dark"`, the shape that made a one-element array a valid role and a valid access level twice already.
- **THE ANNOTATION IS LOAD-BEARING AND ONLY A COMPILE FINDS IT.** An unannotated `export const SITE_MODE = "light"` has the LITERAL type `"light"`, so `SITE_MODE === "dark"` is `TS2367` — comparing two disjoint literal types — and **every light build fails to typecheck**. It has to be written `: "light" | "dark"` in the container's emitted line AND in the template's checked-in placeholder, or the two disagree and the template stops building on its own.
- **BOTH PUBLISH SPINES CARRY IT, and the cheap one is the one that is easy to miss.** Every text fix, colour change, picture swap, logo change and data reorder republishes through `recompileAndPublish`, and the container writes `site-brand.ts` on EVERY build — it must, or one site's mode leaks onto the next. So a path that does not send the stored value sends nothing, and nothing is light: a customer's typo fix would silently put their dark site back to white. **Asserted as a DERIVED property — every `lang` hop must have a `mode` beside it** — rather than as a count of the three that exist today, since a hand-written list is exactly what leaves the fourth one bare.
- **NOT AN ESCALATION.** The look lane sends `family` and `structure` up to a full page rewrite because the container never sees them; it DOES see this one, so escalating would spend ~27 credits to reach the same recompile.
- **ITS `enum` IS THE GATE, NOT THE PROSE.** A cap a model is merely told about is not a cap — the `MAX_CLARIFY` lesson — so `["light","dark"]` is in the tool and `normalizeMode` is the belt behind it. The description also says **not to also send tokens**, or the model reaches for the approximation it has always reached for and a patch fights the theme's own dark palette on the one site that needed neither.
- **MY BLOCK-SCOPE SCANNER FLAGGED MY OWN TEST, TWICE, AND BOTH WERE FALSE.** `worker-imports.test.mjs` counts braces as TEXT, so `[^}]` in a regex on a `const` line drives its depth negative on that very line and every later use reads as a ReferenceError that is not there; and a variable called `dark` was "read" by the assertion message containing `.dark`. Same family as `logoImg` five hundred lines up — the scan is deliberately narrow rather than a parser, so the fix is code and names it can read.
- **AND THEN I CONTORTED CORRECT CODE TO SATISFY IT AND SHIPPED IT RED.** Appeasing that false alarm meant rewriting a PASSING assertion to slice from `indexOf(":root")` — which finds Tailwind's own preamble thousands of characters before any palette — and the container was not re-run afterwards. Green here at 146/146 on the old form, **`145 passed, 1 failed` in CI** on the new one, returning two empty strings. The rule the repo already has for prompts applies to tests: **re-run the thing the change is asserted by, not the thing that complained.** The declarations sit at the top of the script now, which is below the scanner's own indent threshold.
- **THE ASSERTION IT REPLACED WAS ALSO WEAKER THAN IT READ, found by measuring the real stylesheet rather than guessing twice.** A compiled bundle carries **FOUR** `--background` declarations, because the template's own shadcn base palette is emitted before the theme's — so a regex taking the FIRST match compares the base light against the base dark and is **true whatever theme was built**, including one whose theme never reached the stylesheet at all. It reads the LAST of each now (later wins, the same source-order rule the colour override already rests on) with a second assertion that there ARE two, and the pair was driven against three stylesheet shapes to prove each catches a failure the other misses.
- 3408 tests, template typechecks, `site build` **147/147** against the real container, **21/21 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer build has carried it.

## The frame's own arrangement, and the type that would have cost every site (2026-08-18)

**`SiteHeader` took brand/links/action and set `h-14`, `max-w-6xl`, `ml-auto` and `sticky` ITSELF.** So *"centre our logo"*, *"run the header right across"* and *"stop the menu following me down the page"* had no path **at any price** — not by typing, and not by paying for a full rebuild either, because there was no slot to fill. `className` could not reach it: `SiteChrome` puts that on the outer div, and **0 of 329 corpus pages pass one**.

- **THE CLOSED UNION WOULD HAVE LOST THE WHOLE SITE, and the render harness caught it before a line of wiring was written.** A page holds its frame as `const CHROME = { …, layout: { brand: "centre" } }` and spreads it — **261 of the 318 exemplars use exactly that shape** — and a `const` object's property widens to `string`, which a bare literal union REFUSES. Measured live against the real reference pages: `TS2322` on `account.tsx`. So a customer asking to centre their logo gets a failed typecheck and the placeholder. `| (string & {})` accepts the widening while keeping both names visible in `COMPONENT_API`, and an unrecognised value falls back to the default arrangement rather than rendering something broken. **`contact` did not hit this** because its fields are plain strings — which is why the hazard is specific to an ENUM prop in the spread-from-const position, and why the next one added needs the same arm.
  - **THE REAL GATE IS THE TOOL'S OWN `enum`, in code.** The type's job here is to TELL, and this repo already records that a cap a model is merely told about is not a cap.
  - **PROVED TO DISCRIMINATE, not assumed**: the closed union is put back and `site build` goes red on *"a const CHROME carrying a layout compiles"*, then green when it is restored. Only a real compile can show it — the unit half can read the type text and nothing more.
- **ONE SLOT FINDER FOR `contact` AND `layout`.** Locating the object literal in a page's source is the hard, comment-hazard-prone half and is IDENTICAL for the two; only the value kinds differ. `applyChromeList` was unified for the same reason one prop family over, and two copies drift toward a scanner that silently stops finding one of them.
- **A FIELD EQUAL TO ITS DEFAULT IS DROPPED, which is what gives both removals a verb with no removal verb of their own.** An empty string takes a contact field away; `sticky: true` puts a header back — and when every axis is at its ordinary value the whole property goes, so **a site restored to the default frame is byte-identical to one that never had a `layout` at all**.
- **CENTRING IS `flex-wrap`, NOT A SECOND LAYOUT.** The brand takes a full row and the rest wraps below it, so the nav, the button and the mobile menu are rendered ONCE. Two branches of duplicated JSX is how one of them stops getting the next fix. **Measured on a real build: one `<nav>` and one menu button in every arrangement.**
- **HEIGHT IS DELIBERATELY NOT AN AXIS.** It reads like a fourth one and it is a coupling: a centred brand stacks onto two rows, where a fixed height either clips it or leaves a gap. The `density` style axis already moves spacing site-wide, which is what "make it slimmer" usually means.
- **`width` MOVES BOTH HALVES.** A full-width header over a contained footer reads as a mistake, so the footer shares the axis — measured on a real build, `1280/1280` for both.
- **RENDERED BEFORE ANYTHING WAS WIRED**, which is this repo's own rule and the method that caught the duplicate phone number an hour earlier. All three arrangements built through the real container and photographed at two widths: default `pos=sticky innerW=1152 brandX=132`, centre `h=88 brandX=640` (dead centre of 1280), full `pos=static innerW=1280`.
- **TWO REAL GAPS FROM THE SWEEP, both mine, and both the same shape:** making `brand` and `sticky` unconditional survived the entire unit suite. The writer wrote them, the response reported them, the customer was told it worked, and the header rendered exactly as before. The guard is DERIVED from the spec — every axis must be READ by the component — so a fourth is covered without anybody remembering the file.
- **TWO MUTANTS WERE PARTIALLY INERT AND REPORTED GAPS THAT WERE NOT THERE**, for the fourteenth recorded time: deleting the digest's HEADING while leaving the lines it labels, and replacing the router clause's opening while leaving its examples. Re-run with anchors that remove what they claim to.
- **AN EXISTING GUARD WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE**, pinned to `<SiteLink href="/" className="font-semibold` — a test about word order, which the test two below it in the same file already records as the lesson. Re-anchored on the property (the wordmark is routed, never a bare anchor).
- 3364 tests, template typechecks, `site build` **135/135** against the real container, **21/21 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit.

## The footer slots only a fresh build could fill — and a comment that blinded the nav lane (2026-08-18)

**The contact slots shipped an hour earlier and were reachable by nothing on an existing site.** The generator fills them on a new build; every site published before that could never get a phone number in its footer short of a ~27-credit rewrite. That is the on-disk-and-reachable-by-nothing shape this repo has deleted 289 files over, arriving in code I had just written.

- **THE `nav` LANE, NOT A NINTH ONE, and the precedent decides it.** `contact` is the same operation `action` already is: one object, spread into every page, changed everywhere at once, on a Haiku call the lane is already paying for. **The `page` lane is explicitly the wrong home** — it edits ONE page, and a footer that differs between pages is the inconsistency its own description warns about.
- **FIELD-LEVEL MERGE, because the alternative is vicious.** "Change the phone number" replacing the whole object wipes the address and the opening hours, and the customer finds out when somebody cannot find the shop. Absent means unchanged, as everywhere else here; **an empty string is the removal verb**, so a field can still be taken away.
- **A NO-OP MUST NOT REPUBLISH.** The writer re-serialises the whole object, so an answer restating what is already there rewrote every page, ran the container and cut a version — and told the customer it worked. Compared on the SERIALISED form, which is the only thing that reaches the file. Found by a test, not by reading.
- **`readNav` HAD AN EARLY RETURN THAT DID NOT KNOW ABOUT `contact`**, so a footer-only answer — the commonest shape this field produces — fell out as `null` before anything could apply it. The whole feature would have been dead on arrival. Also found by a test.
- **AND THE ROUTER SAID "THE TOP OF EVERY PAGE"**, so *"put our number in the footer"* had no reason to arrive at this lane at all. Without that sentence every layer below it is unreachable — the same trap one level up, which is why it is asserted.

**AND THEN THE PART THAT WAS NOT MINE: BOTH BRACKET SCANNERS WERE COMMENT-BLIND.**

- **AN APOSTROPHE IN A COMMENT DISABLED THE ENTIRE NAV LANE FOR THAT PAGE.** `// The footer's small print.` inside a `const CHROME` block opened a string `braceEnd` never closed, so it ran to the end of the file, returned -1, and the page yielded **NO SLOT AT ALL** — no menu, no button, no contact, silently. My own comment triggered it; the bug was already there.
- **MEASURED, AND THE NUMBERS ARE WHY IT SURVIVED SO LONG.** Of 307 corpus pages with a nav or CHROME structure, **112 carry a comment with an unbalanced quote** and exactly **ONE** broke — because the hazard only fires when the quote sits INSIDE the span being scanned. Rare enough to survive every test, and triggered by writing an ordinary English comment in the wrong place.
- **THE FIX IS SURGICAL, AND THAT WAS PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Both scanners are shared by every menu and button edit on the platform, so old and new were imported side by side and diffed across the corpus: **identical on 328 of 329 pages**, the one difference being `index.tsx` getting its action slot back. Which is the bug.
- **THE ORDER IS THE PROPERTY**, not the comment skipper itself: quotes are handled BEFORE comments, so a `//` inside a string stays a string. My first mutation of that was **inert** (it needed an `x/` outside a string to fire); hoisting the comment check above the quote block is the honest one and dies against five tests. **Thirteenth recorded inert mutant.**
- **An existing guard was pinned to the exact phrase `THE TOP OF EVERY PAGE`** and went red on a correct change — the word-order own-goal, re-anchored on the property (this lane is the same on every page, hyphenated or not).
- **PROVEN AGAINST A REAL BUILD, which is what the unit half cannot show.** A site with its contact block stripped out — the shape of every site published before today — was given one by `applyContact`, and the four edited files compiled in the real container and rendered clean at three widths with the new number and a two-line address. 3332 unit tests, **17/17 mutations as expected** including a comment-only control that survived. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit.
- **`social` AND `legal` FOLLOWED THE SAME HOUR**, so all three footer slots are now reachable on a site published before they existed. **ONE IMPLEMENTATION FOR BOTH, because they are the same thing wearing different field names**: a short array of two-string objects, spread into every page, meaningful only as a whole. `contact` earned its own reader because it is a MERGE (naming the phone must not wipe the address); a list is not — "our socials are Instagram and Facebook" replaces it, exactly as a menu change does. An EMPTY array is the removal verb.
  - **AN ALL-REFUSED LIST IS NOT "EMPTY IT", and collapsing the two wipes a footer nobody asked to change.** A `legal` link to a page the site has not got is refused — it would be a not-found from the bottom of EVERY page — and a list whose every entry we refused is reported rather than applied.
  - **NOT THE SHAPE WE UNDERSTAND IS LEFT ALONE.** An array holding a spread or a variable refuses the slot rather than being overwritten, since replacing it would drop the binding — the skip-rather-than-guess rule `navSlots` already lives under.
  - **TWO REAL GAPS CAME OUT OF THE SWEEP, both mine.** Only the `const CHROME` form was exercised, so the step that eats the JSX `}` of `social={[…]}` was covered by nothing; and the runner test checked `changed` and the reply and never THE PAGES IT HANDS BACK, so returning the pre-apply pages — which is what gets published — passed everything. Both closed and re-proven.
  - **PROVEN AGAINST A REAL BUILD**: three social icons and a Privacy link, written by `applyChromeList` into a site that had neither, compiled in the real container and rendered clean at three widths. 3343 tests, **26/26 mutations as expected** with a comment-only control that survived.

## The footer could only ever say four things (2026-08-18)

**Asked what the FRONTEND was still missing and diffed what a build can produce against what the lanes can change.** The answer was not an edit-lane gap at all: `SiteFooter` took `brand`, `tagline`, `links`, `className` and **that was the entire prop list**, with `SiteChrome` its only caller. So the bottom of every generated page could render the business's name, one tagline, the same links as the header, and © year. **No phone. No address. No opening hours. No social links. No privacy or terms link. Ever** — not by typing, and not by rebuilding either, because there was no slot to fill.

- **THE MODEL WAS ALREADY WORKING AROUND IT, which is what makes this more than a missing feature.** 302 of 318 exemplars use `SiteChrome`, so it is effectively every site — and **165 of 318 put a phone or an address somewhere on the PAGE**, because page content was the only place available. `social-links.tsx` has been in the kit the whole time, referenced by the chrome zero times: on disk and reachable by nothing, the property the 27 blocks and the 196 examples were deleted for.
- **PROPS ON `CHROME`, NOT A SITE-LEVEL STORE, and the precedent decided it.** `links` and `action` are already repeated per page in a `const CHROME` object spread into every route (261 exemplars do exactly that), and the `nav` lane already edits them all at once. Contact details are the same kind of fact, so they follow the same path — and they are string literals in page source, which is what makes them reachable by the CHEAP lane. **Measured through the real `extractText`: `phone`, `email`, `hours` and `address` are all offered as editable**, so "the phone number is wrong" is a ~0.3-credit text edit rather than a rebuild.
- **THE `tel:` LINK IS DERIVED FROM THE NUMBER, never a second prop.** A phone number that is not tappable is the commonest small-business footer fault and most visitors to these sites are on a phone. The caller writes it as a person writes it; `telHref` strips to digits and a leading `+`, and the DISPLAYED text is never touched — how a business writes its own number is a local convention and normalising it would be us overruling them on their own page.
- **`hours` IS ONE LINE AND DELIBERATELY NOT STRUCTURED.** A footer wants "Tue–Fri 9–6"; a real timetable is rows in a table, where the `data` lane already owns it. A structured prop here would be a second, worse place to keep opening hours, and the two would disagree.
- **`legal` IS SEPARATE FROM `links`, and not for tidiness** — one array would put Privacy in the header nav. It is also the one slot the reference page deliberately does NOT demonstrate: the template has no privacy route, and an example linking to a page it has not built teaches exactly the dangling link the router refuses. The rule carries it instead, bounded to pages that exist.
- **THE DUPLICATE PHONE WAS FOUND BY LOOKING, and nothing else could have found it.** The reference page carries its number as a nav link *because for a barber ringing is the action* — so with `contact` added it printed **twice on one footer row**, which reads as a fault rather than a convention. The footer now drops a `tel:`/`mailto:` nav entry when `contact` supplies that channel; the header keeps its copy. **BY CHANNEL, NEVER BY NUMBER**: `0114 270 0000` and `+441142700000` are one line written two ways and matching them needs country-code knowledge this kit refuses elsewhere (`phone-input` does not validate for the same reason). The cost, stated: a business with a bookings line in the nav and a main line in `contact` loses the nav one from the FOOTER, and still has it in the header.
- **AND THE REFERENCE PAGE THEN STATED TWO ADDRESSES FOR ONE BUSINESS** — my footer said 18 Cutler Row while its own find-us section said 14. Found by measuring which strings the text lane offers, not by reading. Corrected, and the same block was given to the other three reference routes: the footer is chrome, so a site whose footer changes between pages is the inconsistency the rule tells the model to avoid.
- **THE EMBEDDED COPIES ARE SYNCED FROM DISK BY SCRIPT NOW, not by hand.** `page-gen.mjs` reproduces every reference page inside a template literal and a drift guard enforces "the file wins" — which is only safe if applying it is mechanical. Escapes backticks and `${` and nothing else, or the copy stops being the file.
- **Everything is optional and an absent slot renders byte-identically to before**, which is what makes it safe against every site already published.
- 3309 unit tests, template typechecks, `site runtime` 47/47 in a real browser (the derived `tel:` href, the two-line address, the de-duplicated number, and the header keeping its own), `site build` 132/132 against the real container, **7/7 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline including a comment-only control that had to survive. **Dark is NOT verified by the footer harness** — it sets `prefers-color-scheme` and this theme keys dark off a class, so the shots are identical; `theme-render` asserts light and dark differ and passed 7/7 with the change in place. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the model account is out of credit, so no customer build has carried it.

## Three more things a customer could not change by typing (2026-08-18)

**Asked what the edit path still could not reach, and the answer was three separate holes — two of them live on real generated sites right now.** All three are structural editors: find a literal in the stored TSX, rewrite it in place, back to front per file so offsets stay valid. No page generation.

- **458 LINKS LIVED IN THE PAGE COPY AND ONLY THE `page` LANE COULD TOUCH ONE, a page at a time.** The `nav` lane owned the chrome — the menu and the header button, both site-wide — and everything else was one page, one model call, for a destination. **The `text` lane cannot help by design**: it refuses `href` and `to` by name, which is the rule that stops it rewriting a route id. Measured over the 337 pages the generator learns from and writes: 600 links in the chrome, 458 in the bodies. They move together now, on the Haiku call the lane already pays for.
  - **NAMED BY THEIR WORDS, because that is how a customer refers to one** — "the Send an enquiry link", not "the third anchor". Matched on words, destination, or both; given both, both must match, which tells `Book now -> /book` apart from `Book now -> /classes` on a site that has each. **A change naming NEITHER matches nothing**, deliberately: an entry carrying only a destination to move TO is a model that answered the field and not the question, and applying it to every link on the site is the worst thing this lane could do.
  - **A TYPED LINK CAN ONLY EVER POINT AT A REAL PAGE.** `<Link to>` and `<SiteLink>` are checked against the route tree `tsr generate` emits, so an anchor or a `tel:` there is not a degraded link — it is a build that FAILS and a customer left with the site they had plus a bill. Refused with a reason; the plain `<a>` in the same change still goes through.
  - **MY FIRST DRAFT'S CHROME EXCLUSION WAS INERT AND WRONG, and the way that was settled is the lesson.** It skipped everything syntactically inside a `<SiteChrome>` tag to stop two lanes owning one href. Measured: it excluded **ZERO of 458** — the menu is an array of `{label, href}` objects and the button is one object, so neither can hold a JSX link tag — and it was WRONG in the one shape it CAN fire on: `footer={<a href="/terms">Terms</a>}` is legal today and reachable by neither other scanner, so skipping it made that link editable by nothing. Deleted, and the property it was standing in for (**no href owned by two scanners**) is asserted directly: 306 menus and 306 buttons against 458 body links, zero overlaps. **A guard that fires on nothing is not automatically safe to keep — check which case it DOES fire on.**
  - **THE CAP WAS ONE NUMBER DOING TWO JOBS.** `linkSlots` feeds the APPLY as well as the digest, so a bound tuned to what a model can read silently leaves links this lane cannot see — the customer asks for every "Book now" to move, some do, and nothing says which. Caught because the corpus sat at **exactly 400** under the old number, which is a truncation wearing the shape of a clean run. What the model is SHOWN is bounded separately.

- **A CAFÉ'S OPENING HOURS READ FRIDAY, MONDAY, SATURDAY — in committed generator output.** `useRows<Hour>("hours", { order: "day", dir: "asc" })`, day names sorted alphabetically. This file already records the same failure on a pizzeria's menu (Dessert, Pizza, Starters) and the fix was a rule in the generator's prompt: **that makes it happen less often and does nothing for the owner looking at it today.**
  - **THE ARGUMENT IS THE WHOLE MECHANISM.** The database has no opinion, `defaultSort` is parsed and acted on by nothing, and the Data panel shows rows in fetch order — so what a visitor sees is decided by a string in the page source. The `page` lane could rewrite ONE page, and a table read on the home page and the menu page then comes out in two different orders: the failure `orderingMoved` can report and cannot fix.
  - **IT RIDES ON THE DATA LANE'S EXISTING CALL**, so no new router layer and no prompt-token tax. The lists block is omitted when there are none, so a caller that passes no pages sends the request it always sent.
  - **THE COLUMN IS CHECKED HERE, NOT LEFT TO POSTGREST.** A sort by a column the table does not have answers 400, `useRows` turns that into the list's error state, and the owner gets an EMPTY list on every page reading it — worse than the order they disliked, and silent. `id` and `created_at` are always allowed, because the schema engine stamps both on every table and neither is in the declared list, so without them "put the newest at the top" is refused.
  - **EDITED IN PLACE, NEVER REBUILT.** The options object holds things that are not ours — `usePublicRows("appointments", { date })` is a filter — so only the span between the quotes moves. **THREE ANSWERS FOR THE SECOND ARGUMENT** and collapsing any two is a bug: none means insert, a literal means rewrite, anything else means refuse and say so.
  - **THE DATA LANE PUBLISHES FOR THE FIRST TIME, on exactly one path.** Rows are live the moment they commit; an order lives in the page source. Two existing guards rested on "the data lane never publishes" — a premise that legitimately changed — and both now assert the real property: the publish is gated on the reorder, and **every compile-failure branch is checked for its own survival promise** rather than two totals compared across the whole block, which passes when one branch says it twice and another not at all.
  - **STILL UNREACHABLE AT ANY PRICE: an arbitrary sequence.** "Put the Fade above the Beard trim" is not a sort at any column — it needs a position stored per row, which is a schema change, a backfill and a page rewrite. The tool says so, so the model does not fake it with a column that happens to put those two the right way round and moves everything else.

- **HALF A MESSAGE WAS DROPPED IN SILENCE AND THE REPLY CALLED THE OTHER HALF A SUCCESS.** `layer` is one value and always has been, so "make the background yellow and add a booking form" does the colour and answers `✅ Updated the look — page colour.` **Structural, not probabilistic**: there was no field a second ask could arrive in, so it was silent every time.
  - **A NOTE, NEVER AN ACTION.** Nothing branches on `alsoAsked`, so a wrong one costs a sentence about something they did not ask for and never a wrong change — asserted by driving the router with and without it and requiring the decision to be byte-identical. That asymmetry is what makes it safe to ship without live verification.
  - **TWO THINGS SAID ABOUT ONE CHANGE IS STILL ONE CHANGE**, and getting that wrong is the failure mode. Yellow background AND rounder corners is a single look edit; Book first AND drop Prices is a single menu edit; the phone number in the header AND the footer is one wording edit in two places. It counts only when the leftover goes to a DIFFERENT layer, and the description closes on *when in doubt, say nothing*.
  - **NOT ON A BUILD**, which rewrites everything and folds a second ask in by construction, and not on `ask`/`clarify`, where no work happened for a leftover to sit beside.
  - **THE THIRTEENTH FIELD OF THIS SHAPE.** Twelve have been decided correctly and dropped between `readRouting` and the client, and from outside "the model did not set it" and "we did not forward it" are the same absence. Added WITH its wire; the addon rung had no routing decision in scope at all until now.

- **A MUTANT PROVED MY OWN GUARD VACUOUS, one layer further out than usual:** asserting `alsoTail` exists and is called by both replies passes perfectly against one that returns `""`. The works-but-cannot-say-so disease applied to a test. The sentence's own content is asserted now.
- **THE WORD-ORDER OWN-GOAL, TWICE MORE.** A guard pinned to `finish(editReply(e) + renderTail(e))` went red the moment a second honest tail was appended — reporting *"the edit reply drops the render sentence"* about a reply that carries it perfectly. Both it and the new one assert the property.
- 3298 tests, template typechecks, **43 mutations as expected across three sweeps** from verified-green baselines, each with a comment-only control that had to survive. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — merged with `[skip smoke]`, so no paid build ran; every edit route was probed unauthenticated on the deployed Worker and answers 401 with the control answering 404, which proves reachability and nothing about what the models answer.

## Three families died of the same error and three different causes (2026-08-18)

**`festival /`, `entertainer /` and `box-office /event` all failed with minified React #418 `args[]=text` — a hydration mismatch — and not one of the causes was in the family page.** These are the pages the generator is SHOWN as the known-good example for those trades, so a broken one teaches the model the broken thing.

- **THE ICU DIVERGENCE IS THE ONE WORTH KNOWING, because no amount of locale-pinning reaches it.** `date-enquiry.tsx` asked for `Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-GB", {weekday:"short", day, month:"long", year})` in one call: Node (ICU 78.2) returns `"Sat, 19 September 2026"`, this Chromium returns `"Sat 19 September 2026"`. **Same locale, same timezone, one comma.** `pinSiteLocale` makes an ABSENT locale mean the site's and deliberately leaves an explicit one alone — correct, and irrelevant here, because both sides already agreed on `en-GB`. This is two ICU **versions** disagreeing about one locale's pattern.
- **IT CANNOT BE FIXED BY MATCHING ENGINES, which is why the fix is to stop asking.** We SSR in workerd and hydrate in whatever browser the visitor brought, at whatever ICU it ships — so any format whose output depends on the ICU version WILL mismatch for a share of real visitors however the build machine is configured. **The proof that agreement is luck: CI's Node and CI's Chromium DO agree on that date, so `family apps` was green on `entertainer /` while it failed here on the same commit** — and CI's own single failure was `box-office /event`, a family nobody had reported. `pretty()` now composes two formatters with a separator of ours; both halves were measured stable on their own.
- **THE SCOPE WAS MEASURED, NOT GENERALISED, and that changed the rule twice.** 237 weekday/month/day/year combinations across en-GB/en-US/en: **12 differ and all twelve are en-GB with a weekday AND a day AND a month in one formatter.** Across 16 locales, only en-GB. So the guard is en-GB-shaped rather than a blanket ban — and since `pinSiteLocale` resolves an absent locale to `SITE_LANG`, and en-GB is the platform's primary market, the 12 kit components still in that family are live on UK sites (`event-card` alone is on 11 exemplar pages). Held as an exactly-equal **ratchet** in `test/site-locale.test.mjs`: a thirteenth fails, and a fixed one cannot sit in the list looking like debt still owed.
- **`timeZoneName: "shortOffset"` DIVERGES AT ZERO OFFSET, AND SO DOES `"longOffset"`** — `GMT+0` / `GMT+00:00` against `GMT`. Measured both, which killed the obvious fix of asking for the wider form. It is the zero offset specifically: London, Paris, New York, Tokyo, Kolkata, Sydney and Chatham all agree. That bit because **`"UTC"` is in `timezone-picker`'s own default list**, so every server-rendered picker carried an option the browser then disagreed with. It computes the offset from numeric parts now and writes the string itself.
- **A COUNTDOWN CANNOT BE SERVER-RENDERED, and calling that a downgrade gets it backwards.** `countdown.tsx` seeded state with `target - Date.now()`, so the `sec` field differed whenever the SSR-to-hydration gap crossed a second — a race in the harness, and at real network latency the ordinary case. Whatever the server serialises is stale before anybody sees it, so the old behaviour was not "the right answer, early", it was a wrong answer presented as fact. It renders `--` and fills in on mount; **measured on a real render, the effect lands before the screenshot, so the placeholder is one frame.** Deliberately NOT `suppressHydrationWarning`, though React documents it for exactly this: it silences the error while leaving the mismatch real, and it has to sit on the precise text node, so it stops covering the moment the tree changes — silently.
- **EVERY PAGE VIEW WAS FILING AN ERROR REPORT.** `error-reporting.ts` POSTs to the site's own `_errors`, so a countdown on a published site put a row in the owner's error panel per visitor. A hydration mismatch is not only a console error.
- **`solar` WAS NEVER BROKEN.** `timed out after 150s` is `STEP_TIMEOUT` — container load in a 100-family sequential run — and it builds and renders on its own and in CI. Recorded because a timeout in a long sequential run reads exactly like a defect in whatever family it lands on.
- **MY OWN GUARD FAILED AGAINST CORRECT CODE FIRST, on the recurring own-goal.** `indexOf("resolvedOptions()")` found the sentence in the comment EXPLAINING the fix, which sits above the effect it was asserting came first — **prose describing a bug contains the bug's spelling.** Comments are blanked before any ordering or absence is judged here, length-preserving and by WHOLE LINE (blanking from any `//` would eat a line holding a URL).
- 3055 tests, template and kit typecheck clean, **7/7 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline (6 caught plus a comment-only control that had to survive). Proven by rendering: all 28 pages across the 10 families that use a changed component. **`timezone-picker` is verified by measurement and typecheck only — no exemplar renders it.**

## The packaged Worker was never RUN, and both bugs in it were total (2026-08-17)

**Every check on the packaged script was a fact about the FILE — its size, its imports, the strings in it — and all of them passed on a bundle that could not serve a page.** Executing one costs about a second, needs no Cloudflare account and no credits, and found two separate bugs, each of which would have broken every page of every site on its first live build.

- **THE SHELL WAS THE LITERAL TEXT `[object Object]`, FIFTEEN CHARACTERS, ON EVERY ROUTE.** The call site passed `dist["index.html"]` and `collectDist` returns every text file as `{t: "…"}` — so `String(shell || "")` coerced an object into a perfectly valid string. The config module generated, vite bundled it, the size check passed, the script would have uploaded. **The same family as `String(["a","b"]) === "a,b"`**, which this file has recorded three times: a coercion that turns a shape mistake into a value nothing downstream can tell from a real one. `siteConfigModule` now REFUSES a non-string rather than coercing, and `packageWorker` gates on `shellIsUsable` before it builds anything.
- **`canServeAsWorker` WAS WRITTEN TO CATCH EXACTLY THIS AND HAD NO CALLERS.** Its own comment says a shell with no root element must be refused; it sat correct and unreachable while every document came out as fifteen characters. Its `ssr` half cannot be asked inside `packageWorker` (that build produces the SSR bundle itself), and **a gate that can only be half-asked is a gate nobody wires** — so the shell half is `shellIsUsable`, which has a caller.
- **THE ASSERTION MEANT TO CATCH IT WAS SATISFIED BY THE CODE THAT LOOKS FOR THE THING.** `w.code.includes('<div id="root">')`, titled *"…and the HTML shell to render into"* — and `entry.js` declares `const SLOT = '<div id="root">'`, the string it searches the shell FOR. So the bundle contained it whatever the shell was. Vacuous, and green throughout.
- **THE SECOND BUG WAS `ReferenceError: window is not defined` ON EVERY ROUTE, and it was silent by design.** `entry.js` catches a render failure and serves the bare shell, so the site answered **200** with an empty `<div id="root">` and none of the page's own words — a working status code over exactly the blank-to-a-crawler state this whole move exists to end.
