  - **`ssr.target: "webworker"` PUT `browser` IN THE CONDITION SET AND NOT `workerd`.** So `@tanstack/router-core` resolved `./isServer` to its CLIENT variant, `RouterCore.update` believed it was in a browser, and `createRouter` threw — even though `entry-server.tsx` correctly passes a memory history and touches no browser global itself.
  - **BOTH LIBRARIES THAT DECIDE THIS SHIP THE ANSWER**, which is why the fix is a condition and not a patch: `router-core`'s `./isServer` maps `workerd` to its server build, and `react-dom`'s `./server` maps `workerd` to `server.edge.js` — the Cloudflare build — where `node` would give `server.node.js` and pull in `node:stream`. Neither pure choice works alone: webworker conditions break the router, node conditions break react-dom.
  - **ORDER IN `resolve.conditions` IS NOT WHAT DECIDES.** Export-map resolution walks the MAP's keys and takes the first present in the set, so membership is what matters. `browser` is kept as the fallback for packages with no worker condition: being wrong toward a browser build costs a guarded global, being wrong toward Node pulls in a builtin the runtime does not have. `react-server` is deliberately absent — it is the first key in react-dom's map and selects the RSC build.
- **THE DURABLE FIX IS THAT `site-build` NOW EXECUTES THE BUNDLE**, with a fake R2, and asserts the home page renders into the real shell **with the page's own copy in it**, that a declared route is 200 and an undeclared one 404, and that an asset comes off the bucket. **NODE IS NOT WORKERD, so this is a ONE-WAY filter and it says so**: passing does not prove it runs on Cloudflare, failing proves it does not.
  - **THE PAGE-COPY ASSERTION HAD TO NAME A SENTENCE ONLY THE PAGE HAS.** `Fold Coffee` is the brand, so it is in the shell's own `<title>` and would have passed with the render producing nothing at all — the same vacuous shape as the shell assertion it sits beside.
- **AND MY REPLACEMENT STATIC ASSERTION WAS WRONG ABOUT THE BUNDLER, TWICE IN ONE REGEX.** It looked for `export const SHELL = "…` and the emitted code is `const SHELL = '<!doctype html>…'` — the keyword is gone because `site-config.js` is inlined, and rollup re-quotes the value with single quotes and bare inner double quotes. **A regex written for the SOURCE spelling describes a file that is never produced.**
- **`entry.js` LOGS THE STACK, NOT JUST THE MESSAGE.** `window is not defined` names no module and the bundle is one file of ~950,000 characters, so the message alone cost a separate diagnostic harness to answer a question the stack states in one line. Sixth recorded instance of a failure that could not name itself.
- **AND THE HARNESS COLLIDED WITH ITSELF ON A FIXED PORT**, which this file already records: `site-build.mjs` binds 8123, so a previous run still finishing makes the next one die with `EADDRINUSE` — a failure that reads like a code regression and is not.
- **THE `workerd` CONDITION HAD NO GUARD BUT A ~20-MINUTE INTEGRATION RUN**, which is not a guard: a property whose only proof takes twenty minutes regresses between runs. Four source-reads now hold what the unit suite can hold — the condition set (with `react-server` refused, since it is the FIRST key in react-dom's map and would outrank `workerd`), the shell gate and its ORDER (after the vite build it costs a container run to learn what a string comparison knew; after the staging write it leaves one site's config for the next build), the call site passing text rather than the dist entry, and the stack in the log.
- **`packageWorker` CANNOT REJECT, verified across all four contributors rather than assumed** — `run()` resolves on close, error and timeout alike; `routePaths()` catches and returns `[]`; `shellIsUsable` cannot throw; the staging is in a try. That matters because the call sits inside the route's own try, so a rejection would turn an optional step into a failed build — **on the cheap-edit spine as well**, where the dist already succeeded. The property is asserted from outside by the no-slug case, which comes back `ok: true` with `worker.ok: false`.
- **THE DEPLOY RISK WAS CLOSED BY EVIDENCE, NOT BY READING.** A binding naming a namespace that does not exist fails the WHOLE deploy — three merges shipped nothing that way on 2026-08-07 — and `527483b` deployed **green** with `dispatch_namespaces` already in `wrangler.jsonc`, so `gofarther-sites` resolves.
- 3008 tests, `site build` **137/137** against the real container, **9/9 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline (8 caught plus a comment-only control that had to survive).

## The TanStack survey, and the two gaps it found (2026-08-17)

**Asked what we have and what we do not, and the useful half was neither.** 9 direct packages, 22 including transitive. Three are load-bearing — **Router** (file-based routing, `tsr generate`, 324 exemplars), **Query** (wrapped entirely by `rows.ts`, so the model never imports it and cannot misuse it), **Virtual** (one kit component, `VirtualList`, reachable through `COMPONENT_API`). One is installed and forbidden — **Form**. Three are installed and reachable by nothing — **Table**, **Store**, **Pacer**, about 12 MB of install between them and Form.

- **"WHY DROP THEM WHEN WE CAN USE THEM TO MAKE IT BETTER" IS THE RIGHT INSTINCT AND THE ANSWER DIFFERS PER PACKAGE.** For two of the four, adopting would make it worse for a reason this repo has already paid for. **`react-table`**: the kit has ~80 hand-written table/sort/filter/search components and they are not shells — `multi-sort.tsx` exports real `toggleSort`/`applySort`. Replacing them with a foreign API is the trade already rejected when 400 bought components were measured: *"we own these APIs, so the usage notes are accurate by construction"*, and the proof was `message-scroller`, which typechecked, bundled and hard-crashed the page. **`react-form`**: shadcn's `Form` IS react-hook-form's `FormProvider`, so adopting means rewriting the kit, retraining 324 exemplars and every rule, for nothing a customer sees.
- **THE GAPS THE OTHER TWO POINT AT ARE REAL, AND THE PACKAGES ARE NOT THE RIGHT SHAPE FOR THEM.** Both were built here instead.
- **A SEARCH BOX SPENT THE SITE'S READ ALLOWANCE PER KEYSTROKE.** Measured: `search-input.tsx` and `table-search.tsx` carry no timers at all, and reads share ONE rate-limit bucket for the whole site (`DEFAULT_READ_PER_MIN` 300) — so one visitor typing steadily can exhaust the allowance for every other visitor's every other page. Settled inside `useRows`/`usePublicRows` rather than exposed as a hook a page must reach for, which is the `safe-image` and `spam-guard` rule: **a guard the model must remember is one it eventually forgets**, and this one's forgetting is invisible until a site is busy. Ten lines against 3.9 MB.
  - **COMPARED BY VALUE, NEVER IDENTITY, and that is the whole difficulty.** Every caller writes `useRows("menu", { order: "name" })` — a fresh object each render — so an effect keyed on the object restarts its timer every render and NEVER fires: the list freezes on its first result for good.
  - **THE PROBE THAT CAUGHT IT HAD TO BE MADE NOISIER.** The first version of the search page re-rendered only when you typed, so the identity mutant still worked and SURVIVED. It bites only when something else re-renders during the settle window — a clock, a countdown, a carousel, a "3 left" badge, which a generated site has more often than not. The page ticks now, and the mutant dies.
- **A BASKET COULD NOT SURVIVE LEAVING THE PAGE.** Measured over the corpus the generator learns from: **0 of 324 exemplars** hold any state across routes and **0 use `useCheckout` at all**, so "add here, check out there" had no shape on a platform that takes card payments. `useCart(table)` is that, on React's own `useSyncExternalStore` — **so `react-store` did not earn its place after all**, which corrects what I said an hour earlier.
  - **THERE IS NOWHERE IN A LINE TO PUT A PRICE.** `parseCart` drops any price, total or currency a browser sends; a basket carrying them would be inviting a page to send them. Caps mirror the server's own `MAX_CART_LINES` 50 / `MAX_LINE_QTY` 999, because a basket the server will refuse is a checkout that fails at the till after the customer has filled it.
  - **IT EMPTIES ITSELF ON `?paid=`** — the Stripe return URL. Without it the basket they just bought is still sitting there, which invites paying twice. Done here rather than asked of the page, same rule as the debounce.
  - **EVERY MUTATOR READS THE STORE, NEVER THE RENDERED LINES.** Two calls in one tick — a double-clicked Add — would otherwise both start from the same snapshot and the second would erase the first. Caught by a button that adds twice in one handler.
  - **A CORRUPT STORED BASKET DEGRADES, NEVER THROWS.** That value is hand-editable and survives deploys; parsed carelessly it throws during render and the shop is broken for that visitor for good.
- **THREE OF SEVEN MUTANTS SURVIVED THE FIRST SWEEP AND THE THIRD IS THE INTERESTING ONE.** My corrupt-basket fixture gave every bad line an invalid QUANTITY, so the quantity check caught them all and the id check never had to fire — the fixture now uses an object, a boolean and an empty string as ids, which only it can refuse. And the hydration mutant is **INERT**: React calls `getServerSnapshot` for the CLIENT's hydration render too, so no mismatch occurs whether or not `ready` gates it. **My comment credited `ready` with preventing it and that was false** — corrected in the source to the two things `ready` really does, because a comment crediting a line with something it does not do is what gets believed when somebody deletes it.
- **AN EXISTING GUARD FALSE-ALARMED ON `<Button>`**, which `button.tsx` certainly exports — as `export { Button, buttonVariants }`, the form every shadcn primitive uses, and the guard knew only `export function` and `export const`. Latent until a rule example happened to cite a shadcn primitive rather than one of ours. Fixed in the CHECK rather than the rule, with a negative case beside it so widening the pattern could not turn the guard off instead of fixing it.
- **AND THE GENERATOR IS TOLD**, which is the step whose absence is this file's most-recorded failure. Rule 16 now names `useCart` with a worked call, since a capability nothing can reach is the exact shape the 27 blocks and 196 examples were deleted for.
- 3038 tests, `site build` **142/142**, `site runtime` **40/40** in a real browser, **7/7 mutations as expected** (5 caught, 1 proven inert, 1 control). **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against either feature.
- **AND THEN ALL FOUR CAME OUT (owner's call, same day).** `react-form`, `react-pacer`, `react-store`, `react-table` — 22 packages to 12, and `@tanstack/react-start` left the lockfile with them, since it was only there as `react-form`'s optional peer.
  - **THE RULE THAT DECIDED IT: rent engines, own vocabularies.** A library the model NEVER NAMES costs zero prompt tokens, cannot be misused, and can be swapped underneath — `react-query` and `react-virtual` are both invisible behind `useRows` and `VirtualList`. A library the model IMPORTS is an API we did not write, cannot describe accurately, and cannot change; `message-scroller` typechecked, bundled and hard-crashed the page for exactly that reason. **That is the test for the next dependency, TanStack or otherwise: will the model import this directly?**
  - **MEGABYTES ARE THE WRONG UNIT.** Vite tree-shakes, so a visitor paid nothing for any of them. The recurring cost is the prompt — `PAGE_RULES` is **~36,000 tokens on every build**, and a change to it re-writes the cache (32 credits cold against 21 warm). Describing a foreign library is a bill you pay forever.
  - **THE SECOND RULE: a dependency exists only if something imports it.** Not "might be useful" — that is invisible debt nobody re-evaluates, and it is the on-disk-and-reachable-by-nothing property the 27 blocks and 196 examples were deleted for. Reversible in one command, which is what makes removing it the safe direction.
  - **`react-form`'s RULE AND LINT WENT WITH THE PACKAGE**, on the `@/examples/*` precedent exactly: they existed BECAUSE it was installed — an import typechecked clean (exit 0, measured), so the page compiled, lint-warned and PUBLISHED a form that silently did not validate, since `problems` is computed and nothing branches on it. Uninstalled, `tsc` refuses the import for free. **A test asserts the PREMISE — the four are absent, the three that are reachable are present — rather than the rules it removed**, because reinstalling one without restoring them brings the silent failure straight back. Proven to fire in both directions.
  - **WHEN `react-table` LEGITIMATELY COMES BACK:** the day `DataTable` needs sorting AND pagination AND column state together — and then behind our own component the way `react-query` is, never named in the prompt. `pacer` and `store` will not: the debounce now lives where it cannot be forgotten and `useSyncExternalStore` is in React.
  - **`git checkout package.json` TO UNDO A TEST MUTATION ALSO UNDID THE REAL REMOVAL**, leaving `package.json` carrying four packages the lockfile had dropped — which is a broken `npm ci` in the build container, i.e. every build on the platform. Caught by re-reading the file rather than trusting the command. The sweep scripts use a sidecar for this exact reason; a one-off mutation deserves the same.

## The first live build: a lost link preview, and a delete that reported success (2026-08-17)

**One real build through the whole path, and it found two bugs no static check could have.** Both are the same shape one layer apart — a thing that was true when it was written and stopped being true when something moved underneath it.

- **THE SHELL WAS BAKED INTO THE SCRIPT, AND THAT COST EVERY PUBLISHED SITE ITS LINK PREVIEW.** The comment stated the reasoning at length: it is *"byte-identical for every request to this site, so reading it from R2 would put a round trip in front of every page view to learn something known at upload time."* **Both halves were false.** `injectMeta` adds the description, the Open Graph tags and the twitter card in the platform Worker at PUBLISH time — which is *after* the container has produced the bundle the script is packaged from — so a baked shell is always the version from before the tags existed. Measured on the first live build: a real page whose entire head was `<title>Forno</title>`. WhatsApp, Slack and iMessage fetch the HTML once and read the head; they do not run JavaScript, which is the whole reason `injectMeta` exists.
  - **THE COST ARGUMENT WAS BACKWARDS TOO, and that is what settles it.** Before this tier existed, serving a page WAS one R2 get. So the round trip the note refused to spend is not a new cost — it is the old one, and refusing it bought nothing while losing the tags.
  - **PER ROUTE, so the per-page previews survive.** Every route is prerendered to its own document with its own meta; serving `index.html` for all of them would put the home card on every address, which is the bug the per-page injection was written to fix.
  - **A MISS AND A THROW ARE TREATED COMPLETELY DIFFERENTLY, and the asymmetry is the safety net for the OTHER bug here.** Nothing published means the site is gone, so a miss answers **404** — a delete removes the files and then the script, so when the script removal fails a baked shell would keep serving a site whose every trace has been erased. A throw is transient and keeps the baked shell, because an R2 blip must not take a live site down. `SHELL` survives as exactly that fallback. No window to race: a publish OVERWRITES `index.html` rather than deleting it first.
- **CLOUDFLARE ANSWERS 200 WITH `{"success": false}`, AND READING THE STATUS ALONE IS WHY A DELETED SITE KEPT SERVING.** A real `build smoke` run deleted a site — 21 R2 objects gone, the Neon project dropped, the ownership row removed — and it was **still answering 200 fifteen minutes later**, because its script was never removed and the delete reported success. The v4 API refuses a class of calls that way; a status-only check turns "we refused" into "done", silently. Precisely the `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA auth` shape: a statement that reported success and changed nothing.
  - **`success` IS ONLY BELIEVED WHEN IT IS PRESENT AND FALSE.** A 204 carries no body and a body that will not parse is not evidence of refusal, so it can only ever DEMOTE a status that was otherwise fine.
  - **AND MY OWN TEST FOUND THE BUG IN THAT FIX.** Cloudflare sends `success: false` with **404** too, as it does for every error status — so the first draft turned already-gone into a refusal, on the commonest path there is (most deletes are of sites that never had a script). A `conclusive` list keeps 404 meaning done. Found by writing the test, not by reasoning about it.
- **THE ORPHAN SWEEPER BROKE IN TWO WAYS AT ONCE, and both were consequences of the fixes above.** It exited 0 when `/s/<slug>/` answered 404 — which used to prove there was nothing to clean and now proves nothing at all, because a leaked script over an empty prefix answers exactly the same 404 as no script. **So it would have refused to clean precisely the case this failure produces.** It sweeps either way now (idempotent and cheap on an empty slug; the alternative leaves a billed script holding a name nothing else can remove), and it reads the route's own `workerRemoved` — the only thing that CAN prove the script went. Three states, one failure: `false` names Cloudflare's code, because 10000 (token scope) and 1404 (product not enabled) need completely different actions, and `undefined` is the Worker having nothing to say, printed rather than passed silently.
- **EXECUTING THE BUNDLE IS WHAT FOUND EVERYTHING, and it costs about a second.** The harness had asserted on the script's TEXT; running it against a fake R2 immediately caught two total failures that every static check passed — `[object Object]` as the whole document (the call site passed the dist entry rather than its text, and `String()` obliged), and `window is not defined` on every route (`ssr.target: "webworker"` resolves the `browser` condition, not `workerd`; fixed with an explicit `resolve.conditions`). **`canServeAsWorker` was written to catch exactly the first one and had zero callers** — split into `shellIsUsable` so it has one.
- **The recurring own-goals, all three of them again in one sitting:** an ordering guard of mine found `writeSiteDistToR2` in a *different function* and passed against a hoisted call (scoped to the enclosing function now); two existing guards pinned to `publish: async (dist, pages)` went red on a correct third argument; and one harness assertion was vacuous, satisfied by `entry.js`'s own `SLOT` constant rather than by any shell.
- 3025 tests, `site build` **137/137**, **13/13 mutations as expected** across two sweeps from verified-green baselines — including one proved **INERT** and said so in the source rather than falsely asserted (removing the `env.SITES` guard makes `.get` throw on undefined, the catch takes it, and the answer is the same 200 on the baked shell). Twelfth recorded inert mutant.
- **THE DELETE FIX IS PROVEN LIVE TOO**, on the same run: `worker: removed`, followed by `the published files are actually gone`. That is the `success: false` read working against real Cloudflare — the shape that had a deleted site still answering 200 fifteen minutes later.
- **AND THE WHOLE TIER, END TO END, FOR THE FIRST TIME.** A script was uploaded, served the site, survived a revise, a version restore that really reverted the live page, and a free text edit, and was removed on delete. **38 credits cold** (13 schema · 25 pages), `renderMs 13192`, `buildMs 89467`.
- **STILL OPEN: one orphaned script.** `smoke-mswmfg71-6rm44.gofarther.app` was answering 200 with its ownership row gone. The sweeper is fixed and tested and the fixed Worker is now deployed, so it is one `workflow_dispatch` away — which this session cannot trigger (403).

## Dispatching in front of the serve path dropped three things it did (2026-08-17)

**Went looking for anything else of the "true when written, false once something moved" shape, and the serve path had three at once.** Putting each site behind its own Worker moved where a document is produced, and the static path was doing three jobs besides producing it. None of them moved. Every one is invisible to the script's own tests — the script is perfectly correct in isolation.

- **EVERY ASSET REFERENCE IN A PUBLISHED DOCUMENT IS RELATIVE, and the platform had always been fixing that.** Measured on a real container build: `./assets/index-BZW04i8N.js`, because the template sets vite `base: "./"` — and a browser resolves that against the DIRECTORY of the URL it was served at. Right at the mount root and wrong everywhere else: `/book/` asks for `/book/assets/…`, `/services/cuts` asks for `/services/assets/…`, and a 404 on the bundle is a **blank page**. The static path rewrote them to absolute; the script served its document verbatim and put it back. A trailing slash is not exotic — people type them and crawlers normalise both ways.
- **`robots.txt` AND `sitemap.xml` ARE PUBLISHED CARRYING A PLACEHOLDER ORIGIN**, on purpose, because the same bytes serve at three addresses and only the platform knows which one a request arrived on. The script's own asset branch would have served them straight off R2, so a customer's sitemap would list `https://__SITE_ORIGIN__/book` and every URL in it would be unusable to a crawler. They are simply not dispatched now.
- **A RENAMED OR DELETED PAGE STOPPED REDIRECTING.** The map rides in `index.html`'s head and `decideFallback` has read it since the SEO tier shipped; dispatching in front of it made every one of those a hard 404 — so an owner renaming a page broke every link already indexed and every share already sent, silently, on the feature built specifically to stop that. **The fix is a FALLTHROUGH rather than a second copy of the rule**: the script answers 404 for an address the site does not have, and the platform's existing code — which reads the shell and decides — runs exactly as it did. Extensionless only, the same restriction the SPA fallback lives under.
- **THE ALTERNATIVE WAS THE SCRIPT IMPORTING ALL THREE, AND IT WAS THE WRONG CALL.** `site-domains.mjs` and `site-seo.mjs` are pure, so a bundle can carry them — but the entry is staged into `<APP>/src/` before the vite build, which breaks every relative specifier, and those two modules live at the repo ROOT where the container image does not copy them. That is exactly the silent-COPY failure the Dockerfile already carries a warning about. **Keeping all three on the platform costs no duplication at all**, since the platform is the side that owns the mount and the manifest.
- **`mountRootFor` AND `absolutizeAssets` ARE IN `site-domains.mjs` NOW**, beside `isPublishedSiteRequest`, which was extracted for the same reason: it was the third place that had to answer "which mount is this", and this made it the fourth.
- **THE CONTAINER HARNESS CAUGHT MY OWN PROCESS GAP.** The R2-shell change was committed on a green unit suite and a green sweep, and `site build` had not been re-run — its fake bucket held only an asset, so with the shell coming from R2 **every page 404'd**. That is the change working, and it went in as a fixture that now holds what a published site holds: the REAL built documents, marked, so *"served from that route's OWN document"* is assertable at all. Two new assertions came free and both are the take-down safety net: a site whose files are gone answers 404 whatever the script still is, and an R2 **throw** keeps it up on the baked shell.
- **`test/site-worker-serve.test.mjs` DRIVES THE REAL ROUTER**, which is the instrument the worker harness was built for — a source-read cannot see any of this, and a source-read is what guarded this layer for the twelve features that shipped dead.
- **MY OWN FIXTURE SPELLED THE MANIFEST TAGS WRONG** (`isibi:routes` for `site-routes`), so it parsed to null and every redirect assertion failed against correct code. Built through the real `injectMeta` now — two fixtures agreeing with each other and with neither end, the `routeOf` lesson.
- **TWO EXISTING GUARDS WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE**, both pinned to the exact inline expression that moved into the shared helper. The word-order own-goal, again; both assert the answer through the real function now.
- **BOTH FIRST-SWEEP SURVIVORS WERE REAL AND BOTH FIXES NAMED A BETTER CASE.** The whitespace anchor is what stops `data-src` being rewritten — a real attribute holding every lazy-loaded image's true source, not a contrived one. And the extension gate's discriminating case is a script REFUSING an asset the bucket still has: without it the platform serves the file its own site just declined to, which is two sources of truth for what a site serves with the wrong one winning.
- 3038 tests, `site build` **142/142** against the real container, **10/10 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline.
- **PROVEN LIVE, ALL THREE, on run `31994268837` — 100 passed, 0 failed.** `worker: UPLOADED — this site is served by its own script`, then `the script serves the PUBLISHED document, with its share tags` · `…with its asset references made absolute` · `sitemap.xml carries a real origin, not the placeholder` and `…its URLs are on this site's own hostname`. **The run was made to prove them first**: every other check in that file passes on a site with all three broken — it builds, it publishes, it answers 200 — so a run that only re-confirmed the build would have said nothing about any of this.

## A site's own Worker is uploaded, refreshed and taken down (2026-08-17)

**The packaging and the forwarding both shipped and nothing connected them: the container could produce a script, the platform Worker could dispatch to one, and no path ever uploaded one.** So the whole tier was inert — a feature that reads as switched off, which is this file's most-recorded shape. This is the wiring, and it is one rule with two halves.

- **A PATH THAT COMPILES UPLOADS; A PATH THAT CHANGES R2 WITHOUT COMPILING TAKES THE SCRIPT DOWN.** The script is a SECOND copy of what a site serves and it bakes in the shell — which names that build's content-hashed assets — plus the route list. So it goes stale the instant the files move underneath it, and both halves of the rule exist to stop that.
- **THE HALF THAT IS EASY TO MISS IS THE CHEAP-EDIT SPINE.** `recompileAndPublish` is what every text fix, colour change, picture swap and rules change publishes through, and it does not go anywhere near `publishPages`. Wired to the build path alone, a typo fix would republish the files and leave the script serving a document naming the PREVIOUS build's chunks — which survive one publish on the sweep's grace period and are gone after the next. **The site would go blank one edit later**, with nothing connecting it to the edit that did it. It asks for `worker: true` and uploads too.
- **THE UPLOAD IS LAST, AFTER THE FILES, and that ordering is the whole safety argument.** The dispatch sits IN FRONT of the R2 read, so the moment a script exists it answers everything — including the asset requests it serves out of R2 itself. Uploaded first, a site renders its document and 404s every stylesheet and bundle underneath it for as long as the writes take. In this order a site keeps serving its last good build until the new script lands.
- **THREE PATHS TAKE IT DOWN, and each would otherwise be broken in its own way.** A **rollback** restores an older build's files, so a standing script serves a document naming assets about to be swept — blank, at a public address. The **offline switch** wipes the files, and the script renders the document from its own baked shell without ever reading R2 — so *"offline"* would carry on answering with a fully rendered page, which is the one outcome that route must not produce. And a **delete** leaves a script that is billed per script, whose only record was the ownership row just removed, holding the slug's script name against whoever claims it next.
  - **A ROLLBACK REFUSES IF THE REMOVAL FAILED**, rather than proceeding. Rolling back anyway is the blank page; refusing leaves the customer the site they were merely unhappy with. **`null` is not a failure** — it means no credentials are configured, which is every site until this deploys, and reading it as one would stop every rollback on the platform the day this shipped.
- **THE SCRIPT TRAVELS AS AN ARGUMENT, NOT A CLOSURE.** `deps.publish(built.files, pages, built.worker)` — read off `built`, which is REASSIGNED on the salvage path, so what is uploaded is packaged from the same source as the files beside it. A side channel would make *"the script never reached the upload"* and *"no script was made"* the same observation, which is the failure this repo has recorded twelve times.
- **ONE READER FOR THE CREDENTIALS.** Five call sites each deciding what "configured" means is five chances for one to disagree, and the shape of that disagreement is a path that quietly skips the upload while its neighbours do it. `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID` now reaches the Worker beside the token it already had — **REQUIRED and with no fallback**, safe to list because the deploy step itself cannot run without it, and dangerous to default because a placeholder account id sends every customer's script to a namespace nobody serves from.
- **EXISTING SITES ARE UNTOUCHED AND UPGRADE THEMSELVES.** Nothing is backfilled (owner's call): a site with no script is served from R2 exactly as today — the fall-through property, asserted three ways — and gets one the next time anything republishes it. So the switch-on is per site, on demand, with no migration.
- **`test/site-worker-wiring.test.mjs` IS DERIVED, not a list of today's call sites**, because a hand-written list is exactly what leaves the eighth path uncovered. The uploading half is found from `worker: true` (the only way a script can exist) and the removing half from the destructive R2 calls.
  - **ONE GUARD OF MINE HAD THE OVERLAPPING-WINDOW BUG, in the check written to hold an ordering.** It looked for the nearest `writeSiteDistToR2` ANYWHERE above the upload — and `recompileAndPublish` is defined earlier in the file, so hoisting the build path's upload above its OWN write still found that other function's one and passed. Scoped to the enclosing function now. **Found by mutation; it survived.**
  - **AND THE MUTANT THAT FOUND IT WAS INERT ON THE FIRST TRY**, moving the call a few lines earlier while still leaving it after the write — which reads in a sweep exactly like a test gap and costs a hunt through a check that is fine. Eleventh recorded instance.
- **TWO EXISTING GUARDS WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE**, both pinned to `publish: async (dist, pages)` and both failing the moment the dep grew a third argument — tests about word order, this repo's most repeated own-goal. Re-anchored on the property.
- 3002 tests, **13/13 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline (12 caught plus a comment-only control that had to survive). **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against it, so nothing has ever been uploaded; the first green signal is a script appearing in the namespace after a publish.

## THE ACCOUNT WAS TOPPED UP AND `build smoke` WENT 99/0 — the first fully green run (2026-08-15)

**Read this before the "the account is empty" bullets below it, all of which are now historical.** Run `31864141084` on `bc6f7be`, 9m35s, **99 passed 0 failed** — the whole path proved live for the first time since the render check, the prerender sandbox, the publish sweep and the seven edit layers shipped.

- **What it proved, end to end:** a barber shop designed from one sentence → Neon project → 6 seeded rows → 4 generated pages compiled and bundled → prerendered → published → 301 to `smoke-mstvb8km-dbrdg.gofarther.app` → **a real Chromium mounted React with no console errors from the site's own code** → the booking form submitted and Postgres answered **201** → the owner read that exact row back → a revise changed colour and radius and the site was **never half-published** → version archive, restore, and the live site really reverted → a free text edit republished at zero cost → delete, 34 objects gone.
- **`bookings_public` was called by the live page.** `publicView` — the taken-slots hint, unserved since the Neon migration — is working on a real site.
- **THE COST, MEASURED ON A COLD CACHE: schema 12 credits (in 236 · out 1103 · cacheWrite 19008), pages 26 (in 5192 · out 9830 · cacheWrite 53228), total 38.** The account held 38 going in (40 funded, minus 2 for routing), so the build spent essentially all of it. **No photographs**: 19 credits was not left for one and `imagesAffordable` correctly returned 0, so the fal balance is still untouched and the buying half is still unproven.
- **THE RENDER CHECK RAN LIVE FOR THE FIRST TIME AND CAME BACK CLEAN** — `renderMs 30913`, nothing found on either width. Its false-alarm rate on real generator output is now 0 for 1, which is the measurement it was built to need before it can ever be allowed to gate anything.
- **A REAL BUG ON EVERY PUBLISHED SITE, found by the browser step and not by any assertion.** *"Cloudflare injects its analytics beacon on this zone and the site's CSP refuses it — a console error on every page view, and no analytics either."* Every customer site on `gofarther.app`. Two fixes and they are not equivalent: turning off Web Analytics auto-injection for the zone removes the error and collects nothing, while allowing `static.cloudflareinsights.com` in the site CSP removes the error and starts collecting visitor analytics on **customers' sites into our account** — which is a decision about their data, not a config tweak. **Owner's call, and not yet made.**
- **`og 165294ms` WAS A NUMBER THAT READ AS THE WRONG THING, and it was mine.** Moving the `og` resolution inside the publish closure (2026-08-14, correct — it has to happen after photographs are bought) moved its trace mark with it, and that closure runs INSIDE `publishPages`, so the mark became the first one since `fonts` and its delta swallowed generate + typecheck + vite + prerender + render. The trace reported one R2 list call as **77% of a 214s build**. Anyone triaging a slow build off that optimises the wrong thing. A `container` mark now closes that span first; guarded on the ORDER inside the closure, because a `container` mark landing after the lookup fixes nothing and would satisfy a check that only matched the string.
- **`WARNING: could not remove Neon project` WAS NOISE THAT READ AS A LEAK.** The delete route drops the project inline, so the run's own cleanup gets a 404 — and `dropUserProject` throws on any non-ok. A Neon project is a capped billed resource whose only record is a Supabase row, so a warning saying one leaked is one somebody has to go and check. `site-teardown.mjs` settled this exact question long ago (**404 is DONE, 401/403 is NEVER done**) and this was the one cleanup that had not learned it. Read off `e.status`, never the message — a substring match for "404" also hits a project id.
- **The generator's own `notes` flagged a real gap honestly:** it built `/manage` as an explainer rather than a live lookup *"because there's no useClaimedRow/useRpc function declared for it"*. Correct, and worth acting on — a booking site whose confirmation is the end of the flow is thinner than it should be.

## Every published site logged a console error, and the setting to stop it does not exist (2026-08-15)

**Found by `build smoke`'s browser step on the first green run, and the diagnosis was wrong twice before it was right.** Cloudflare injects its own analytics beacon on the `gofarther.app` zone and the published-site CSP refused it — a console error on **every page view of every customer site**.

- **THE FIRST TWO ANSWERS WERE ADVICE THAT COULD NOT WORK**, which is the `1404` failure this file already records (*"told the reader to add SSL and Certificates: Read — advice that cannot work"*). The warning said *"turn off Web Analytics auto-injection for gofarther.app"*, and the account's Web Analytics list holds **one site, `eventvendora.com`** — no gofarther zone at all. Then Speed → Optimization, also wrong. The zone's own Web Analytics page reads **"No data available — Enable RUM for free"**, i.e. it is offering to turn RUM **ON**. Nothing is configured, so there is nothing to un-configure.
- **MEASURED ON A REAL CUSTOMER SITE, not on the smoke fixture.** `forno-and-co.gofarther.app` serves `<script src="https://static.cloudflareinsights.com/beacon.min.js/…" data-cf-beacon='{"token":"16ed…"}'>` and `gofarther.dev` serves none — which is what proves it is a property of the `.app` zone rather than of us. **A default `curl` sees nothing**: the beacon is injected only for browser-like requests, the trap this file already records for exactly this host.
- **SO IT IS NOT A DECISION ABOUT A CUSTOMER'S DATA, and the earlier framing of it as one was wrong.** With RUM off nothing is collected whichever way the CSP reads; the only difference is whether the browser complains about a script Cloudflare inserts regardless. If RUM is ever enabled deliberately, THAT is the moment to weigh it — and this line will already be right.
- **BOTH DIRECTIVES OR NEITHER.** The beacon loads from `static.cloudflareinsights.com` and POSTs its measurements to `cloudflareinsights.com` — a **different host**, no `static.` — so allowing only the script moves the error rather than removing it: the load succeeds and the send is refused. Same class as the `frame-src` fix one line below it, where naming one of two hosts would have left `VideoEmbed` rendering nothing. Asserted per DIRECTIVE, because one regex over the block passes when both names land on the same line.
- **THE WARNING BECAME AN ASSERTION, and the two claims inside it were both false.** *"No change to this repo can stop it"* (the CSP stops it) and *"the switch is a Cloudflare zone setting"* (there is no such switch). While it really was unfixable a red run would have trained everybody to ignore `build smoke`; now a violation means either the allow-list regressed or Cloudflare moved the beacon, and both put the error back on every published site. Kept SEPARATE from the site's own console errors either way, so a real page error can never hide behind a known one.
- 2865 tests, **4/4 mutations as expected** (3 caught plus a no-op control). **Verified live as an injection and a refused CSP; the fix itself is proven only on the next `build smoke`**, where the assertion above is what reports it.

## THE DESIGNER STOPPED SEEDING THE CAFÉ, and the report could not say why (2026-08-15)

**`schema gen eval` ran funded for the first time in two days and came back 17/20, down from 19/20 — and all three failures are ONE shape.** The café brief (`menu`) failed on samples 1, 3 and 4 with `unseeded: menu_items,opening_hours`. The same signature appeared once on 2026-08-13. **One failure is variance; a column of the same error is a mismatch**, which is the eval's own rule, and this is now a column.

- **THE CORRELATION IS EXACT ACROSS BOTH RUNS.** Every failing sample declared **TWO** display tables (`menu_items` + `opening_hours`) and seeded **NEITHER**; every passing sample declared **ONE** (`menu_items`) and seeded it. 4 for 4 and 6 for 6. That is not "forgot the second table" — it is "produced no seed at all", and the two have different fixes.
- **NOT TRUNCATION, measured rather than assumed.** Output is **1,576 tokens a sample** against `SITE_SCHEMA_MAX_TOKENS` 8,000. The obvious hypothesis — two display tables plus a week of opening hours overran the budget — is wrong by a factor of five.
- **AND THE REPORT COULD NOT NAME THE CAUSE, which is why nothing was reworded.** `unseeded: a,b` is true of five different answers: no `seed` key, `seed: null`, a list, `seed: {}`, and — the one the tool description does not name — **a `seed` full of real rows under keys that are not table names**. That last one is a completely different bug and reads in the report as identical to not answering. `CHECKS.seeded` now prints the shape, and prints the keys it DID use whenever there are any.
- **THE RULE THIS FOLLOWS IS THIS FILE'S OWN, and it was written after paying for the alternative.** *"FIVE PROMPT REWRITES WERE SPENT ON A SYMPTOM… before rewording a prompt because a field came back empty, check that the field can arrive."* The `seed` description is already 1,100 characters of increasingly emphatic instruction ending in **COUNT YOUR OWN TABLES BEFORE YOU FINISH**; a sixth paragraph written against a guess is the exact move that history warns about. Instrument, run once (~0.67 credits), then fix what the run names.
- **PRODUCTION IS COVERED AND THE COST IS REAL.** `topUpSeed` catches this at build time, so a real café's menu does get filled — measured on the same day's green `build smoke`, where a booking site needed no top-up at all. What it costs is an extra Haiku call on roughly **60% of menu-shaped builds**, for a field the designer is required to answer.
- **The other three scenarios are 15/15**, including the two that exist because of live incidents: every booking sample carried a slot constraint, and every marketplace sample came back browsable — three of the five through `publicView` on an own-scoped table, which is the 2026-08-10 fix working rather than luck.
- 2864 tests. **The next eval run answers the question**; this change is in that workflow's own path filter, so committing it is what fires it.

### …AND THE NEXT RUN REFUTED THE HYPOTHESIS, WHICH IS WHY IT WAS INSTRUMENTED

**One run, 0.67 credits, and the two-display-tables correlation is dead.** The shape is **"no `seed` key at all"** in all four failures — not `{}`, not empty arrays, not wrong keys. And three of the four have ONE display table (`booking` now fails too, on `services`), so the 4-for-4 / 6-for-6 correlation across the first two runs was coincidence. **A prompt rewritten around "when you declare two display tables…" would have targeted a phantom** — which is precisely what the rule exists to prevent.

- **`strict: true` IS THE OBVIOUS FIX AND THE API FORBIDS IT HERE.** Tool `required` is advisory unless strict validation is on, so strict is exactly the mechanism — and structured outputs require `additionalProperties: false` on every object and reject it set to anything else. `seed` is `additionalProperties: {type:"array",…}`, a map keyed by TABLE NAME, which cannot be enumerated in advance because the tables are what that very call is inventing. **Shipping `strict: true` would 400 every build on the platform.** Checked against the API contract before writing a line, after two rounds of Cloudflare advice-that-cannot-work in the same session.
- **RESHAPING `seed` TO SUIT STRICT COSTS MORE THAN THE MISS.** Rows are column→value objects, so a strict encoding needs a fixed envelope (`{column, value}` pairs) — ~3-4× the tokens on a field already ~41% of the designer's output, on EVERY build, against a miss `topUpSeed` catches with one Haiku call on a minority of them. An order of magnitude the wrong way.
- **THE MEASURED SHAPE IS ALREADY HANDLED, verified in the code rather than assumed.** `seedGaps`' first line turns an absent `seed` into `{}`, so every display table reads as a gap and the net fills them all; `null` and `{}` are both pinned in `test/site-seed.test.mjs`.
- **SO THE GUARD FORBIDS THE COMBINATION, NOT THE IMPROVEMENT.** `test/schema-eval-scope.test.mjs` walks the real tool for any non-`false` `additionalProperties` and fails only if `strict` is set while one exists — green with strict on the day `seed` becomes expressible. Its floor is asserted first, because a broken walk reports a strict-ready schema and passes on a Worker one edit from 400-ing every build.
- **AND THE TREND I REPORTED WAS OVERSTATED.** `SITE_SCHEMA_TOOL` is **byte-identical** (56,096 chars) between the 19/20 run and both 2026-08-15 runs — measured, not recalled — so nothing we changed caused it. 14/15 → 12/15 → 10/14 on n=15 with an unchanged prompt is within noise; calling it a consistent direction was reading a trend into three small samples. **The eval cannot tell variance from a mismatch at 5 samples a scenario**, which is its own stated job — raising `EVAL_SAMPLES` is the lever, at ~0.7 credits a run.

  - **BOTH GUARDS BUILT FOR THIS PAID OFF, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS.** `edit smoke` **stopped after 1 second** — *"⛔ THE MODEL ACCOUNT IS OUT OF CREDIT — stopping here… every lane would fail for this one reason"* — 2/2 rather than the 17/18 the same condition produced before the bail existed, and it **kept** the fixture account rather than tearing down on a run that proved nothing. And the eval **refused to record a compile rate**: *"THE RUN NEVER REACHED THE MODEL… PAGE-GEN.md is left as it was: a run that could not ask is not a compile rate of zero."* An empty account writing `0%` into the series would have been a permanent lie in the one file that tracks generator quality.
  - **EVERY FREE CHECK IS GREEN** — `unit tests`, `site build`, `member smoke`, `payments smoke`, `confirm smoke`, `frame policy`, and the deploy itself. `build smoke`'s 6/16 is the documented shape: the two routing failures at the top are `readRouting`'s "unclear resolves to build" firing because the routing call 503'd too, and `caller was charged for the build → {"balance":20}` is the ledger correctly untouched.

Cost: **1 second and nothing charged** (the refusal is before provisioning, the deposit refunds, and the `billing === true` break correctly did not fire a second attempt).
  - **THE BAIL AND THE ATTEMPT LINE ARE WHAT MADE THAT ONE LOOK.** The identical condition produced 17/18 two days ago and 2/2 here, and the reason arrived on the attempt line rather than in a dig through Cloudflare. Both were built in the previous two sittings; this is the first run either has paid off on.
  - **IT DOES NOT EXPLAIN THE 07:46Z FAILURE TWENTY MINUTES EARLIER, WHICH IS A DIFFERENT ONE.** That had `upstream: null`, `upstreamType: null` and NO `billing` — an empty account answers with a 400 body and would have set all three. So a non-HTTP throw remains unexplained; `kind` will name it if it recurs (`TypeError` is the fetch, anything else is ours).
  - **NEITHER FIXTURE EXISTS** (`esmoke-fixture` and `-r1` both `rows=404/0`), which is expected rather than new: the 42/1 run built a site and deleted it at the end — the teardown gating fixed in `7e58eec`. The first funded green run leaves one behind and every run after it skips the build.
- **EMPTY AGAIN AT 05:52Z, ~2 HOURS AFTER A TOP-UP, AND THE DRAIN IS NOW SELF-INFLICTED.** `edit smoke` reported **17 passed, 18 failed** and every one of the 18 descends from ONE line: `503 stage:"design" upstream:400 upstreamType:"invalid_request_error" billing:true` on the addon. The routing call 503'd too, so `a rule change routes to the RULES layer` reported `intent=addon layer=undefined` — `readRouting`'s documented fallback working, not a routing bug — and `the rule change succeeds` answered `422 error:"send"`, which is the model call failing. **`THE DATABASE REALLY REFUSES A STRANGER NOW` failed for the same reason and is NOT a security regression**: the rule was never applied, so the table was still readable, which is correct. `cost: 0` throughout and the deposit refunds.
- **THE CAUSE THIS TIME WAS THE ITERATION LOOP ITSELF.** Six `edit smoke` cycles in one sitting, each a real first build plus a real addon — two full page-generation calls a run, on top of `build smoke` and `page gen eval`. **Debugging a live check by re-running it is a spend, and at this cadence it empties the account faster than a day of customers would.** The lesson is to batch fixes into one run rather than merging each one and watching, and to read a red run's FIRST model failure before reading any of the others.

- **STILL EMPTY 2026-08-12 03:23Z, and this is what the pattern looks like from the OTHER side.** The 2026-08-12 deploy of `a36a652` was green — Worker deployed in 3m43s, `unit tests`, `member smoke`, `payments smoke`, `confirm smoke` and `frame policy` all passing — and `build smoke` reported **6 passed, 16 failed** while `edit smoke` reported 2/2. **Every one of the 18 failures descends from ONE 503** at `stage:"design"` with `billing:true`, in about a second, before anything is provisioned or spent. **The two that read like a router bug are the router working**: `a brief that names no trade is asked about it` failed with `{"ok":true,"intent":"build","cost":0}`, which is `readRouting`'s documented "every unclear case resolves to build" firing because the routing call itself 503'd — and `cost: 0` proves nothing was billed. **So a red `build smoke` on an empty account looks exactly like a broad regression and is one line of cause.** Read `upstreamType`/`billing` on the FIRST failure before reading any of the others; `caller was charged for the build → {"balance":20,"paid":false}` in the same log is the ledger correctly untouched, not a billing bug.
- **AND AGAIN — measured 2026-08-11 22:19Z, and the drain is now a PATTERN rather than an incident.** Third time in three days, and the third time it happened INSIDE TWENTY MINUTES of a working run: `edit smoke` was 29/1 at 22:10Z and at 22:19Z the first model call answered the same `503 stage:"design" upstream:400 upstreamType:"invalid_request_error" billing:true`, twice, in 2s and 1s. **A top-up that morning does not mean the account is funded that evening** — a build is ~27-93 credits of real spend and CI drives several a night, so the honest read is that this account is being run at a level where it empties in hours, not days. **The cost, stated plainly: while it is empty NO customer can build a site on gofarther.dev** — that is a production outage wearing the same shape as a test failure, which is why the shape is worth knowing cold. Nothing is consumed by the refusal and the deposit refunds.
- **THE ANTHROPIC ACCOUNT RAN OUT — measured 2026-08-09 21:49Z**, and the bullet below it demonstrated its own lesson for the second time by claiming the account was funded while every build on the platform was refusing. It drained INSIDE TWELVE MINUTES of a working run: `build smoke` was 82/83 at 21:37Z, and at 21:49Z the first model call answered `503 stage:"design" upstream:400 upstreamType:"invalid_request_error" billing:true`. **`billing: true` is the fact, not an inference** — `upstreamKind()` sets it only when the provider's message matches *"credit balance is too low"*, so this is the account and not the code, and it was confirmed by the commit at HEAD not touching `worker.js` at all. **The ledger did the right thing**: balance untouched at 20, deposit refunded, nobody charged for the refusal. **Read the DATE on this line before believing it** — it is true until somebody tops the account up, and the whole point of the bullet below is that nothing announces when that happens. The one durable reading: that exact 503 shape is the FIRST model call refusing and nothing is consumed by it.
- **THE ANTHROPIC ACCOUNT WAS TOPPED UP 2026-08-08 — this bullet used to say it had none, and reading it now would send you the wrong way.** Builds run live: `build smoke` was green 57/57 on `f1c9a3b` that morning and `page gen eval` runs again. **So a red `build smoke` IS a regression now**, which is the opposite of what this said — it told the reader to dismiss exactly the signal that matters, and it did so while a real failure (a generated page using `require()`, which typechecks, bundles, publishes and then throws in the browser) sat red at HEAD. That one is fixed and linted. Kept as a record because the shape is the lesson: a status bullet that is true for a day and stays for a week is worse than no bullet. What the earlier state proved and still holds: a 503 at `stage:"design"` with `upstream:400` / `upstreamType:"invalid_request_error"` / `billing:true` is the FIRST model call refusing, nothing is consumed by it, and `upstreamKind()` naming it in the response body is why that took one look rather than a dig through Cloudflare logs. **The fal balance is still empty**, so no photograph has ever been generated.

## `build smoke` runs as a real account, and the teardown deletes users (2026-08-15, owner's call)

**"Use my account, don't raise the credit thing."** A new account cannot finish a cold first build — `buildFloor(sonnet)` reached 20 against a 20-credit grant and the routing call spends 1 before the gate — and the owner's answer is NOT to raise the free grant but to run the smoke test as an account that brings its own credits. The whole difficulty is that this points a run whose `finally` block ends with `DELETE /auth/v1/admin/users/<id>` at somebody's actual account.

- **THAT CALL CASCADES AND HAS NO UNDO**: chats, credits, purchases, storage rows, every `user_site_project` record. And the failure is silent in the worst way — the run passes, reports green, and the account is gone. So the gate is a PROPERTY OF THE SOURCE (`test/build-smoke-safety.test.mjs`, 9 checks) rather than a rule somebody has to remember while editing a 1,400-line script.
- **`createdUser` IS THE GATE AND IT CANNOT COME FROM CONFIGURATION.** It is set in exactly one place, from the id the admin create returned, so "did this run make this user" can only be answered by having made them. The obvious alternative — gating on `useSupplied` — answers *"was a real account configured"*, which is a different question, and a mis-set secret then deletes the account it was pointed at. Asserted on the assignment's RIGHT-HAND SIDE, and it starts `false`, because a gate that defaults open is not a gate.
- **THE `user_site_project` WIPE IS ON THE SAME GATE, and not out of caution.** That table is the only record of an account's Neon projects, and deleting the row does not delete the projects — it makes every one of them invisible and billing forever, which is the exact leak `site-teardown.mjs` exists to stop, arriving through a test. This run's own project is dropped by slug, which is what makes leaving the table alone safe.
- **THE SECOND LEDGER WRITE WAS THE DANGEROUS ONE AND ONLY LOOKING FOUND IT.** The first top-up was already guarded; the revise's `balance: 60` was not, and **both SET the balance rather than adding to it** — so against a real account holding 500 credits that write is a deduction of 440. One `fundAccount` now, refusing before the fetch and returning: two copies of a rule drift, and the direction this one drifts in is a customer's ledger. The safety test asserts the ledger is written in **exactly one place**.
- **THE CHARGE ASSERTION WOULD HAVE GONE VACUOUS, and where the reading is taken IS the assertion.** It compared against a constant this run chose (first 20, then the funding amount), which says nothing about an account whose balance is the owner's business. A before/after reading fixes that — but taken at sign-in it falls whether or not the build charges anything, because the two routing calls above cost a credit each. Read immediately before the build POST, the only thing between the two readings is the build. Asserted BOTH ways: that it compares against the reading, and that the reading sits after the routing calls.
- **THE SCAN IS DERIVED AND ITS FLOOR IS ASSERTED.** Every Supabase URL interpolating `${userId}` must be inside the gate's block — the two that exist today and any third written tomorrow — and a separate check requires the scan to still find both known shapes, because a regex that stops matching reports a clean file, which is the reassuring way to say nothing was checked. "After the gate" is not "inside it": a mutation moving a call one line past the closing brace dies on the block-still-open check.
- **MY OWN WORKFLOW GUARD FALSE-ALARMED ON CORRECT CODE FIRST.** Derived from every `SMOKE_*` the script reads, it flagged `SMOKE_SKIP_JOURNEY` — a deliberately local escape hatch with a documented default that must NOT be in CI. Narrowed to the `SUPPLIED_*` constants, which is exactly the set that decides `useSupplied`. Same rule this file keeps recording: a check that cries wolf on correct code is worse than the miss.
- **THE COST, STATED: `build smoke` NO LONGER NOTICES THAT A NEW CUSTOMER CANNOT BUILD COLD.** That failure did not go away; it stopped being CI's job to report it. `test/build-models.test.mjs` pins the shortfall (grant 20, one routing call, floor 20), so the day the grant moves that test goes red and says what to update.
- **AND IT DOES NOT MAKE CI GREEN.** Go Farther credits do not pay Anthropic. The latest run on `7aa3928` failed at `stage=design` with the balance untouched at 40 — the build never reached the model. This fixes the floor; the Anthropic balance is a separate top-up.
- 2863 tests, **11/11 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline (10 caught plus a no-op control that had to survive). **Not proven live** — the model account is empty, so no run has used the supplied path.

## The last ten missed items, verified — three refuted (2026-08-15)

**Round 22.** The `missed` list's remaining ten, checked against the live code rather than believed off their line numbers. **Six confirmed, three refuted, one confirmed as a scope limit and deliberately left.** Recorded in full because a finding that says a feature is broken is worth ten minutes of driving before it is repeated — and because three of these were already closed by earlier rounds, which is exactly what an un-triaged list looks like after two weeks of work.

- **REFUTED — the two provisioning races (`saveRoute`, `saveAuthInfo`/`saveDataInfo`).** Both were extensions of the slug race, and the claim + CONVERGENCE fix closed the class: a racer that loses at the project destroys its own, reads back the winner's, and continues, so `lookupProject(slug)` returning the winner's project IS the convergence rather than a leak. Using the local `proj` — which the finding asked for — would now be the bug. `saveRoute` writes the converged connection, so last-writer-wins is writing the same value twice.
- **REFUTED — the stale quality evidence.** That one is about the AUDIT's own citation, not the codebase: `SCHEMA-GEN.md` on disk reports 19/20 and the auditor cited something else.
- **CONFIRMED AS A LIMIT AND LEFT — the dangling-link guard cannot see a `$` route.** Measured: `to="/deals/$id"` matches nothing, so the rewrite never fires on a dynamic link. What the miss costs is a typecheck failure instead of a silent repair, and widening the pattern risks rewriting a CORRECT dynamic link on the one shape the corpus cannot calibrate against — **0 of the 318 exemplars declare a dynamic route**, so the model has never been shown one. A rule wrong on a third of its hits is worse than one that is merely narrow.
- **CONFIRMED — `schemaDigest` had the LAST TWO raw-access reads, and one of them was consequential.** `if (t.teamScope && access === "user")` read the STAMPED preset, so a team table declared as a pair (`{read:"own", write:"own"}`) arrived stamped `collect`, **the SHARED WITH THE TEAM line was never printed**, and the generator wrote *"your notes"* on a table the whole team reads and edits. The line below it had the same read and made one fact conditional on spelling: a pair-declared display table got a `usePublicRows` line its preset-declared twin does not. **The fix is that the stamped name is no longer BOUND in that scope at all** — a variable holding the preset a table never declared, in the one function that describes tables to the model, is a trap that has been fallen into once per reader; six instances across this file before these two. With nothing to reach for the mistake cannot be made here again.
- **CONFIRMED — three stale claims, each guarding something.** The clarify gate said a gated `clarify` is *"simply overruled into a build"* and `FALLBACK_WITH_SITE` is **`addon`**, so on an existing site — which is every revise, i.e. exactly where the gate is closed — it is not a build. The seeding note said *"no owner-write route exists"* and `handleOwnerWrite` exists, its own docstring saying it closes that exact gap (the seeding CONCLUSION is untouched and still right — a café should not have to type its whole menu into a data panel before the site is worth showing anybody). And `build smoke` promised *"a revise buys none, so funding this one cannot start that"*, which stopped being true when `budgetFor` replaced the flat `revise ? 0` rule: a revise on a site showing NO photograph buys them, which is precisely a smoke site whose first build could not afford any. **That last one guards a real fal bill** — 60 credits is up to three images at ~19 each — and is latent only while the balance is empty.
- **CONFIRMED, ALREADY A DECISION, AND WORSE THAN THE FINDING SAID — a new account cannot build.** `buildFloor(sonnet)` is **20**, the grant is **20**, and the routing call spends 1 before the gate, so a first build is refused by one credit with **zero** questions answered. The finding's own point adds to it: each answered clarify round is another routing call, so 3 questions leave 16 against a floor of 20 — **engaging with the builder's own questions moves a customer further from being able to build.** Already recorded in `build smoke` as an owner's call (fund the test, do not hide the shortfall) and pinned by `test/build-models.test.mjs`; what was not stated anywhere is the clarify multiplier, which means the grant has to cover floor + 1 + MAX_CLARIFY, not floor + 1.
- **CONFIRMED AND ALREADY MITIGATED — `FREE_GRANT = 20` is hand-copied.** The real grant lives in the Supabase RPC outside this repo, so any in-repo statement is a restatement; the test names it and says exactly that in a comment. No action worth the churn, recorded so the next reader does not re-derive it.
- **A guard was pinned to `renderReport(seen, { cut })`** and went red on a correct change the moment a second honest field joined it — the word-order own-goal, third time this session. Re-anchored on the property.
- **AND MY OWN NEW GUARD HAD THE OVERLAPPING-WINDOW BUG, in the test written to hold the fix for the thing it missed.** It sliced from `const rw = resolveAccess(t)`, so a mutant re-binding `const access` on the line ABOVE sat outside the window and survived. Anchored at the top of the callback. This repo's other recurring own-goal, and the second time in two rounds.
- 2854 tests, **7/7 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline after that survivor was closed and re-proven.

## The audit's own MISSED list, seven of them verified and fixed (2026-08-15)

**Round 21.** The 30-step audit produced 81 confirmed findings and a separate `missed` list of 22 secondary ones the auditors recorded and nobody promoted. Eight were checked against the live code; one had been fixed incidentally and **seven were real**. The theme is not the confirmed findings' — every one of these is a check or a rule that LOOKS right and answers the wrong question: SQL in the wrong dialect, an entity class nobody matched, a preset name standing in for a resolved pair, a missing branch, a missing ORDER BY, a stub that does not cover what the pages reference. None of them errors, which is why they survived an audit's worth of reading and needed measuring.

- **`DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS <name>` WITH NO `ON <table>` — the re-apply hygiene loop has been dead since the Postgres migration.** That form is SQLite's; Postgres requires the table, so every statement was a syntax error swallowed by a bare `catch {}`. `pgTrigger` twenty lines up gets it right, which is why creating a trigger works and only REMOVING one was broken — and what that costs is exactly what the loop's own comment describes: turning `audit` or `history` off on a revise left its AFTER trigger standing, firing into a side table on every subsequent write. Asserted over **every** drop in the file, since a second one written tomorrow would fail just as quietly.
- **A HEX CHARACTER REFERENCE SURVIVED INTO EVERY SHARE PREVIEW.** `pageMeta`'s stripper was `/&[a-z]+;|&#\d+;/gi` — no hex — and **`&#x27;` is exactly what the prerenderer emits for an apostrophe**: measured against this template's own React, `renderToString` turns *"Mo's Cuts & the barber's chair"* into `Mo&#x27;s Cuts &amp; the barber&#x27;s chair`. So any business with a possessive in its name shipped a WhatsApp card reading `Mo&#x27;s Cuts`. **The ones it DID match were mangled differently** — replaced by a SPACE, so `Mo&#39;s Cuts` read as *"Mo s Cuts"*. Both fixed by DECODING rather than stripping, which is the correct layering and is safe by construction: every value goes out through `esc`, so a `<` or `&` recovered here is re-escaped at the boundary. An out-of-range numeric reference is left as written rather than throwing.
- **`acceptsVisitorUploads` ASKED THE STAMPED PRESET — the FOURTH instance of the pair bug**, after the lint's write gate, the digest's PAID line and the response's `schema` field. `normalizeSchema` stamps `access: "collect"` on any table that did not name a preset, and the design tool tells the model to leave `access` out when it declares a pair — so this read a name nobody wrote, and was wrong in **both** directions: a pair-declared table with no writes at all accepted files, and a `{read:"public", write:"own"}` members' gallery — the exact shape the feature exists for — was refused. `site-access.mjs` answers it now; the two named predicates between them are "write is not `none`", which is the set the preset list was reaching for.
- **THE `useRow` LINT HAD NO `if (!t)` BRANCH** while `useRows` and `usePublicRows` both do, so `useRow("ghost", id)` against a table the schema never declared was the one table-name typo the lint stayed silent about — the same compiles-then-404s class it catches everywhere else. All three read hooks are now asserted together, because the gap existed precisely because the three loops were written separately.
- **THE JOBS DRAIN COULD STARVE A JOB FOREVER, and each tick reported a healthy run.** The registry read was `limit=200` with **no `order`**, and `dueJobs` filtered and then took the first 25 **in read order** — so the same 25 won every tick and everything behind them never fired. Sorted stalest-first now, at both ends: the query fetches the 200 most in need of running and the module sorts the same way, so the read and the selection agree rather than fight. **The rotation falls out on its own** — a job that runs has its stamp refreshed and goes to the back — and a never-run job (a site just built) starts at the front. Held in the MODULE and not left to the caller's query string, because a property the caller happens to supply is one a later edit drops silently.
- **THE RENDER CHECK 404'd EVERY UPLOAD, so it reported a fault on correct sites.** `/u/…` carries an extension, so it fell through to the asset branch and out of the dist — a console error and a broken image on every picture-led site **and every site with a logo**, in a verdict that goes to the CUSTOMER. A false alarm, which this repo rates strictly worse than the miss. It serves **a real one-pixel image rather than a 204**, because the Round 18 contrast pass asks whether text sits over a picture and a body-less response leaves nothing for it to see.
- **THE BROWSER RUNS THE MODEL'S CLIENT BUNDLE UNSANDBOXED, and the first fix made that WORSE.** Round 8 sandboxed the SSR prerender; this is the second execution surface and it launched with `--no-sandbox`. The first draft "tried" a sandboxed launch and fell back — and **Playwright adds `--no-sandbox` ITSELF when the parent process is root**, so the attempt always succeeded and the browser was unsandboxed anyway. Measured against a real browser: launch with no such flag in the arguments, then read the spawned process's real argv, and the flag is there. **That draft would have shipped an unsandboxed browser wearing `sandboxed: true` — strictly worse than the honest flag it replaced.** The verdict is now derived from the condition that decides it (running as root) and reported only when FALSE, the `prerenderUnprivileged` rule. It flips to true on its own the day the build service runs as a non-root user, which is the real fix and is a Dockerfile change.
  - **The measurement that caught it needed two attempts** — the first `ps` pattern matched this very node process and reported the flag present for the wrong reason. A harness that cannot fail honestly is worse than none, one layer up from the thing being measured.
  - **AND THE SWEEP FOUND THE SAME DEFECT ONE MORE TIME IN MY OWN FIX**: hardcoding `sandboxed: true` in the return survived everything, because the verdict function was still correct and still called and its answer discarded. The guard now drives `launchChromium` with a fake `chromium` and requires the flag and the ARGV to agree — a report saying "sandboxed" while `--no-sandbox` went to the browser is the exact false reassurance being fixed.
- **A guard was pinned to `renderReport(seen, { cut })`** and went red on a correct change the moment a second honest field joined it — a test about word order, this repo's most repeated own-goal, third time this session. Re-anchored on the property: every report return must carry the flag.
- 2848 tests, `site build` 120/120 against the real container, contrast-cases 16/16, **22/22 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline (21 caught plus a comment-only control that had to survive), after the one survivor above was closed and re-proven. **Not proven live** — the model account is empty, so none of this has run on a customer build.

## A republish stopped breaking the people already on the site (2026-08-15)

**Round 20, and the last open audit finding — deferred in Round 9 and built now, because the reason for deferring turned out to be half wrong.** Write-then-sweep fixed the click-through 404 for a visitor ARRIVING during a republish and not for one already there: a generated site is code-split one chunk per route with content-hashed names, so somebody with the page open still holds the OLD document, and clicking to a route they have not visited asks for a chunk the sweep had just deleted. **On every republish — every revise, text fix, colour change and picture swap.** The publish docstring's *"atomic from a visitor's side"* was true of the arriving visitor and of nobody already there.

- **THE DEFERRAL SAID `build smoke` WAS THE ONLY THING THAT COULD CATCH A MISTAKE HERE, AND THAT WAS THE WRONG INSTRUMENT.** The decisions are about which KEYS survive — no container, no model call, no real R2 — so `site-sweep.mjs` takes its store injected the way `publish-pages.mjs` takes its side effects, and all twenty mutations run against a fake in about a second. The first half of the reasoning stands (it is the publish path of every live site, and it earned a module rather than a patch); the second half named a check that was never the right one.
- **ONE PUBLISH OF GRACE, kept in a marker OUTSIDE the served prefix.** This publish deletes what the LAST one marked and marks what this one orphaned, so a chunk outlives the build that replaced it by one publish. `orphans/<slug>.json`, for the reason the page source lives under `source/` and the backups under `backups/`: `sites/<slug>/` is what the public is served, and a list of a customer's file names is not something to put there.
- **SELF-HEALING, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT SAFE TO DEPLOY AT ALL.** The deferred list is recomputed from a LIVE listing every time and never carried forward from the marker — so a marker that could not be read, or was never written, costs one round of cleanup and nothing else, because an orphan is still an orphan next publish. The failure mode is delayed cleanup, never a leak and never a loss. **An unreadable LISTING therefore marks nothing rather than marking an empty list**, which is the one way a lost round could have become permanent.
- **THE ONE DANGEROUS FAILURE IS DELETING SOMETHING LIVE, and it has exactly one guard: a key this publish just wrote is never removed, whatever the marker says.** Not defensive — **`rollbackVersion` republishes an OLD build**, so keys the previous publish marked as orphaned are live again by design. Without that rule the marker deletes the site it has just restored, on the one action somebody takes when their site is already visibly wrong.
- **A ROLLBACK IS A PUBLISH AND GETS THE SAME GRACE.** It writes a full dist and orphans whatever it replaced, so sweeping immediately there reopens the exact 404 this closes. Injected (`deps.sweep`) rather than imported, so `site-versions.mjs` stays driveable with nothing but a fake store — and OPTIONAL, which is what keeps its old path honest rather than dead. **Asserted through the module, not by reading the Worker**: an injected dep that nothing calls is the precise shape of the twelve dead features this repo has recorded.
- **THE MARKER IS CAPPED, AND THE ASSETS ARE WHAT IT KEEPS.** A marker growing without bound is a worse failure than one rare in-session 404, and the in-session case IS a code-split chunk — everything else under there has a stable name and was overwritten rather than orphaned. Overflow is deleted now rather than silently kept, and a nonsense cap falls back to the default rather than deleting almost everything: the same fail-safe direction as `pruneVersions` and the audit-log retention.
- **`deleteSitePrefix` LOST ITS `keep` PARAMETER rather than keeping an unused one.** Both remaining callers are total wipes, so a `keep` nothing passes reads as a protection the function offers and does not — this file's own rule about guards that cannot fire, applied to a parameter.
- **DELETING A SITE TAKES THE MARKER WITH IT.** It lives outside `sites/<slug>/` precisely so the served prefix cannot expose it, and is therefore untouched by the prefix wipe — keyed by slug, and nothing would ever find it again. The `neon_teardown` and version-archive class, which is why each of those has its own line there too.
- **The cost, stated:** each site holds one publish of stale chunks (tens of kilobytes) plus one small JSON, and a publish pays one extra R2 get and one put. **A guard of mine was pinned to `rollbackVersion(versionDeps(env)` and went red on a correct change** the moment a rollback started getting the sweep — a test about word order, this repo's most repeated own-goal, re-anchored on the call.
- 2830 tests, **20/20 mutations as expected** from a verified-green baseline. **Not proven live** — the model account is empty, so no real publish has run against it; every decision here was driven against a fake store rather than reasoned.

## Nine claims that were not what they said (2026-08-14)

**Round 19, and the last of the audit's open findings that were mine to fix.** One theme, which is why they went together: **not one of them is a wrong answer.** Every build worked, every site published, every refund was attempted. What was wrong in each case was a MESSAGE, a COMMENT, a computed field, or a guard — something that claimed one thing while another was true. That is exactly why nine of them survived a 19-auditor sweep's worth of reading and needed measuring instead.

- **A BROKEN BODY WAS REPORTED AS A MISSING BRIEF.** `request.json().catch(() => ({}))` turned invalid JSON into an empty object, which then failed a thousand lines later as `no brief` — so an integrator with a trailing comma was told the one field they had definitely sent was missing. The shape check beside it is the same refusal for the same reason: `null` and `[]` are both valid JSON, neither has a `.brief`, and reading through one is a TypeError rather than a 400.
- **TWO READINGS OF "WHICH SITE IS THIS", IN ONE REQUEST.** `editSlug` was trimmed and lowercased; the claim was ALSO stripped to `[a-z0-9-]` and capped at 60 — so a body slug carrying a strippable character, or one over 60 characters, read the edit state under one name and built under another. Silent by construction: the `_meta` read simply misses, `editState` stays null, and the designer is never told to edit only what was asked. One expression now, and the guard asserts the PROPERTY — exactly one slug-cleaning expression in the whole route — rather than the spelling. **A non-string is refused rather than coerced**, because `String(["a","b"])` is `"a,b"`, which strips to a real slug nobody asked for.
- **THE SSRF GUARD IS A LOOP AND THE LOOP WAS HELD BY NOTHING.** `hostIsBlocked` is well tested against lists of hosts; the property that makes `safeFetch` safe is that it is re-checked on EVERY redirect hop, and the only guard on that was `assert.match(worker, /hostIsBlocked\(/)` — which any single call site satisfies. A refactor validating only the entry URL passes the entire suite, and **that is not hypothetical: the 2026-08-08 audit confirmed exactly that shape in `callApi`.** Now driven through the real `/api/import/fetch` with the global fetch replaced, and **the assertion is which addresses were actually requested** — "it answered 502" is equally satisfied by a guard that refuses everything and by one that fetched the metadata address and disliked the answer. Five internal classes, a three-hop chain whose third leg is `169.254.169.254`, and **a positive control** (a redirect to another public host IS followed), without which all six pass against a `safeFetch` that makes no request at all.
- **THE SHORTFALL WAS COMPUTED AND DROPPED AT THE WIRING**, for the umpteenth time. `publishPages` sets `billed` precisely so "the shortfall stays visible instead of vanishing" — and the route never carried it, so a build that billed 21 and collected 4 reported `cost: 4, charged: true` with the gap readable by nobody. `use_credits` is a gate rather than a till, so that shortfall is the ORDINARY outcome on a low-balance account: systematic under-collection was a revenue number no response could answer. Omitted when it agrees, so an ordinary build's response is byte-identical and the field's PRESENCE is the signal.
- **A FAILED REFUND WAS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A SUCCESSFUL ONE, at all thirteen call sites.** `creditBack` swallowed a throw AND ignored a non-ok response — and the RPC hard-caps one call at 10 credits, so a caller that has not gone through `refundCredits` really can be refused. The customer stays charged up to the whole fee more than the response reports, with nothing anywhere recording it. It answers now, and every caller gets the log for free. **Nothing FAILS over it** — a refund is the recovery path, and turning a failure to refund into a failure of the request would take the customer's site as well as their credits.
- **A COMMENT SAID THREE FIELDS "AGREE TODAY" AND THEY DISAGREE ON A COMMON PATH.** `schemaCredits` prices `schemaUsage` alone; `schemaCost` settles against `pageCredits(schemaUsage, seedUsage)` — so **every** build where the seed top-up fired shows a divergence with everything working perfectly. The direction still discriminates (a failed settlement diverges downward) so triage survives, and the fix is the comment rather than the numbers: folding the seed in would bury a second call's tokens inside the schema call's, which is how `sumUsage` came to charge one model at another's rate. **And the difference is now ACCOUNTABLE** — explaining a divergence in a comment is half the job if the response states two numbers and omits the third that reconciles them, so `seedUsage` is itemised beside `schemaUsage` and `pagesUsage`, for the same reason those two are apart.
