**Owner asked for an honest audit of every step of the build route, on Fable 5 with ultracode.** 38 agents: 19 auditors over coherent slices of the 30 steps, an adversarial verifier on every medium+ finding (CONFIRMED/REFUTED/UNCERTAIN, default-refuted), and a gaps agent told to grep before claiming anything missing. **Result: B+ overall (7.7/10, 13 A · 15 B · 1 C · 1 D), 30 confirmed medium+ findings, 0 refuted** — zero refutations is itself suspicious by this file's own rule, so the two HIGHs were re-verified by hand and by reproduction before anything was changed, and both held. Full detail in the scratchpad's `audit-synth.json`; the two grades below A-tier were both in code paths no customer had hit yet, which is the only reason they were not incidents.

- **THE SEED NET CRASHED THE BUILD THE MOMENT IT FIRED (the D), fixed.** `worker.js` read `slug` 140 lines before its `const` declaration — a TDZ ReferenceError on exactly the branch `topUpSeed` exists for, landing in the design catch as a 503 wearing a model-outage message. Never fired live only because no run yet had a gap. AND `seedGaps` read the raw tool input with a bare for..of, so a map-shaped `tables` (a form `normalizeSchema` deliberately accepts) threw OUTSIDE the try, voiding the module's own "it can never fail a build" contract; a string-shaped list silently reported no gaps. The log now names the site from what is in hand; `seedGaps` has the normaliser's tolerance; the contract is tested at the entry point across every shape.
- **PARTIAL VALIDATE MANGLED WORKING LINKS (the C), fixed.** The dangling-link rewrite judged against only the pages POSTED, and the edit/addon lanes post only what the model returned — so a one-page edit with a correct `<Link to="/book">` published with the link pointed at "/" and problems[] told the customer their live page did not exist. Both lanes pass `knownRoutes` from the stored site now; a partial set with NO known routes does not judge links at all (tsc is the real guard there — the lanes compile changed pages with the stored site and the route union makes a truly dangling link a type error).
- **THE VISIBILITY TRIO, fixed** — the works-but-cannot-say-so disease in three places: prerender was invisible on production builds (`preMs` + per-route skips now carried and forwarded; the trace's timing list is DERIVED BY SHAPE, any numeric `*Ms` key, because the audit caught the previous "derived" list already missing a field that existed the day it was written); a TOTAL prerender failure silently disabled the render check (the `pre.done.length` gate is gone — checkRender's own first line answers an empty list honestly, before any browser launches); a salvaged build stripped its first failure's `error`/`cited` (the gate was `page === "app"`, which a salvage answers) and the routing response dropped `failed:true` (an Anthropic outage wore the costume of a real routing decision on the one route that never 5xxs by design). **A guard had to be re-anchored that was asserting the exact bug** — it demanded checkRender sit on the true branch of the prerender gate.
- **ROUND 2, the confirmed singles:** `setTitle` corrupted a page on titles carrying `$$`/`$&`/`$'` (String.replace treats those as replacement PATTERNS; "Mo$'s Cuts" doubled the head — function replacers now, here and in the `site-identity.mjs` twin). The build route and `/api/site/route` parsed uncapped bodies (24MB and 2MB caps now, before buffering, like every other body-taking route). `siteOgImage`'s `|| objs[0]` fallback let a STRANGER'S upload become the business's WhatsApp preview image exactly when the library held only visitor uploads — owner uploads or nothing now. Both name-taken 409s carried no `msg`, so the client showed "try again in a moment" and the customer retried into the same 409 forever (the designer re-proposes the same name) — the message names the cause and the way out now.
- **THE SLUG RACE, fixed at both slug-keyed writes.** Both went through an upserting `write()` (merge-duplicates), so two overlapping first builds of one free name BOTH succeeded: the second `saveProject` overwrote the winner's connection row — the winner's live site then pointed at the loser's project — and the loser's project was orphaned with no teardown entry (the queue trigger is BEFORE DELETE; an upsert UPDATE never fires it). `claim()` now inserts with `ignore-duplicates + return=representation`, atomic on the PK: a full representation is ours, an empty one means somebody else holds the slug. **The module decides what losing means at each site — CONVERGE at the project (destroy own, read back the winner's, continue: the database create is idempotent), REFUSE at the slug (`conflict:true` → the existing 409 + refund + the new message).** A crashed build's retry can never lose to its own stale row, because `lookupProject` runs before `createProject`. Claim-first (claiming the slug before creating anything) was considered and rejected: `neon_db` is NOT NULL so it needs DDL, and a pending claim row would make a crashed build's retry read as a revise.
- **ROUND 3, THE JOBS TIER — four holes in one feature, all confirmed, all fixed.** (1) **The stamp is a CONDITIONAL CLAIM now, not a fire-and-forget write.** Cloudflare cron ticks OVERLAP when one outlasts its 2-minute interval — 25 jobs of up to 100 sequential provider sends can take many minutes — so two ticks could both read a job as due and both mail its whole batch: the exact double-send the stamp-first ordering exists to prevent, open through a different door. And the old write was never `r.ok`-checked, so Supabase in read-only mode (reads fine, writes 5xx) let the send proceed unstamped and re-mail everyone every tick until writes recovered. The PATCH's WHERE re-states dueness (`or=(last_run.is.null,last_run.lt.CUTOFF)`, the cutoff mirroring `dueJobs`' 30s slack) with `return=representation` — a row back means we won, empty means another tick did, `!r.ok` and a throw both read as LOST, because a claim that cannot be recorded is a claim lost. The loser returns `skipped` before `callFn` — it touches neither the site's database nor the vault nor the wire — and **writes no `last_result`**, or every overlap would bury the winning run's outcome under "skipped". Strictly `=== false`, so a dep that cannot say behaves as before. (2) **`callFn`'s three bare nulls got NAMES** — database unreachable, job no longer declared, unusable function name — all three used to wear the broken-SQL sentence in the owner's panel; a `jobsSkip` string from the caller becomes the sentence now, and it cannot be faked from data (the sentinel is a bare-object property; a LIST whose rows carry it is just bad messages, dropped one by one). **The JSON parse is fenced with a FIXED sentence**: V8's SyntaxError quotes ~26 characters of the INPUT, and a malformed result can begin with a customer's address — so no parse error ever reaches `last_result`, and the catch binds nothing. (3) **The OFF SWITCH exists** — `POST /api/site/<slug>/jobs {name, enabled}`, owner-gated, a REAL boolean (`enabled:"false"` would switch a job ON), the PATCH scoped owner+slug+name+scheduled with representation so **zero rows matched is a 404, never an ok that switched nothing**. The audit proved NO path could stop a job before this: `_meta.jobs` is a union-merge nothing removes from, the rules lane's CLEARABLE is exactly confirm/sms, and nothing anywhere wrote `enabled:false` — the runner has filtered on that flag all along. The panel's badge IS the button now (filled On / outline Paused, the greyscale discipline), posting the opposite of the server's last answer and repainting by reload, the notify toggle's idiom; driven in a real render, On → POST → Paused. (4) **`deleteSiteFor` deletes the site's `site_functions` rows** — keyed by slug, not uid, so they cascade with nothing; each one left behind was a ZOMBIE doing a stamp write, a project lookup and a result write per period forever. BEFORE the registration row, deliberately: a failed cleanup while the row exists is retryable through the same route; after the row is gone it is permanent, because the delete route 404s with nothing to authorise against. And the runner's `credentials` reads the vault through `siteMailSecrets` — it was a hand-rolled THIRD copy of the four-name loop, one provider away from "no key in Secrets" while confirmations sent fine on that key; the notify guard now asserts the four names are enumerated ONCE in the whole Worker.
- **ROUND 4 — the four wire-cuts and stuck states.** (1) **The routing digest's table list is FED now.** Nothing in the client ever wrote `site.tables`, so the digest sent `tables: []` on every message of every site — while the routing tool's own tie-break and its cheapest `data` layer are both conditioned on "the tables it has are named above": the router decided blind on the one fact separating a free data edit from a ~25-credit addon, and edit-smoke could not see it because its digest is built from the build response's own tables field (a fixture more capable than reality, the `setTotp` shape). The build finish handler and the addon handler both store names now — **a UNION, never a replace**, because on a revise `d.tables` is the DELTA the apply touched (`d.schema` is the merged list and is preferred). (2) **The closed-questions block names NO work intent any more.** It said *answer "build" or "ask" only* — written when those were the only intents, never updated for the ladder — so every message about a LIVE site carried two contradictory instructions and the stale one pointed at the rebuild. The state block is the ONE place legal answers are enumerated; the closed block owns exactly one fact (clarify is over), and the guard is derived over the tool's own intent enum so a sixth intent cannot re-stale it. (3) **The addon lane has the seed net**, narrowed to the tables it actually ADDED (a re-declared filled table is skipped by the seeder, so buying rows for one is a Haiku call spent on rows that are discarded); the bought rows are merged into what is planted, the `{seeded, skipped}` report is KEPT and rides the response with `seedTopUp`, and the top-up's usage joins the one variadic `pageCredits` call — one bill, one rounding. (4) **A site that lost its `_meta` service endpoints can heal.** They are written once each, best-effort, on the non-reuse path — so one blip during a first build was a site answering 501 on every read, form and sign-in FOREVER, while the 501's copy promised a rebuild would fix it and the reuse path called zero deps. `ensureSiteBackend` now asks `missingServices` on the reuse path — the SAME `siteServiceBase` reader the 501 decision uses, never the memoized caches (a stale null must not decide whether to heal) — and re-runs the enables for exactly the missing halves. A reader that cannot tell heals nothing (a blip must not read as missing and spend Neon calls per build); a healthy site pays one `_meta` read and no project lookup; a failing heal warns and never fails the edit; `info: null` is never saved over a stored endpoint. And `enableNeonAuth`'s already-branch now RE-FETCHES the config the way `enableDataApi`'s has since 2026-08-04 — the asymmetry was the bug: null on the already-branch meant no later build could ever recover the endpoint.
- **ROUND 5 — the last four no-dependency mediums.** (1) **The pair gates, both confirmed by execution.** `lintPages`' write gate read `canWriteAccess(t.access)` — the CRIT-D fix updated every neighbouring check to pass the TABLE and stopped one expression short — so a form against `{read:"public", write:"none"}` (stamped `collect` by the normaliser) passed CLEAN and 403'd for every visitor, while a legal write against `display+write:anyone` was falsely flagged with the flag's own message printing the correctly-resolved label. And `schemaDigest`'s PAID line was gated `access === "collect"`, so the same pair table got *"PAID: NO — submit it with useCreateRow"* printed directly under the pair note saying *"no form, no useCreateRow"* — a prompt that both forbids and prescribes the form. Both resolve the pair now, and a payable table gets its do-NOT-useCreateRow warning whatever access it wears: the payment is the fact that decides. (2) **A failed look read FAILS the edit** — `recompileAndPublish`'s read of `site_look`/tokens/style/logo was a console.error catch, and a null `siteBackendBySlug` equally silent, so a transient blip during any cheap edit published the live site with no theme, default fonts and its SLUG as its title, reported success, and archived the stripped version to history. The catch and the null both refuse with `ours: true` — every lane's `compileMsg` then says the honest sentence (our side, try again, nothing charged) — and a read that SUCCEEDS with no rows still proceeds, so a pre-look-era site keeps its cheap edits: only cannot-tell refuses. (3) **A bare re-declaration keeps its RLS clauses.** Two halves: the four policy booleans (`trash`/`expires`/`scheduled`/`teamScope`) adopt `retiredOf`'s own contract — `undefined` when absent, so the absent-means-unchanged merge can tell silence from clearance, while an explicit `false` still clears (that IS the removal verb) — and the stored-table fill is HOISTED above the DDL loop (`fillFromStored`, exported so the fix is provable by composition through the real `policiesFor`: before the fill a teamScope+expires+scheduled+trash `deals` re-declared bare emits `USING (owner_id = app_user_id())` alone; after it, all four clauses return). The hoist also closes the grant half — a re-declared RETIRED table used to have its public grants re-emitted for the apply — and the late merge reuses the same read, one `loadSiteSchema` per apply. The `DERIVED_TABLE_FIELDS` exclusion is belt-and-braces and says so in the source: `norm.push` overrides all six, so a mutant removing it is inert against the current loop. (4) **The render report reaches the customer.** Every edit lane pays ~6s for the in-container render check and threw the result away; the spine now returns it under the build response's own noteworthy contract (the report rides only when it failed or found something — a clean edit's response is byte-identical), all seven call sites forward `render`+`renderNote` (derived guard over the call sites, so an eighth lane is covered automatically), and the client appends the sentence to the edit and addon replies via one `renderTail` at the call sites.
- **Findings known and deliberately NOT yet fixed:** research executed by nothing; photos never executed (fal unfunded); the gaps package (database backup — the one HIGH-value gap — sitemap, JSON-LD, branded 404, redirects, error telemetry delivered to nobody). Each is in `audit-synth.json` with file+line.
- **Sweep notes, all three rounds:** 18/18 + **17/17** real mutants caught from verified-green baselines. One "survivor" was my mutation not applying (escaped quotes inside the 409 msg defeated the perl pattern — verified by re-applying with a pattern that bites); one absence-guard matched its own fix's COMMENT (the og test now blanks comments before judging, since prose explaining a bug contains the bug's spelling); the route-`failed` guard's first byte-window was 3k short because the handler is well-commented — landmark-anchored now. Round 3's own pre-sweep catch: two of the jobs-visibility tests were `at + 1800` byte windows and went red the moment the handler grew its POST branch — the jb window runs to the `nt` landmark now — and my first reload assertion matched the panel's own initial `load();`, vacuous against removing the toggle's repaint, re-anchored on `return; }` + reload.

## `worker.js` CAN be imported, and the belief that it could not is why twelve features shipped dead (2026-08-13)

**Asked to fix the wiring layer, and checked the premise before moving anything.** "worker.js cannot be imported" is stated as fact in five places in this file and is the justification for every guard on the Worker being a source-reading regex. **It is false.** The file imports fine. What failed was ONE dependency — `@cloudflare/containers`, whose published `dist/index.js` re-exports from `"./lib/container"` with no extension. Node's ESM resolver refuses that; wrangler's bundler resolves it. Two symbols, one shim, and the real router can be driven with real requests.

- **THE FIX IS NOT WHAT THE ENTRY BELOW IT PROPOSED.** The plan was to extract handlers into importable modules — weeks of work, and it does not even retire the bug: something in `worker.js` still has to route to them, and *that* line is still untested. What actually retires it is a test that boots the REAL Worker with empty bindings and drives real requests. `test/fixtures/worker-harness.mjs`, ~60 lines.
- **THE WHOLE INSTRUMENT IS ONE DISTINCTION: 404 vs 401.** The router's bottom answer is `404 {"error":"not found"}`; a route that exists and refuses an unauthenticated caller answers 401. That is exactly what nobody could tell when `/versions`, `/text` and `/domains` were answering the bottom 404 to their own owner — **and what a live probe could not tell either**, because `assertOwner` answers 404 on purpose so a stranger cannot enumerate slugs. Matched on the BODY, not the status alone, or a handler's deliberate 404 reads as unreachable.
- **PROVEN AGAINST THE HISTORICAL BUGS, not asserted.** Two mutants reproduce the real failures — a route dropped from the `if (om || mm || …)` dispatch condition (the `dm2` bug) and the outer `startsWith("/api/site/")` gate narrowed (how `/versions` and `/text` were dead end to end). **Both are caught by the drive and by NOTHING in the source-read half**, measured by running each mutant against both and reporting which tests went red. 4/4 mutants caught.
- **IT FOUND A FALSE ALARM OF ITS OWN FIRST, and that is the lesson.** The first scan read every call as a GET and reported `/api/game/delete` dead. It is DELETE-only and perfectly fine. The verb window had stopped at the first `)`, which is the one inside `encodeURIComponent(slug)` — so the check would have accused a working route on its first run. Chunked by the next `apiFetch(` now, and a test asserts the scan still finds a DELETE.
- **VALIDATED BY REPRODUCING THE KNOWN ANSWER.** Driven over all 43 client calls it reports exactly the three routes already in `KNOWN_DEAD` — `collections`, `preview`, `scan` — and nothing else. A new instrument that independently arrives at the existing answer is one worth trusting with the next one.
- **THE STUB THROWS RATHER THAN SUCCEEDING.** `getContainer` in the fixture raises, because a routing test that reaches a container has left the layer this is for and is about to wait on a build that never comes. A fake success there reads as a passing test of something never exercised. The loader redirects exactly ONE specifier and passes everything else through — a broad hook quietly stubs a real module and makes green meaningless, which a mutant confirms.
- **The source-reads are KEPT, not replaced.** They cover the client's own half — that a button posts to the route it should, that the note block holds what it should — which no amount of driving the Worker can see. What changes is that reachability stopped being their job.
- 2521 tests. **The five stale claims in this file are corrected below rather than deleted**, because the false premise is what makes the twelve-times bug intelligible: nobody was being lazy, everybody believed the layer was untestable.

## The render check was dead on its first CI run, and one assertion said it was fine (2026-08-13)

**Merged the render check to main and it came back 102 passed, 6 failed — `Cannot find package 'playwright-core'`, six times.** It had been 110/110 locally an hour earlier. The workflow's own comment predicted it in the file I was editing: *"a browser step placed above this one passes locally and fails only in CI — which is exactly what theme-render did on its first run on main."* I added a browser step above that line.

- **`import("playwright-core")` RESOLVES FROM `builder/` UPWARD, so the template's `node_modules` is never consulted.** `site-build.yml` runs `npm ci` in `builder/lovable/template` and never at the root — and this repo's own root install happens to have playwright-core, which is the entire reason it passed here. The workflow now installs it at the root.
- **PINNED TO THE TEMPLATE'S EXACT PLAYWRIGHT, not to a range.** The browser is downloaded by the template's `playwright` and located by playwright-core **by exact revision directory**, so two copies a patch apart look for `chromium-1193` and `chromium-1194` and one is not there. Already a near-miss: template 1.62.0, root 1.62.1.
- **BOTH OBVIOUS ANSWERS FOR THE BROWSER PATH ARE WRONG, and each was measured.** Hardcoding `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium` is this container's path and exists on no runner — that was the original bug. Removing the default and letting playwright resolve **also failed, here**: `Executable doesn't exist at .../chromium_headless_shell-1234/...`, because the root playwright-core wants revision 1234 and this container has 1194. `chromiumEnv()` uses an explicit path when given, falls back to the container symlink **only if the file really exists**, and otherwise says nothing. Checking existence is the whole difference.
- **ONE ASSERTION PASSED WHILE THE CHECKER WAS DEAD, and it is the shape this file keeps recording.** *"…and the kit's own panel, which was FIXED to bg-popover, is reported clean"* is `[].every(…)`, which is `true` — so with no browser it reported the panel clean while the render check two lines above returned `ok:false`. **A negative assertion has to prove the observer was alive first**; it is gated on `render.ok === true` now. Proven both ways: with the browser broken it FAILS (101/7 against CI's 102/6, the extra failure being exactly the one that used to lie), and with it working the run is 110/110.
- **The good-page block was already gated (`if (r && r.ok)`) and that is why only six failed rather than nine.** The design was half right; the negative cases were the half nobody gated.

## `schema gen eval` had been dead for a day, on a name nobody added (2026-08-13)

**It dies at startup on `SITE_STYLE_AXES is not defined` — on this commit and the one before.** The 17 style axes landed in `design_schema` the day before and the eval's hand-written `scope` never got them, so the designer's own quality measurement has recorded nothing since.

- **THE EVAL CANNOT IMPORT THE TOOL**, which is why the scope exists: `design_schema` is a literal inside `worker.js`, read as text and `eval`ed against a list of the identifiers it references. That list is maintained by remembering, and it drifts the moment the tool grows.
- **`test/schema-eval-scope.test.mjs` RUNS IN THE UNIT SUITE, deliberately.** The eval failed loudly and in CI, and it is one job among six on a push — so it went unread for a day. The check belongs where it is seen and where it costs nothing: no API key, no model call.
- **DERIVED AT BOTH ENDS.** Scope keys are read out of the eval, the tool block out of `worker.js`, and the block is evaluated with every scope name bound to a permissive stub — only an undefined name can throw. Nothing restates either side, so a constant added tomorrow is covered without anybody remembering the file.
- **`instanceof ReferenceError` DOES NOT WORK ACROSS A `vm` CONTEXT**, and that nearly shipped broken. A new realm has its own intrinsics, so the error is built from the VM's constructor; matching on `e.name` is what works. **Found by the negative test** — the positive one was green and would have rethrown a raw error instead of naming the missing identifier, which is precisely the failure it exists to describe.
- **The stub has to iterate as ENTRY PAIRS.** The first draft yielded a bare string and died on `Object.fromEntries(SITE_STYLE_AXES.map(…))` with *"Iterator value x is not an entry object"* — a `TypeError` short-circuits the evaluation, so a `ReferenceError` further down never surfaces, and the negative test then passed for the wrong reason.
- 2524 tests, **3/3 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline.

## A new account cannot build on a cold cache, and the gate for that is stale (2026-08-13)

**Measured on a real `build smoke` run: 35 passed, 2 failed, `page=placeholder stage=credits`.** The schema half ran perfectly — 2 tables, 6 seeded rows, a Neon project, 20.8s — and page generation never started. *"Your database is live, but there weren't enough credits left to write the pages."*

- **`SCHEMA_PROFILE` IS THE MEASUREMENT `buildFloor` GATES ON AND IT IS 42% LIGHT.** Recorded 2026-08-08 as `{in: 236, out: 1319, cacheWrite: 13357}` → 9 credits. The same call today: `{in: 236, out: 1490, cacheWrite: 19008}` → **12 credits**, matching the run's own line exactly. The schema prompt has grown since — the 17 style axes among other things.
- **SO THE GATE PASSES AND THE BUILD STILL CANNOT FINISH.** `buildFloor(sonnet)` is 9 + 8 = 17 against a 20-credit grant, so nothing refuses up front; then `20 − 1 routing − 12 schema = 7`, below `MIN_CREDITS` 8. **This is the exact bug `buildFloor` was written to prevent**, recurring on the default model because its profile went stale. It is not confined to the test: a real new customer lands on 7 too.
- **IT IS A COIN FLIP, WHICH IS WORSE THAN A CONSTANT FAILURE.** Cold cache → 12 credits → refused, charged, placeholder. Warm → 4 credits → 15 left → builds. The cache is global to the prompt, not per-account, so whether a new customer's first site works depends on whether anyone built in the last few minutes.
- **NOT FIXED, because the one-line fix is not the decision.** Updating the profile makes `buildFloor` 20 — exactly the grant — so a cold new account would be refused up front **with a refund** instead of charged 12 for nothing. Honest, and it means no new account ever builds cold. The real options are raising the grant or shrinking the cold schema call; that is the owner's call.
- **STILL NOT FIXED, AND THE OWNER'S ANSWER IS NOT THE GRANT (2026-08-15).** *"Use my account, don't raise the credit thing."* The profile was updated, `buildFloor` is 20, and the routing call spends 1 first — so the shortfall for a real new customer is one credit and **that is still true today**. What changed is only that `build smoke` runs as a funded account and therefore stopped reporting it; `test/build-models.test.mjs` is what pins it now. See the entry at the top of this file.
- **THE PREVIOUS RUN FAILED COMPLETELY DIFFERENTLY AND HID THIS**: `billing: true`, 6 passed / 16 failed, no model call at all. The Anthropic account has been topped up since, so the outage was masking a distinct problem. **Read the failure shape before the failure count.**
- **The render step is STILL unproven in production** — the build stopped at the floor, so generation, compile and render never ran. And `build smoke` logs neither `render` nor `renderMs`, so even a successful build could not tell you the step happened: a clean render and a render that never ran look identical from outside.

## Nothing in the build path had ever LOOKED at the site (2026-08-13, owner's call)

**Asked what the pipeline was missing, and the answer was one thing with overwhelming evidence behind it: all four checks are TEXTUAL.** `validate` reads the files, `lint` reads them against the schema, `typecheck` compiles, `build` bundles — and not one of them ever draws a page. So a generated site could be blank, unreadable or throwing on load and score a clean run on all 26 steps: compiled, bundled, published, reported as a success. **`render` is the new 1.t**, between build and salvage; salvage/publish/charge shift to 1.u/1.v/1.w.

- **THE EVIDENCE IS THIS FILE'S OWN HISTORY.** Every visual failure ever recorded here was found by a HUMAN looking at a screen: all 70 charts rendering grey · every uploaded image 404'ing on the only address anybody shares · a dark background leaving every line of body copy invisible · the CJK favicon overflowing its box · a see-through modal on every site · an alphabetical menu. **Eight-plus, and the pipeline had no opinion about any of them.** A compile pass proves the code is valid, never that anybody can read the page.
- **REPORTING ONLY, NOTHING IS GATED (owner's call).** No salvage, no refusal, no charge question. The first version measures its own false-alarm rate on real sites before it is allowed teeth — which is this file's own rule, applied to itself: a check that cries wolf teaches everyone to ignore it, and a false *"your site is blank"* that refused a publish would be catastrophic.
- **THE MODULE JUDGES AND THE HARNESS OBSERVES.** `probe()` runs in the page and returns numbers; `site-render.mjs` decides what a number MEANS and has no browser, so all of it is driven with literal objects. Same shape as `publish-pages.mjs` taking its side effects injected — the layer where a mistake produces a false alarm on a customer's good site is the layer that has to be testable without infrastructure.
- **THE PHONE WIDTH IS THE POINT, not a bonus.** Nothing in this pipeline had ever rendered below 1280px, and the see-through modal was found by somebody tapping a hamburger. Most visitors to a barber shop's site are on a phone, so the untested width was the primary one. **Proven live:** a 900px block reports 525px of sideways scroll on the phone and NOTHING on the desktop, and the finding names the widest offender.
- **THE CONTRAST FLOOR IS 3, NOT WCAG'S 4.5, and that is a false-alarm decision.** Every theme's muted caption, placeholder and disabled control sits in the 3-4.5 band ON PURPOSE; reporting at 4.5 would flag deliberate design on every page of every site. 3.0 is where legibility actually stops — the recorded failure it exists for measured ~1.2.
- **COLOURS ARE READ THROUGH A 1x1 CANVAS, NEVER PARSED.** The palette is oklch and what `getComputedStyle` returns for one varies by engine and version. A parser is a thing to keep up to date forever and a wrong parse is a false alarm on correct code; painting the colour and reading the pixel makes the BROWSER do the conversion, which is the one implementation guaranteed to agree with what the visitor sees. **A translucent ancestor chain is SKIPPED rather than composited** — a glass theme is translucent by design and guessing what is behind it is exactly the guess that flags a working page.
- **TWO HARNESS BUGS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, and the second decides whether the check is honest at all.** (1) It loaded `/index.html`, so the router matched no route and **every correct page reported `blank: only 9 characters`** — which is the exact length of "Not found". A route path maps to its prerendered file now. (2) **The data API is stubbed empty**: a generated page calls `/api/db/<slug>/data/<table>` on load and there is no database near this container, so unstubbed EVERY site with a list would log a failed request and render its error state — a fault reported on every build it ever ran. An empty array is also the truthful stand-in, and it exercises the empty state the generator is required to write.
- **THE ASSERTION THAT MATTERS IS THE CLEAN ONE.** `site-build` posts the reference page and requires **zero** findings; the three below it (blank, overflow, a throw only the browser can see) prove it can still fail, or a clean run means nothing. **Posting `index.tsx` alone is a trap this file already records and I walked straight back into** — its `<Link to="/menu">` fails the TYPECHECK, so the build never reaches a browser and the failure reads as "the render check did not run". My assertion did not name the stage, which cost a hunt at the wrong layer; it does now.
- **THREE OF SEVENTEEN MUTANTS SURVIVED AND ALL THREE WERE MINE.** `/apt-get install[\s\S]*chromium/` passed with the package deleted from the install list, because `CHROMIUM_PATH=/usr/bin/chromium` further down still contains the word and `[\s\S]*` reaches it. `--no-save` matched the COMMENT above the RUN line, so stripping the real flag survived. Both anchored on the command now. The third was inert — a mutation that only moves a comment — for the umpteenth recorded time.
- **THE BROWSER IS THE DISTRO CHROMIUM driven by `playwright-core`**, exactly as `builder-game/smoke.mjs` already does, and installed `--no-save`: `npm ci` refuses a lock that disagrees with package.json, and a published customer site must never carry a browser driver in its dependency tree.
- **The cost, stated:** ~6s on a 20-40s build, 0 credits, and ~150MB of container image. `checkRender` has a try around everything and returns a report either way, so a browser that will not start costs a report and never a build — and `ok:false` is carried through rather than omitted, because a harness that silently reports nothing reads exactly like a site with nothing wrong.
- **AND THEN IT LEARNED TO CLICK, which is the other half of the see-through modal.** A static render cannot see a panel that does not exist until somebody opens it, so the check now opens what the KIT owns — never arbitrary elements, because a stray click submits a form or navigates away. **Same page, same browser, phone only**: the load is already paid for, a hamburger sheet is the case, and a panel's colours do not change with the width. **PROVEN LIVE, both directions**: a real Sheet with a translucent panel comes back *"an opened panel ("Menu") is 35% opaque"*, and the kit's own untouched panel — the one FIXED to `bg-popover` because of this bug — comes back clean.
- **THE RULE IS INVERTED FROM THE CONTRAST ONE, deliberately.** There a translucent chain is SKIPPED because guessing what is behind it false-alarms on a glass theme. Here translucency IS the fault — a modal exists to cover the page — so the panel's own alpha is the measurement and a `backdrop-filter` is the exemption, since that is exactly what makes a glass panel readable. **A trigger that opens NOTHING is not a finding**: far more often a control the harness could not drive than a broken button.
- **FIVE OF THE SEVEN TRIGGER SELECTORS ARE INERT AGAINST THIS TEMPLATE, and it is written down rather than left looking load-bearing.** The overlays here are RAW Radix (`const SheetTrigger = SheetPrimitive.Trigger`), so nothing renders a `data-slot` and the whole check rides on the two `aria-haspopup` lines. The five are kept because newer shadcn stamps them and a kit refresh is the likely way this template grows them — and a **derived test measures the kit** and goes red if that stops being the story, because a list where most entries can never match is protection that reads real and is not.
- **FOUR OF TWELVE OVERLAY MUTANTS SURVIVED THE FIRST SWEEP, ALL MINE.** Two were tests that could not discriminate — `readOverlay({opened:false})` carries no alpha, so deleting the guard changed nothing; and two assertions were written in terms of `MIN_PANEL_ALPHA` itself, so moving the floor moved the test with it. One was a **real gap**: dropping `seethrough` from the note's wording left the finding computed, returned and never said out loud. And one was a **vacuous match** — `/catch \{/` hit the `page.$$` catch three lines above, so replacing the click loop's catch with a rethrow survived.
- 2517 tests, `site build` **110/110**, **17/17 + 12/12 mutations** from verified-green baselines. **NOT PROVEN IN THE REAL CONTAINER** — the image has not been built here, so the apt install and the `--no-save` layer are unverified against a real `docker build`; everything else ran against the real `build-server.mjs` and a real Chromium.

## A scheduled job worked and nobody could tell (2026-08-13)

**Asked whether `jobs` was any good, went and read it, and the mechanism is careful — `runJob` stamps `last_run` BEFORE sending on purpose, and re-reads the site's own schema so a job dropped by a revise really stops. It also computes an honest FOUR-WAY outcome and every caller threw it into a `console.log`.** A Cloudflare log is not a surface a small business has.

- **SO FOUR DIFFERENT THINGS WERE ONE SILENCE:** *sent 14 reminders* · *the model's SQL is broken* · *the owner never pasted a mail key* · *nothing was due*. For a REMINDER that is the worst failure shape there is, because the customer does not know they were meant to get one either — the only symptom is a no-show months later that looks like ordinary business.
- **THE OUTCOME ALREADY EXISTED. Nothing was computed wrong; it was discarded.** Same shape as `notes` on the build response, which the model wrote and nothing rendered — and as `oneClickBlocked`, computed by the route and shown by nothing. `jobOutcome` turns it into one plain sentence and `last_result` (a new column) carries it to the owner. **NO RECIPIENT EVER APPEARS IN IT** — this is a platform table beside every other site's, and counts and reasons carry what the owner needs. Same allow-list discipline as the audit log.
- **A SECOND WRITE, AFTER the run, never folded into `stamp`.** That one goes first on purpose: stamped afterwards, a job that dies mid-send is due again on the next tick and mails everyone it already reached. **Losing the note costs a line of history; moving the stamp costs somebody four copies of the same reminder** — asserted by ordering, and a mutant that swaps them dies.
- **THE PANEL WAS DEAD THREE WAYS AT ONCE, which is a record.** The Cloud card was forced Off in `DEAD_PANELS`; its handler rendered `spec.steps` from the eight-verb runner deleted with the D1 runtime; and it called `/api/site/functions`, which does not exist. The comment directly above `DEAD_PANELS` warns about precisely this — `versions` sat Off for four days after its route shipped. **Read that list as a live hazard, not documentation.**
- **THE RATCHET FORCED THE CLEANUP, and that is it working.** `KNOWN_DEAD` in `client-routes.test.mjs` is exactly-equal rather than an allow-list, so removing the last caller of `/api/site/functions` went RED until the entry came out. A fixed route cannot sit in that list looking like debt.
- **`api-auth`'s WINDOW WAS RAISED 45 → 60 ONCE ALREADY for the same reason and a third raise would be treating the symptom.** Its own note says *"the gate did not move; the declarations in front of it grew"* — and documenting the new matcher pushed `authUser` out of range again. **Comment lines no longer count toward the window**, blanked line-wise and conservatively (a whole-file blanker on `worker.js` eats 46% of it — measured). That also closed a hole in the other direction the old form had: a comment merely MENTIONING `authUser(` satisfied the check for a route that never called it. Both proved by mutation.
- **A MUTATION SURVIVOR THAT DISTINCTNESS COULD NOT SEE.** With the `ok === false` branch dead, a job that CRASHED falls through to the success path and reports *"Ready to send, but threw."* — a sentence different from every other, so "all five outcomes differ" stayed green, and it reads like the mail-key case: one small thing away from working. It is not. A failure must announce itself, asserted separately.
- 2473 tests, **13/13 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline. **Not proven live** — no job has been observed running, and on a site with no mail key it never will send.

## The look was two knobs and eleven frozen decisions (2026-08-13, owner's call)

**"The token needs to be able to do all of that."** A customer could change a COLOUR and, later, the corner radius. The other twelve decisions a theme makes were frozen the moment the build finished — ask for square buttons and one of two things happened: nothing, or the designer swapped the whole theme looking for one that has them, which changes the colours and the fonts and the spacing too. **They asked for one thing and got a different site.** `builder/site-style.mjs` is the twelve, as a plain module.

- **THE MEASUREMENT IS WHAT DECIDED THE SHAPE, and it says "not more tokens".** Six of the twelve emit CSS custom properties and could be patched exactly the way a colour is; **three emit ordinary RULES** (`.lucide { stroke-width }`, `.border-input`, a border-radius on the button selector) and one emits both — so a token patch reaches two thirds of them and silently misses the rest. But every one is read off the THEME OBJECT by an emitter that already exists (`typeCss(theme.scale)`, `buttonsCss(theme.buttons)`, …). **So the patch belongs one layer earlier**: merge it into the theme and all twelve are GENERATED correctly rather than overridden afterwards — no source-order trick, no specificity question, and `writeTheme` changes by one call.
- **ENUMS, NOT VALUES, and that is what makes it cheap.** There is no "slightly rounder button" — there is pill, sharp, or let the radius decide. Twelve axes × ~3 options is 38 legal answers, all ours, all known before the build starts. `isColor`/`isLength` exist because a colour is arbitrary text on its way into a stylesheet; **nothing here needs a parser, because nothing here is arbitrary** — the tool carries a per-axis `enum`, so an option the engine would refuse is impossible rather than merely dropped. ~508 tokens, in the cached block.
- **THE OPTIONS AND THEIR LABELS ARE DERIVED FROM THE ENGINE'S OWN CONSTANTS.** A restated list is a second thing that can drift, and the direction it drifts in is describing an option to the model that the engine then refuses — reported to the customer as a change that did not happen. Only the PLAIN NAME is hand-written, because it is the one thing the engine does not know: its own label for `density` is *"close — more on a screen, a working tool"*, which is right for somebody choosing a theme and wrong for a sentence saying what just changed.
- **THE FIVE "WORLD" AXES WENT IN TOO (owner's call, same day), AND RENDERING DISPROVED THE EXCLUSION IN FOUR OF FIVE CASES.** They were held back on the `--chart-1..5` argument — a set whose whole job is being coherent with each other. Driven and LOOKED AT one at a time on a flat theme: `backdrop` alone is fine (crisper, if anything), `decor` alone is a texture on the paper, `skin` alone is the card's own shape, `ambient` alone is motion over whatever is behind. **Only `surface: glass` is coupled, and it is worse than the exclusion claimed** — not "subtly wrong": `surfaceCss` sets `--card` to 0.5 alpha, so on a plain ground the cards **VANISH**, the same mechanism as the see-through-modals bug. The dependency runs ONE way and is one axis wide. The kit is **17 axes**; the prompt cost went ~508 → ~995 tokens (decor's 20 options are 902 chars of it), in the cached block.
  - **THE COUPLING IS SATISFIED, NOT REFUSED.** `applyStyle` gives glass a wash when nothing is behind it (`aurora`, whose own label is *"the glass canvas, for any surface"*). Derived **at the point of use and never stored** — exactly where `withContrast` sits and for the same reason: what is kept is only what the customer asked for, and it cannot spend a slot of their `MAX_STYLE`. Silent, also like `withContrast`, because this is not a second change made on their behalf — frosted panels with nothing to frost against is the request FAILING, not a cheaper version of it. An explicit backdrop always wins.
  - **`plain` IS NOT A BACKDROP, found by mutation and a real hole.** Its own label is *"nothing behind the page"*, so counting it as one leaves glass with nothing to frost against — the exact failure the coupling exists for. Every test used a theme whose backdrop is ABSENT, so the distinction was covered by nothing. **0 of the 100 shortlist themes declare it explicitly**, but a customer can ask for it: `glass` + `plain` is self-contradictory and resolves toward the page that works.
  - **TWO MORE NAMES READ AS EACH OTHER, and the equality guard could not see them.** `background` beside `background wash` and `background motion`, and `cards` beside `card style` — the exact-clash bug one step quieter, live the moment the world axes landed. The colour tokens are `page colour` and `card colour` now, and the guard checks prefixes as well as equality.
- **THE ONE PLACE TWO PATCHES COLLIDE, and it was already a live bug.** `stripThemeRadius` drops a theme's own hard-set `border-radius` rules when a radius is asked for — and it is a regex, so it cannot tell those from the ones `buttonsCss`/`inputsCss` emit. "Rounder corners AND pill buttons" got the first and silently lost the second. The rule that resolves it is the one already in force here: **an EXPLICIT corner opinion beats an implicit one** — the theme's gives way, the customer's does not — so the axes they NAMED are re-emitted after the strip. `RADIUS_AXES` is held in code and **derived in the test**, which runs all twelve emitters over every option and asserts the list is the whole of it.
- **A LOOK EDIT COUNTS IT AS A CHANGE, or the whole thing is worse than useless.** Without `styleMoved` in the no-change check, "square buttons" escalates to a ~27-credit page rewrite that **cannot put square buttons on anything either** — the rung above recompiles from the same stored look.
- **ITS OWN `_meta` KEY, never a field on `site_look`.** `mergeLook` rebuilds its output from `EDIT_FIELDS` alone and drops anything else stored there, so a customer changing a colour would silently lose their buttons — the reason `site_tokens` and `site_logo` are separate keys too. Accumulated like tokens, because a revise names only what it is changing.
- **THE MUTATION SWEEP FOUND THREE REAL GAPS AND ALL THREE WERE MINE, in the wiring.** (1) Nothing asserted the container APPENDS the re-emitted corner rules — the module's own test drives `explicitRadiusCss` in isolation, so deleting the append put the collision straight back with every test green. (2) **My payload scan read ONE payload twice**: the two container payload lines are BYTE IDENTICAL, so `match` + `indexOf` answered the same position for both and a mutation deleting the other one's `style` survived the entire suite. `matchAll` and an index assertion. (3) Nothing asserted the stored patch reaches `buildAndPublishPages` at all.
- **AND MY OWN FIX FOR (3) ANCHORED ON THE DECLARATION.** `buildAndPublishPages(env, {` matches `async function buildAndPublishPages(env, { brief, spec, …` too, whose destructuring braces close 190 characters later — the `confirmSubmitter` failure this file has already recorded twice. Anchored on `await`.
- **TWO EXISTING GUARDS WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE, both pinned to spellings.** One matched the exact `_meta` key list `'site_look','site_tokens','site_logo'` and failed the moment a fourth concern was added beside it; the other matched `writeTheme(payload.theme, { dropRadius: wantsRadius })` and failed on a second option. **The logo one then took two more drafts to get right**, and the drafts are the lesson: widened to "every read naming `site_look`" it flagged the router's context read, widened to "every read naming `site_tokens`" it flagged the look-edit lane — **both correct code**, because neither builds a payload. There is no precise textual discriminator for "this read reaches the container", so it is asserted of the two variables that carry it. A wrong universal is a false alarm on correct code, which this file already says is worse than the miss.
- **PROVEN AGAINST THE REAL COMPILED BUNDLE, in `site-build`, at $0.** A CSS change is invisible to `tsc`, to vite, to the lint and to every unit test — the grey charts and the see-through modals — and a hand-written proxy proves only that a proxy works. So the ten new checks run the REAL `build-server.mjs` against the REAL template and read the REAL Tailwind output: a rule axis (`stroke-width: 2.5`) lands, the theme's own answer for it is gone, a property axis lands, an unusable patch neither fails the build nor reaches the stylesheet, and **the collision holds — a radius and a pill asked for together both survive.** Measured against `broadsheet`, whose own answers are the OPPOSITES of everything the patch names, so nothing can pass on a build where the patch did nothing.
  - **The collision check counts against the radius-only build rather than matching a pattern**, because `9999px` can legitimately come from Tailwind's own `rounded-full` if a page uses one — a bare match would be vacuous. The same build with and without the axis is coupled to nothing in the fixtures.
  - **Both halves proved to DISCRIMINATE, not assumed.** Reverting the merge fails 3 of the new checks; reverting the re-emit fails exactly 1, with the honest message *"0 with the axis, 0 without — the strip ate it"*. `site-build` 90 → 100.
- **THE TWO VOCABULARIES COULD SAY THE SAME THING, and one pair did.** `site-tokens.mjs` and this module compose SEPARATE sentences that land in the same note block, and both called their thing **"borders"** — so a build that changed the border COLOUR and the border WEIGHT printed *"Changed the borders."* twice, about two different things. `primary`/`buttons` and `input`/`inputs` were the same trap one step quieter. A colour now says it is one (`border colour`, `button colour`, `input colour`) and the axes say what they are (`border weight`, `overall spacing`), asserted by a guard **derived across both modules** — the next name added to either list has no way of knowing what the other one already says.
- **AND `border` IS ON BOTH LISTS, which is a routing problem as well as a wording one.** Here it is a colour, there a weight — so *"make the borders thicker"* can land in the colour slot, be refused for not being a colour, and come back as *"ask again with a hex code like #ffcc00"*, which is advice that cannot work. The tool now appends a pointer to the other slot, **derived from the overlap** rather than naming `border`, so a name that gains a twin later is disambiguated without anybody remembering.
- **THE FIRST SWEEP OF THAT GUARD PROVED NOTHING, AND ONLY THE SURVIVOR SHOWED IT.** Reverting ONE side of the clash is not a clash — with the token saying "border colour", an axis saying "borders" is still distinct — so that mutant was **inert**, and the one that "was caught" died against a wording pin in `site-tokens.test.mjs` rather than against the new guard. **The cross-module check had never fired.** Re-run with an axis taking a name a token really uses today: 7/7, including two axes colliding with each other. Ninth recorded inert mutant, and the first where the inert one was the useful signal.
- 2462 unit tests, **43/43 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline, `site-build` 100/100. **Not proven live** — the Anthropic account is empty, so no customer build has run against it.

## A route audit, and what it found (2026-08-12)

**Eight parallel auditors over the ~62 route matchers in `worker.js` — dispatch, auth, ownership, the builder lanes, client drift, money, the public edge, error hygiene — then an adversarial verifier on every medium-or-worse finding. 48 raw, 39 unique, 14 confirmed, 0 refuted.** All 14 are fixed. **0 of 14 refuted is a smell by this file's own rule** (a skeptic that confirms everything is not a skeptic), so six were re-checked by hand against the source, chosen for being cheap to falsify — all six held.

- **FIVE OF THE SEVEN HIGHS WERE A LIVE SERVER FEATURE THE PRODUCT COULD NOT REACH**, which is this file's most-recorded shape arriving five times in one audit. **Version history** was forced Off by a stale `DEAD_PANELS` entry four days after its route, its archive and its rewired panel all shipped — four lines under a comment saying to flip a name out the moment its route exists. **The only Restore button anybody could reach** was the localStorage one, which prints "↩ Restored to…" and cannot touch a published site, so **there was no working restore path anywhere in the product**. **The Members card** was clickable, promised "Accounts that sign up in your app", had no branch in the cloud dispatch and fell through to `else siteInbox(site)` — the form-submissions modal — while list/role/suspend/reinstate/remove sat live and tested on the server (there is a real panel now). **`readEdit` dropped `remove` for the logo layer**, returning early for every non-`page` layer before reading it, so "drop the logo" was answered with "attach a logo" — the exact inversion the flag was added the day before to prevent. And **`sitePathOf` still required the `src/routes/` prefix the lanes stopped sending**: the FIFTH instance of one path shape being read by code anchored on the other, in the copy that lives in the browser.
- **`tPub` APPEARS ONCE IN 12,604 LINES — used, never declared.** Every compile failure on `/text` was a `ReferenceError` 500 instead of the friendly 422 that branch exists for. Same family as `vidRefN` and `du.id`, and **the existing scanner cannot see it**: that one walks FORWARD from a declaration, and this name has none. The new guard is narrower and derived — every `compileMsg(X, …)` must name something the file declares — because six sibling branches each have their own local (`pub`, `pPub`, `aPub`, `cutPub`, `rPub`, `out`), which is exactly why the wrong one is easy to write.
- **EVERY ADDON OVERCHARGED, and it was measured rather than argued.** The bill was `pageCredits(design) + pageCredits(pages)`; `pageCredits` is variadic precisely so several calls land on one bill with ONE rounding, and its own comment says adding separately-rounded results "would charge twice for the rounding". Driven through the real module: **20 credits summed against 19 priced together, and 2 against 1 on two tiny calls.** The guard asserts the property through the module, not the spelling.
- **THE PICTURE LAYER GENERATED PHOTOGRAPHS AND BILLED NOBODY.** It charged tokens only, so a picture costing $0.15 of real fal spend — ~19 credits, the exact charge the BUILD path applies for the identical image — was free. **Latent only because the fal balance has never been funded**; the day it is, "add three photos of the shop" is ~$0.45 out and ~1 credit in. The affordability gate was equally inert: the balance was read once and never decremented, so the per-picture check could not bind across a batch. Both fixed, and the photographs ride the same variadic call as the tokens so they round once together.
- **A NEON OUTAGE BILLED THE CUSTOMER FOR IT.** Only the name-taken branch refunded; every other provisioning failure and the schema-apply failure answered 502 and kept the settled schema charge — 9 credits cold Sonnet, 15 Opus, against a grant of 20. Every sibling refusal on that route refunds, with the stated reason that the caller is left with literally nothing, and infrastructure being down is an our-fault stage by `ourFault`'s own list.
- **`body.schema` SKIPPED THE ENTIRE CREDIT GATE.** The deposit and `buildFloor` live inside `if (!body.schema)` and provisioning runs after it either way, so any signed-in caller posting their own schema reached `ensureSiteBackend` with no check at all. Page generation was still protected by its own floor — **what was free is the NEON PROJECT**, a capped billed resource against a platform-wide cap of 100. Gated on `MIN_CREDITS` rather than `buildFloor`, deliberately: no designer call happens on that path, so charging for one would refuse a build costing strictly less than an ordinary one, and both `confirm smoke` and `member smoke` build this way on a fresh account.
- **ONE FAILED HAIKU CALL BOUGHT A ~25-CREDIT REBUILD.** The data lane returned `escalate: true` on a model throw, against the rule every sibling follows and the look layer states outright. **The audit named one lane and the text lane had it identically** — both fixed, and all four lanes now hand a `send` failure to one `modelDown`, which answers 503 with the provider's type and the billing flag. A billing outage answered "try again in a moment" is the one failure nothing retries past.
- **GAME BUILDS KEPT THE CHARGE WHEN OUR CONTAINER DIED** — charged the moment each generation returned, before any compile, with no refund on any branch of either route and `/api/refund` covering fal jobs only. And **the fix's first draft had the bug it was fixing**: `cost` is declared inside the try, so the refund in the catch would have been a `ReferenceError` on the one path that gives credits back. Hoisted, and asserted.
- **THE PAIR BUG, THIRD PLACE THIS MONTH.** The owner-write member gate asked `def.access`, which `normalizeSchema` stamps `"collect"` on any pair-declared table — and the design tool tells the model pairs are the escape hatch and to leave `access` out. So an owner POST into a member table wrote a row with `owner_id` NULL: the orphan that 409 exists to prevent. Fixed by asking the write axis, and **the echoed label went with it**, so the Data panel stops calling a public menu "submissions" and `canAdd` reads a `memberRows` boolean instead of re-deriving the rule in the browser.
- **THE MUTATION SWEEP FOUND FIVE REAL GAPS OF MINE, all in the un-importable files** — `worker.js` and `public/chat.js`, for the twelfth recorded time. And **two of my own mutants were wrong about themselves**: one was inert (a non-`/g` substitution hit the first `upstreamKind` in the file rather than the one in `modelDown`), and one slipped because **the guard's window ended at the very status it was asserting**, so changing 503 to 422 moved the window's end past it and everything still matched. An anchor that moves with the thing it asserts proves nothing — the overlapping-window bug, in the guard written to prevent a different one.
- 2380 tests, **24/24 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline. **Nothing proven live** — the Anthropic account is empty.

## A design failure with NOTHING to say, against a Worker that had just passed (2026-08-12)

**`edit smoke` came back 2 passed / 2 failed, both descending from one `503 stage:"design"` with `upstream: null, upstreamType: null, billing` absent.** `git diff ff61b44 7e58eec` is **docs and the test file only** — the Worker was byte-identical to the run that had passed 42/43 eleven minutes earlier, which is what rules out a regression without spending a run to find out.

- **BOTH DIAGNOSTIC FIELDS ARE READ OFF A RESPONSE BODY, so a `fetch` that THROWS leaves both null and the reply says nothing at all.** `upstream` is `e.status` and `upstreamType` comes from `upstreamKind(e.detail)`, and `anthropicMessages` sets both only inside `if (!r.ok)`. A network fault, a DNS blip, an aborted connection **and a bug of ours anywhere in that try** (the try also spans `topUpSeed`, `schemaSettlement` and `creditBack`) all produce the identical empty answer. **The numeric status was added for exactly this lesson in the first place and did not go far enough — this is the second time.**
- **THE FIX IS THE ERROR'S CLASS, which is the repo's own precedent rather than a new idea.** `kind: String(e.name)` — a `TypeError` is the fetch, anything else is ours. The message stays withheld from every class **but `ReferenceError`**, whose message is always `"<name> is not defined"`: a programmer bug, never request data, and the single most valuable string this repo has ever put in a response — it is how the `OWN_ZONES` outage was found after every custom-domain call had been throwing for days.
- **RULED OUT BY READING, so no run was spent on it:** `ratesFor` logs and falls back to the dearest rate rather than throwing, `pageCost` cannot throw, `topUpSeed` catches its own send and every other step in it is pure, `anthropicMessages` exists, and `designSiteSchema` returns `input: null` rather than throwing when there is no tool call. The remaining candidates are the fetch and `creditBack`, and the class separates them.
- **THE RUN PAID FOR TWO BUILDS BECAUSE THE REUSE COULD NOT FIND ITS OWN FIXTURE.** The retry mutated `slug` to `-r1` in place and *that* is what got kept, while the scan only ever looked at the bare `FIXTURE_SLUG` — so **one unlucky generation disabled the saving permanently**, and the scan then found the claimed-but-empty husk of the failed attempt and fell through to building. Shipped and broken the same night as the saving itself. **One expression produces the names now and both readers use it** (`slugFor(attempt)`), so the builder and the finder cannot disagree, and the scan tries every name the loop can produce, newest first.
- **A REUSE THAT SILENTLY STOPS WORKING READS EXACTLY LIKE AN ORDINARY RUN**, at ~25 credits a time, so each candidate now logs what it answered. Same class as everything else here: a failure that cannot name itself.
- **THE TWO-LIMITS BUG, still live one call site from the comment describing it.** `ok()` truncates at 900 and the build assertion handed it `.slice(0, 200)` — so the new `kind` was invisible on the one line written to explain the failure. And the per-attempt line now prints `stage`/`upstream`/`kind` itself, because the assertion below reports ONCE for a loop that runs TWICE and two different faults read as one.
- 2353 tests, **3/3 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline.

## The router answered a deletion with a question (2026-08-12)

**Found by `edit smoke` on the first funded run: 37 passed, 2 failed.** Asked to *"Remove the gallery page"*, the deployed router came back `intent=ask layer=undefined remove=undefined page=undefined` — a paragraph in reply to an instruction. The second failure (`escalate:"no-change"`) is the same bug one step downstream.

- **THE CODE WAS FINE, WHICH IS WHY NOTHING BELOW THE ROUTER COULD HAVE CAUGHT IT.** `readEdit` resolves the page against the site's real list and honours `remove === true` exactly as written; the unit suite was green throughout. The failure was entirely in what the model is TOLD, and the only thing that could see it was a live call.
- **THE MISSING SENTENCE IS NOT ABOUT DELETION, IT IS ABOUT CONFIRMATION.** The `edit` clause said "something taken away", the `page` layer said "take one out" about a SECTION, and nothing anywhere told the model not to check first. **Asking "are you sure?" is the natural thing a helpful model does with a destructive instruction, and it is the one answer this interface cannot accept** — there is no yes button, so the round dead-ends and reads as the builder refusing to work. The same shape as the clarify dead-end recorded 2026-08-09, arriving through a different door.
- **Three additions, each mutation-checked**: `ask` may never be a confirmation; the `edit` intent names a whole-page deletion explicitly; the `page` layer says a deletion belongs to it. Asserted on the assembled tool description, because a prompt fix with nothing holding it is one a later edit drops silently.
- **THE CONSERVATISM UNDERNEATH IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED, and that is asserted separately.** `remove` is still `=== true` and nothing merely truthy, still only applies to a page that really exists, and the merge still refuses the home page and any page another links to. The fix is about the model REACHING the layer, never about what happens once it does — everywhere else in that file unclear resolves to WORK, and removal is the one verb where that is inverted.
- **A mutation could not find its own anchor**, because I had split the phrase across a string concatenation with markdown asterisks the rest of the file does not use. The check reported "0 failed" for a guard that was actually real — a false all-clear, which is the more dangerous direction. Rewritten in the file's own caps style, contiguous, and all three fire.
- **What this run also proved, first time live:** the `rules` layer end to end — *"THE DATABASE REALLY REFUSES A STRANGER NOW"*, a real Postgres policy verified by reading as an outsider and then put back. Plus a rename reaching the published page over the wire, and a row changed/removed/undone in Postgres.
- **`text`, `picture` and `logo` are still unproven live** — `edit smoke` drives none of them, which corrects a claim made earlier the same day that this run would cover `text` and `picture`.

## "Hey, this is my logo, put it there" (2026-08-12, owner's call)

**The eighth edit layer, and the owner's own words for it.** A picker in the uploads panel was proposed and turned down for the obvious better thing: attach the logo, say what it is, it goes in the header. **The attachment IS which picture** — nothing to match, nothing to remember — which is what makes the whole layer free of a model call.

- **`SiteHeader` TOOK `brand: string` AND THERE WAS NO IMAGE SLOT ANYWHERE IN THE FRAME.** Every site the platform has ever made showed the business name as text, so a business with a logo could not use it on the site we built them. **The hard half was never the slot** — uploads are stored under a hash of their own contents, so "use the one with the black lettering" has no filename to resolve against, which is why this sat unbuilt rather than half-built.
- **BAKED INTO THE BUNDLE, NOT INJECTED INTO THE HEAD, and only a real build could have proved it.** `site-meta.mjs` puts `site-slug` in the head at PUBLISH time, which is fine for a value read when a fetch happens. A logo is RENDERED, and every route is prerendered to HTML BEFORE the publish step runs — so a component reading that meta tag renders without it on the server and with it in the browser: a hydration mismatch, and a header that visibly flips from the name to the logo on every page load. The container writes `src/site-brand.ts` instead, the way `writeFonts` already writes `src/fonts.ts`. Verified on the real prerendered output: `<img src="/u/…/logo.svg" alt="Sharp Fade Barbers">` is in the snapshot.
- **THE NAME IS THE `alt`, and that is what makes the fallback safe.** Most small-business logos are lockups that already contain the name, so showing both prints it twice — and the alt keeps it for a screen reader, for a crawler, and for the case that matters most: the image failing, where the header degrades to exactly what every site shows today rather than to nothing. `max-w` is not decoration either — an unbounded wordmark pushes the nav off the right-hand edge, and the person who notices is a visitor who cannot find the booking link.
- **ITS OWN `_meta` KEY, NEVER A FIELD ON `site_look`.** `mergeLook` rebuilds its output from `EDIT_FIELDS` alone, so anything else stored on that object is DROPPED by the next look edit — a customer changing a colour would silently lose their logo. Stored beside `site_tokens`, which is a separate key for the same reason. Guarded by asserting `logo` is absent from `EDIT_FIELDS`.
- **BOTH PUBLISH PATHS HAD TO CARRY IT OR A REVISE WOULD TAKE IT OFF.** The container writes `site-brand.ts` on EVERY build — it must, or one site's logo leaks onto the next — so a path that does not send the stored value sends nothing, and nothing means empty. Without `priorLogo`, a customer who attached a logo and then asked for any page change would have watched it disappear, with no error and nothing to point at.
- **THE RULE THAT AN ATTACHMENT SKIPS THE ROUTER IS REVERSED, and the reason it could be is the useful part.** That skip was justified by *"on a revise both non-build outcomes are already closed, so the call could only ever answer build"* — a true statement **about the layers that existed**, which this change makes false. A picture plus "this is my logo" now has a real cheap answer, and a skip would have made the new rung unreachable for the one message shape it exists to serve. **The half that survives is unchanged**: `attached` still closes off `ask` at the router, because answering a file with prose drops the file on the floor.
  - **The guard on that rule had been wrong TWICE, both times by pinning the exact spelling of the condition.** It now asserts the durable property — **nothing bypasses the router**, because a bypass is how a rung silently stops existing — and holds the narrower file-is-never-answered-with-prose rule as its own separate assertion, at the router where it lives.
- **SVG IS REFUSED, and it is the refusal most likely to annoy somebody** — a logo is the single most likely thing a business has as an SVG, because that is what a designer hands them. `/u/` serves `content-disposition: inline` from the site's own origin and an SVG is a document that can carry `<script>`, so accepting one is stored XSS on a customer's domain, which `nosniff` does not help with because the type would be declared honestly. `site-uploads.mjs` already refuses it; one rule beats two. The message says what to send instead rather than just saying no. **An SVG dressed as a PNG is refused by the BYTES**, since the declared media type is whatever the caller wrote.
- **A REFUSAL IS NEVER ESCALATED.** The rung above is a full revise, which cannot put a logo in a header either — it would spend ~27 credits rewriting pages and end with the same missing logo, which reads as the builder ignoring what was asked.
- **NOTHING IS SAVED UNTIL THE IMAGE IS STORED and nothing is published until it is saved.** The other order leaves a site pointing at an address that 404s — a broken-image glyph in the header of every page, which is worse than the text it replaced. **A failed publish says the logo IS saved**, because it is: the next publish of that site carries it, and saying otherwise has them send it again for nothing.
- **A REMOVAL IS ASKED FIRST AND NEEDS NO IMAGE**, or "take the logo off" is answered with "attach a logo" — the opposite of what was asked. It rides the router's existing `remove` field, the same one that decides a page deletion, and is a REAL boolean so nothing merely truthy can strip a customer's logo.
- **My own new guard false-alarmed on a sibling's correct wording**, searching the whole tool schema for "only when layer is page" and matching the `page` PATH field, which is right to say it. Windowed to the `remove` field. Same class as everything else in this file: a check that cries wolf on correct code.
- 2317 unit tests; proven by building a real site through the real container **with** and **without** a logo and looking at both.

## A site the server had already deleted could never leave the workspace (2026-08-12)

**Reported by the owner: the ✕ on a project card kept answering *"Couldn't take that site down just now — it's still live. Try again in a moment."*** Both halves of that sentence were false. Measured: **no `site_backends` row for the slug at all**, and `pulse-fitness.gofarther.app` answering **404** — the site was already gone, and retrying could never do anything.

- **THE OWNERSHIP ROW IS THE ONLY THING A DELETE CAN AUTHORISE AGAINST**, so a site with no row answers 404 *by construction* — which is exactly the state of every site that has already been taken down, and of every build that failed before claiming its slug. The client required a 2xx before dropping the local card, so those cards were **permanently unremovable** and the only advice on offer was to retry forever.
- **404 NOW CLEARS THE CARD, AND THIS IS NOT THE 2026-08-08 BUG RETURNING.** That one dropped the record on a 404 from a route that *had never existed*, so every delete silently failed while the site kept running. This route exists and its 404 has exactly one meaning. **It is said out loud rather than done quietly** — "we took your site down" and "there was nothing left of it" are different facts and the customer is entitled to which one.
- **403 STILL REFUSES**, deliberately: it is the one status meaning somebody ELSE owns that slug, and clearing the card on it would hide an ownership bug rather than report one. Every other failure keeps the card, which was already right.
- **THREE OF FOUR MUTANTS SURVIVED THE FIRST SWEEP, ALL THE OVERLAPPING-WINDOW BUG.** A window of 400 characters after the 404 check ran into the 403 branch, so deleting the 404 branch's own `alert` was satisfied by its neighbour's; the 403 assertion reached the failure branch's `return;` the same way. The three branches are carved by the NEXT condition's index now, so nothing is proved by the branch below it. **This repo's most repeated own-goal, and the third instance this session.**
- Verified with a live read of `site_backends` and a real request to the subdomain — not inferred from the code. 2350 tests, **4/4 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline.

## PAGE DELETION IS PROVEN LIVE, END TO END (2026-08-12)

**42 passed, 1 failed, and the deletion journey is complete**: routed to the page layer with `remove`, the page found by name, the guard's refusal naming what still linked to it, the links taken out, the deletion going through, **the removed page answering 404 over the wire** — and both deletions costing nothing to generate. Eight live runs to get here, and the last four found bugs that were never in the model.

- **THE ONE REMAINING FAILURE WAS THE CHECK, FOR THE FOURTH TIME.** The addon answered `ok: true` with `changed: ["index.tsx"]`, nothing removed, nothing kept, and `problems: ["These pages were linked to and do not exist…"]` — because the cheap lane had ALREADY deleted the page, so it found links pointing at something gone and took them out. Correct, and reported in the field it reports problems in; the assertion listed `removed`, `kept` and `msg` and not that one.
- **THE SCOREBOARD OF THE EIGHT RUNS, because the pattern is the lesson.** Two were real prompt bugs (a confirmation question, then the wrong lane). **Four were ours and silent**: the route never forwarded `remove`, `routeOf` could not read a stored path, the check asked to delete the home page, and the check raced the deploy. **Two were checks calibrated against a live bug** — fixing the bug turned them red. Only the first two were about what the model answered.

## The deletion works, and the check was asserting a flow the product does not have (2026-08-12)

**First run that did not race the deploy, and the answer arrived in one line:** `ok a deletion routes to the page layer with 'remove'`, then `422 error:"kept"` — *"I left /gallery — /, /book, /work still link to it. Ask me to take the link out first."* **The page was FOUND and refused by the guard**, which is the `routeOf` fix working end to end. The look lane and the rules lane both passed clean.

- **`edit smoke` HAD BEEN RACING THE DEPLOY ON EVERY RUN.** It slept a fixed 90 seconds while the deploy takes about three minutes — **measured on the first gated run: 3m26s** — so every previous run tested the Worker mid-replacement with two container images rebuilding underneath it. That is where `internal error; reference = …` (Cloudflare saying the container could not start), a `422 write` that passed next time and a rename that failed to compile and then did not all came from. **Infrastructure churn was being read as the code being wrong, all night.** It polls for this commit's deploy now and falls through rather than failing, so it is never worse than the sleep. It cannot use `workflow_run` like its two siblings: that carries no path filter and would run on every deploy at ~50 credits.
- **THE `<= 2` PROXY WAS CALIBRATED AGAINST THE BROKEN `routeOf` AND WENT RED WHEN IT WAS FIXED.** The addon reverts a changed page that does not mention a route it just added — and with `routeOf` answering `""` for everything, the new-route list was always empty and almost every change got reverted. With the fix the nav link lands in a SHARED header, so all three pages legitimately carry it. A check calibrated against a bug asserts the bug.
- **THE TWO-STEP JOURNEY WAS NEVER DRIVEN.** The design is explicit — the page layer refuses a page other pages link to, and the addon lane is the rung that takes the links out — and the check asserted a one-step deletion, so a correct refusal read as a failure. It now accepts a refusal that NAMES what still links, and then drives the sentence a customer would actually type next: links out, then the cheap deletion, which must go through for nothing.
- **What the live run proved about the addon, and it is worth watching:** asked to remove the page AND take the link out, it changed all three linking pages and removed no page, for 27 credits. The customer is then one cheap step from done — which the check now verifies rather than assuming.

## The whole `page` edit layer was dead, on a path-string mismatch (2026-08-12)

**With the wire fixed, a deletion arrived correctly — `layer=page`, `remove` true, `page=/gallery` — and answered `no-page`.** Diagnosed from the source with no further runs, on the owner's instruction.

- **`cleanPath` STRIPS `src/routes/` AND `routeOf` REQUIRED IT.** Every stored page path is bare (`gallery.tsx`) — the container puts the prefix back when it writes the files — and `routeOf` was anchored `^src/routes/(.+)\.tsx$`, so it answered `""` for **every page on every site**. Proven by driving the real producer into the real consumer rather than by reading.
- **NOT ONLY DELETION — THE WHOLE LAYER.** The page layer finds its target with `eSrc.find((p) => routeOf(p.path) === wantRoute)`, which could never match, so **every page edit answered `no-page` and escalated to a ~25-credit addon**. The addon lane's nav-link logic (`[...added, ...gone].map(routeOf).filter(Boolean)`) and `addonReply`'s page names went with it.
- **THE FIXTURE IS WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT.** `test/site-addon.test.mjs` builds every page as `{ path: "src/routes/" + path }` — a shape the pipeline never produces — so 72 tests exercised a spelling that does not exist in production. **A fixture more capable than reality hides exactly the bug it is there to catch**, the `setTotp` lesson in a path string.
- **THE GUARD IS A COMPOSITION, NOT A SECOND FIXTURE.** `validatePages` decides the stored path and `routeOf` reads it back; two hand-written fixtures agreed with each other and with neither. The new check drives the real producer into the real consumer and asserts every page that can be STORED can be ADDRESSED — and a mutation to either side goes red.
- **Fourth path-shape mismatch in one session**, and they are all the same shape: the build reports `src/routes/x.tsx`, the addon and the store report `x.tsx`, and any code anchored on one is blind to the other. `routeOfAdded` in the live check had it, and now `routeOf` did.
- 2352 tests, **4/4 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — held deliberately, so the next run is spent once.

## Page deletion was never wired to the client, and five prompt fixes chased it (2026-08-12)

**`/api/site/route` returned `intent`, `answer`, `layer`, `page`, `question`, `cost`, `usage` — and never `remove`.** `readEdit` decides it, `public/chat.js` reads `d.remove === true` and posts it on: **both halves correct, the wire cut.** So page deletion has been unreachable in the product since it shipped, and `rmRoute.remove` was `undefined` on every live run whatever the model answered.

- **THE COMMENT DIRECTLY ABOVE IT DOCUMENTS THE IDENTICAL BUG FOR `layer` AND `page`**, found the same way on 2026-08-11 and fixed by adding exactly those two fields. `remove` is decided by the same function and read by the same client and was not added with them. **The wiring layer for the ELEVENTH recorded time.**
- **FIVE PROMPT REWRITES WERE SPENT ON A SYMPTOM, and that is the lesson.** From outside, *"the model did not set the flag"* and *"we did not forward the flag"* are the same `undefined`. Three of tonight's rewrites were real — `intent` moved `ask` → `addon` → `edit`, and `intent` IS forwarded, so those were measured correctly. The two that targeted `remove` itself could not have changed anything observable. **Before rewording a prompt because a field came back empty, check that the field can arrive.**
- **THE GUARD IS DERIVED FROM WHAT `readEdit` REALLY RETURNS**, not from a list of the three known today — it drives the function, collects the keys, and requires the route to forward each. A list is what missed the third field when the first two were fixed. Paired with a check that the client still READS it, since either end alone passes while the wire is cut.
