- **AN EXISTING GUARD WENT RED ON A CORRECT CHANGE**, for the size of its window: it bounded the response literal at 1500 bytes and this repo puts its reasoning in comments, so a well-documented field addition failed a check about how much CODE is in there. It measures the comment-stripped length now.
- **The structural fix was BUILT AND REVERTED, deliberately.** A `pageAction: ["edit","delete"]` enum was written and tested on the theory that the model will fill an enum where it will not raise a flag — then reverted before merging, because shipping it alongside the wire fix means the next run cannot say which one worked. It is one commit away if the wire alone does not do it.
- 2351 tests, **5/6 mutations caught**; the survivor is the `intent === "edit"` gate, which is inert today (`readEdit` sets `remove` on no other path) and is noted in the source rather than deleted or falsely asserted.

## The deletion moved from a question to the wrong lane, and the tie-break was why (2026-08-12)

**Two live runs, two different wrong answers, one buried false clause.** After the prompt fix that stopped `Remove the gallery page` being answered with a confirmation question, the very next run answered **`intent=addon`** — a full page-generation call that came back `no-change`, so the customer pays and the page stays.

- **THE LAST SENTENCE OF A TOOL DESCRIPTION IS THE STRONGEST ONE IN IT.** The `intent` field ended *"WHEN YOU CANNOT TELL, ANSWER \"addon\". It can do everything an edit can and an edit cannot do what it does."* **That claim is FALSE for a removal** — nothing in the addon lane can take a page off a site — and it sat eight lines below the correct rule that a deletion is an edit. The model read the tie-break. The exception now sits AT the tie-break rather than above it, and is asserted both by name and by position.
- **AND THE THIRD POSITION, ONE FIELD OVER.** With the tie-break fixed, the next run was `intent=edit layer=page page=/gallery remove=undefined` — lane right, layer right, page right, boolean refused, against a message that is this field's OWN FIRST EXAMPLE. `remove`'s page clause CLOSED on *"Getting it wrong the other way takes a page off their site"*: a warning against the action, as the final word. Both cautions are kept; only which one closes has moved, and it now closes on what happens if it is OMITTED — the page stays and the customer is told the change was made. **Fourth model, across two call sites, to decline this flag; if it fails again the answer is structural (an enum or its own layer) rather than more words.**
- **The seed fix worked**: `"services" came back seeded` and the run reached all 39 checks instead of dying at 7. The restructure paid immediately — the rules lane went 8/8 including *THE DATABASE REALLY REFUSES A STRANGER NOW*, and the addon 3/3, all of which the previous two runs threw away.
- **AN ASSERTION THAT COULD NEVER FAIL, found by reading a live log rather than the code.** `the change really is in the database` tested `JSON.stringify(rows).includes("26")` — and `created_at` is a **2026** timestamp, so it was true of every row of every table on every run. It reported `ok` on a run where the edit answered **422 `write`** and applied nothing. Managed columns are stripped before the check now. An assertion that cannot fail is worse than none: it is the one that says a broken write worked.
- **AND THE ANSWER, ONE RUN LATER: THE ROUTER WAS RIGHT AND THE CHECK WAS WRONG.** The detail printed `asked to remove "home" (gallery.tsx) · pages sent ["/","/book","/manage","/work","/"]`. `routeOfAdded` was anchored on `^src/routes/(.+)\.tsx$` — the shape the BUILD reports — and the ADDON lane reports a bare `gallery.tsx`, so it matched nothing and fell through to its `"/"` fallback. **The check therefore asked the router to delete the HOME page**, which the merge refuses by design, so `remove` unset and `page: "/"` is the correct answer to a question that should never have been asked. Two runs blamed the router for a guard working.
- **A HELPER WHOSE FAILURE MODE IS "RETURN THE HOME PAGE" is the worst possible default for one used to name a page for deletion**, and that is the durable lesson rather than the regex. It answers `""` now, callers filter it, and the deletion checks SKIP with a printed reason rather than pointing at something real. Both spellings are accepted, because assuming one cost two runs.
- **The rename passed on that run**, so the four `compile` failures were variance too — the widened detail stays, since the cause was never in the log.
- **Still open from that run, neither diagnosed:** the data edit answered `422 write` (`applied.length === 0`) on a table whose row was removed and restored successfully seconds later; and the look edit answered `422 compile` whose `detail` leads with Vite's benign `new URL("../", import.meta.url)` **warning** from `main.tsx`, so the real error is past the truncation — the message a customer is shown carries a warning rather than the cause.

## The designer omits its own required `seed`, so a Haiku call fills the gap (2026-08-12, owner's call)

**Owner picked the cheap top-up over asking the designer again.** `seed` is a REQUIRED field on `design_schema` and the model left it out on two consecutive builds, so sites published with an empty price list and a booking form whose Service select had zero options — permanently, since nothing can write to a `display` table after the build — and the customer was told `✅ Built`.

- **A BUILD THE DESIGNER GOT RIGHT MAKES NO CALL AT ALL**, and that is the property that decides whether this is affordable rather than a tax on every build. `seedGaps` returns nothing when every display table already has rows, and `topUpSeed` returns before touching the network.
- **`seedGaps` ASKS `resolveAccess`, NOT THE PRESET NAME** — the same one question the seeder asks, or the top-up fills a table the seeder then refuses and the call was spent on nothing. **An empty array is a GAP, not a filled table**: that is one of the three silent paths that caused the live failure, and reading it the other way would leave the exact shape of the bug unfixed.
- **PRICED APART AND SETTLED TOGETHER.** This is Haiku while the designer may be Sonnet or Opus, so the two usages can never be merged into one object — the `sumUsage` bug this file already paid for. It runs BEFORE the settlement so the one deposit trues up against both, `pageCredits` prices each at its own rate and rounds once, and `schemaSettlement` now takes a LIST. A mutant settling `parts[0]` alone survived the first sweep: the designer's cost was billed and the top-up's was free.
- **IT CAN NEVER FAIL A BUILD.** Every failure — no gaps, a throw, an unreadable answer, an answer about tables nobody asked about — returns no rows and no usage, which leaves the site in exactly the state it would have been in without the function. A failed call reports no usage, so nothing is billed for it.
- **THE ANSWER IS NARROWED, NOT TRUSTED**: model output on its way into a real customer's database. A table nobody asked about is dropped (a `collect` table's rows are fabricated customer submissions, and this must not become the second place that could produce them), an undeclared column is dropped, and a non-scalar goes with it — an object in a text column renders to a visitor as `[object Object]`. The designer's own rows win a collision: it read the whole brief, this call saw a summary.
- **Two guards went red on correct changes, both pinned to spellings** — `let designed = null;` (a sibling declared on the same line) and `schemaSettlement(schemaUsage, SITE_BUILD_FEE)` (a legitimate second argument). Re-anchored on the properties. Same own-goal as everywhere else in this file, twice in one change.
- 2349 tests, **24/25 mutations** caught across two sweeps from verified-green baselines; the survivor was the settlement gap above, now closed. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — no build has run against it yet.

## A `display` table declared as a PAIR was never seeded (2026-08-12)

**`edit smoke` re-ran to prove the deletion fix and stopped four checks earlier**: a barber shop built clean — `page=app`, 4 files, 55 credits — and `"services" came back seeded -> 0 rows`. **Not cosmetic.** Nothing can write to a `display` table after the build, so an unseeded one is an empty list forever, and a booking form whose required Service select reads it renders with **zero options** — nobody can submit. That is the precise failure build-time seeding was written to fix on 2026-07-28, reintroduced by the read/write axes two days earlier.

- **`seedSiteRows` ASKED THE PRESET NAME, and the axes made the preset name optional.** `t.access !== "display"` — but `display` is a shorthand for `{read: "public", write: "none"}`, and `normalizeSchema`'s allow-list STAMPS `access: "collect"` on anything that did not declare a recognised preset. So a pair-declared display table arrived carrying a level nobody wrote and was skipped **silently**. `applySiteSchema` one file over already asks `resolveAccess(t)`; the seeder was never taught.
- **THE PROMPT STEERS THE DESIGNER STRAIGHT INTO IT**, which is what makes this more than an oversight. `design_schema`'s `access` description ends *"THESE FIVE ARE SHORTHANDS FOR A read/write PAIR. When none of them is the shape you need, set `read` and `write` instead **and leave this out**."* The tool asks for exactly the form the seeder ignored.
- **THE DISPLAY PAIR AND NOTHING WIDER.** `accessNameFor(resolveAccess(t)) === "display"`, so every preset-declared table behaves byte-identically — asserted across all five, not reasoned, because this decides what gets fabricated into a customer's database. `admin` is also write-none and is still NOT seeded; whether it should be is a separate decision, and a mutant widening the gate to `write !== "none"` dies. So does one widening it to `read === "public"`, which would invent a marketplace's listings.
- **`accessLabel` WAS STILL WRONG ONE LAYER UP, and it is the function written to fix this class.** It read the `access` field first, so on the stamped shape every downstream reader sees it answered *"collect"* for a public-read menu. It resolves first now: a preset keeps its familiar name, a pair that IS a preset gets that name, a cell with no shorthand says what it is. The lint's messages get the same correction free.
- **THE REASON COULD NOT BE READ, ONLY REASONED.** `skipped` lived solely in a `console.log`, and the run deletes its own site on the way out — so by the time the question was asked the evidence was gone and the answer came from a source audit rather than a glance. `seedSkipped` is on the build response now (bounded, `undefined` when empty so a clean build's response is byte-identical) and `edit smoke` prints it. **Sixth recorded instance of a failure that could not name itself**, and the mutant deleting the field dies against a source-read.
- **STATED HONESTLY: the hole is real and reachable by instruction, and it is NOT PROVEN to be what fired on that run.** Two candidates survive — the designer wrote no `seed` rows, or gave an `access` string outside the enum. The next run separates them without a second run, which is the whole point of the field above.
- **NOT CHANGED TONIGHT, DELIBERATELY:** the per-table `required: ["name", "access", "columns"]` contradicts that same description telling the model to omit `access`. Worth fixing; changing the highest-leverage prompt in the product would have put an untested variable into the one run being read.
- **KNOWN AND NOT FIXED:** `public/chat.js` reads `t.access` in four places (the Data panel's label, `canAdd`, the notify toggle, the collect filter), so a pair-declared table is mislabelled to the OWNER and offered a submissions-notification toggle on a table nothing can submit to. Harmless in effect; the fix needs a browser-safe copy of the rule, which is its own change with its own guard.
- **THE NEXT RUN REFUTED THE HYPOTHESIS, WHICH IS WHAT THE FIELD WAS FOR.** `seeded={} skipped=[]` — nothing was *rejected*, so the pair gate never fired: **the designer returned no usable `seed` at all** (absent, `{}`, or empty arrays are the only three silent paths). The fix above closes a real hole reachable by instruction and was **not** what fired. Recorded because a diagnostic that only ever confirms is not a diagnostic, and this one cost one run instead of a second source audit.
- **THAT IS A CUSTOMER-FACING DEFECT AND IT IS NOT FIXED.** `seed` is a REQUIRED field on `design_schema` and the designer omitted it on two consecutive builds after one that did not. Nothing notices, nothing recovers, and the site publishes with an empty price list and a booking form whose Service select has zero options — reported to the customer as `✅ Built`. The truncation guard cannot see it: a `max_tokens` stop throws, and these builds returned 200 with `page=app`. **`lang` was ruled out** — it is optional, displaces nothing, and was live during the run that seeded correctly.
- **ONE BUILD-LOTTERY OUTCOME WAS THROWING AWAY THIRTY-ODD CHECKS.** `edit smoke` returned on an unseeded table, so an empty menu killed the router, look, rules, addon and **page-deletion** checks — including the fix the run existed to prove, twice. Only the DATA lane needs a row to name; the rules lane names the TABLE. Now the data lane is skipped and everything else runs, the same "stub the broken page, keep the site" reasoning one layer up. **Deliberately not recorded as a skipped pass**: `ok()` counts, and a check silently green on a run where it never ran is the one failure mode a live harness must not have.
- 2337 tests, **9/9 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline.

## Every site declared itself English, and every site had the same icon (2026-08-12)

**Two of the ranked gaps from the build-vs-edit audit, and both were true of EVERY site the platform has ever published.** One piece because they live in ONE file: `index.html` is what Vite builds from and what every prerendered route derives its head from, the model never sees it, so the container is the only place either can happen. `builder/site-identity.mjs` is the decision half as a plain module — no filesystem, no HTTP — so all of it is tested outside the container.

- **`<html lang="en">` WAS HARDCODED IN THE TEMPLATE and `writeIndexHtml` rewrote only the `<title>`.** So a café in Lisbon and a peluquería in Madrid both published as English. **Not cosmetic: Chrome offers to translate a Spanish page into English when the page claims to be English**, a screen reader pronounces Spanish words with an English voice, and a search engine files the site under the wrong language. Invisible to everyone building the site and obvious to the person it is for.
- **DERIVED FROM THE BRIEF, NOT ASKED FOR.** `lang` is a field on `design_schema`: a person describing their business in Spanish is not going to be asked which language they want, the brief is the answer, and the designer is the one step that has read it. It is on `EDIT_FIELDS`, so `look` can change it, `mergeLook` keeps it when nobody asks, and `movedFields` reports it — one line, and the whole edit path follows.
- **A REFUSAL RATHER THAN A DEFAULT.** `normalizeLang` takes a subset of BCP-47 and answers null for everything else, and the caller keeps whatever the site had. That is what makes it safe to deploy: **every site built before today has no stored language**, so `applyIdentity` leaves the attribute alone rather than guessing — one deploy does not relabel the platform. Case is CANONICALISED (`PT-br` → `pt-BR`), because `zh-hans` is well-formed and matchers built on the convention miss it.
- **ONLY SHAPES THAT NEED NO ESCAPING ARE ACCEPTED, and that IS the security property.** The value goes into an HTML attribute; refusing anything that is not `ll`/`ll-Ssss`/`ll-RR`/`ll-419` means there is no escaping question to get wrong. A non-string is refused rather than stringified — `String(["es"])` is `"es"`, the coercion bug already recorded on `normalizeRole` and on a table's `access`.
- **ALL OF THEM SHARED ONE FAVICON** — a 231-byte dark square with a circle, shipped in the template's `public/`. The same mark in the tab, the bookmark bar and the phone home screen for a barber, a bakery and a builder. Each site now gets one derived from its NAME: up to two initials on a colour hashed from the name. **No upload, no theme dependency, no new user-facing surface**, and it fixes every existing site on its next publish.
- **THE HUE IS FNV-1a, NOT A SUM OF CHAR CODES.** A sum hashes "ab" and "ba" identically, and two salons on one street are exactly the pair that would collide. **Derived from the brand and NOT from the theme, deliberately**: an SVG favicon is fetched as its own document and cannot read a custom property off the page, so a mark that "matched the theme" would bake a colour the theme can then change underneath it.
- **`initials` IS TWO WORDS, NOT TWO LETTERS OF ONE.** "Zephyr" is Z, because "ZE" reads as an abbreviation of nothing while "Z" reads as a mark. Joining words are dropped or "Forno & Co" is F&. **Non-Latin names keep their own characters** — an `[A-Z]` filter gives a Greek or Cyrillic business an empty mark, which is the failure this exists to prevent.
- **THE ICON IS WRITTEN TO `icon.svg`, NEVER OVER `favicon.svg`.** The container is long-lived and serves every build on the platform, so overwriting the template's own file leaves no pristine copy and the first site's mark becomes the fallback for every brandless site after it. Deleted before every build for the reason `src/routes` is. Asserted live: two concurrent builds each get their own mark.
- **THE DESIGNER WAS NEVER GOING TO BE TOLD WHAT LANGUAGE THE SITE IS.** `currentStateNote` is a hand-written list and `EDIT_FIELDS` is not, so a seventh field was a value the model could CHANGE while never being shown what it already was — and the note and the tool schema are both in English, so a designer not told a site is Spanish has every reason to answer `en` and relabel a live site on a request that was only about a colour. **The `publicView` failure twice and the schema digest before it.** Now DERIVED: a test builds a look with every `EDIT_FIELDS` key and requires the note to state each one. Mutation-checked.
- **THE MARK'S SAFETY IS THAT IT CANNOT CONTAIN ANYTHING BUT LETTERS.** `initials` takes the FIRST character of each word and keeps only words starting with a Unicode letter, so `<script>alert(1)</script>` becomes `SA`. The `esc()` around it can never fire — kept, and **said so in the source**, since a guard that reads local and is not is worse than none. The test asserts the real property (letters only, adversarially driven) rather than that escaping happened.
- **THE CJK MARK OVERFLOWED ITS BOX AND ONLY A RENDER COULD SAY SO.** A CJK glyph is ~1em wide against ~0.68em for a Latin capital, so two at the Latin size measure 30 of the 32px box and touch the rounded corners. Valid SVG, correct code, looks broken — **the grey-charts lesson**, found by rendering sixteen marks at 16/32/64px. `isWide` is deliberately narrow: Greek, Cyrillic, Arabic and Devanagari all fit at the Latin size and widening it would shrink marks that are already right.
- **MY FIRST INTEGRATION ASSERTION WAS A SPELLING AND WENT RED ON A CORRECT BUILD.** It matched `href="/icon.svg"`; the template sets vite `base: "./"`, so every asset reference is emitted relative. It asserts the PROPERTY now — the href must resolve to a file this build actually published, which is what stops a head pointing at a 404. **Assert the property, not the spelling**, fifth time this week.
- **NOTED AND NOT CHASED:** that relative base means a NESTED route would resolve `./assets/…` to `/services/assets/…` and 404 — the whole bundle, not the mark, and pre-existing. **Measured: zero of the 318 family exemplars declare a nested route id**, so the model has never been shown one. Latent, not live.
- **`og:image` WAS PARTLY REFUTED on checking** — `siteOgImage` picks the owner's first upload and `injectMeta` publishes it, so the gap is only that the owner cannot CHOOSE which. **A LOGO IN THE HEADER IS CONFIRMED AND DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT**: `SiteChrome` takes `name: string` with no image slot, and choosing which picture has no mechanism — uploads are content-hashed, so there is no filename to match on. Half-building it would mean a picker nobody designed.
- **THE SWEEP'S THREE SURVIVORS WERE ALL REAL, and one repeats a lesson from the day before.** (1) `/icon\.svg/` **matches `favicon.svg`** — it is a substring of the thing it was written to forbid, so a mutant writing the mark over the template's own file passed, and the second assertion could not see it either because the write is `writeFileSync(iconPath, svg)`, a variable rather than a literal. Anchored on the whole filename now. (2) Deleting `"lang"` from `EDIT_FIELDS` **shrank every guard that loops over it** — the `EDIT_LAYERS` failure from 2026-08-11, one day later: **a derived check cannot be derived from the thing being mutated.** The replacement is anchored on the CONSUMER — every `look.<field>` `worker.js` reads must be one `mergeLook` produces, since a field the Worker reads and the merge does not emit is silently `undefined` on every site — and is driven from both ends, because a guard that cannot be shrunk by editing the list must still notice a Worker that stops reading. (3) The third was **INERT**: `slice(0, 2)` of a one-element array already yields one letter, so the `if (words.length === 1)` early return changed no answer for any name. **Deleted rather than tested**, because a line that cannot affect the output reads as a decision being enforced when nothing is enforcing it. **33/33 caught** across the two sweeps, from a verified-green baseline.
- 2288 unit tests, **`site build` 85/85**, including the language and the mark read back off the REAL prerendered pages and a build naming no language proved to keep the one it had. Two of those runs collided on port 8123 because I started the second before the first had exited — `EADDRINUSE`, a dead build server, and a "1 failed" that was mine and not the code's. **A test harness that binds a fixed port cannot be run twice at once**, which is worth knowing before reading its output as a result.

## The schema was WRITE-ONCE, and that was the biggest hole in the ladder (2026-08-11)

**Owner's call: "for edit it needs to be able to edit anything it can build."** Audited on that basis, and the gap was ONE thing rather than a list — **everything the designer can declare at build time was frozen the moment the build finished.**

- **MEASURED THROUGH THE REAL NORMALISER, NOT INFERRED.** The addon lane merges with `normalizeSchema`, whose rule is *"first declaration wins, and later ones contribute any COLUMNS the first did not have"* — written for the BUILD path, where two tables of one name is a model mistake and first-wins is the protection that stopped a duplicate destroying the first's guarantees. Driven with an existing table and a change to it: `columns: name, phone` (the new column survives) · **`confirm DROPPED · payment DROPPED · noOverlap DROPPED`**. So a table on a live site could gain a column and nothing else, ever — **and silently, which is worse than refused.**
- **WHAT THAT MADE IMPOSSIBLE, and every one is a first-week request:** email the customer when they book · text them · stop two people taking one slot (`unique`/`noOverlap`, and this file already records two customers booking the same 14:00) · **let people browse the listings without signing in** (the read/write pair, which has already cost a whole build — the marketplace whose every listing was private came out as the placeholder) · one entry per customer · cap the entries · **close the form** (`retired` was built, enforced, and settable by NOTHING).
- **`builder/site-rules.mjs` IS THE SIXTH EDIT LAYER, AND IT IS DEFINED BY WHAT THE PAGES NEVER SEE.** Every rule is enforced in Postgres or read out of `_meta` on the request path, so switching one on changes no page source, needs no container and publishes nothing — one Haiku call and one schema apply, ~0.3 credits. That is what makes it a rung BELOW `look` rather than a variant of `addon`. A rule that needs a page (a Buy button, a form field) is an addon and is turned away here rather than half-done.
- **THREE REFUSALS, each because the schema engine would otherwise skip the rule SILENTLY and report success.** `noOverlap` needs two INTEGER columns — the engine `console.error("noOverlap skipped")`s on anything else — so it is refused with `unique` named as the answer, on the one table where being wrong means two customers in one chair. A DDL constraint **cannot be cleared by omission**, because `applySiteSchema` is additive and the index would still be standing, so `CLEARABLE` is `confirm`/`sms` (read from `_meta`) and a constraint is refused with a reason. And a recipient column that does not exist: a table that looks as though it confirms and does not is the failure nobody finds until a customer says they never got an email.
- **`mergeRules` IS NOT `normalizeSchema`'S MERGE AND MUST NOT BECOME IT.** Two different intents, so two functions rather than a flag on one — the caller should have to say which it means. It **never removes a column and never drops a table**: `_meta` is what the data API derives from, so dropping a column hides it from every read while the values sit in Postgres, which is invisible data loss. Closing a table is `retired`, which keeps the rows.
- **ONE HALF OF THE PAIR MOVES ON ITS OWN AND NOTHING COMPLETES IT** — `resolveAccess` takes the absent half from the preset `access` names, which is still the table's own. Writing both halves looks tidier and is a second opinion about a question `site-access.mjs` exists to answer once.
- **THE "EDIT CANNOT REACH THE SCHEMA" GUARD BECAME A SCOPE, FOR THE SECOND TIME.** It listed `generateSitePages` until the `page` layer landed, then `applySiteSchema` until this one. A guard that forbids the feature protects nothing; what it holds now is that the apply happens in **exactly one branch**, that the branch publishes nothing, and that `seedSiteRows` stays absolutely forbidden — **a rule change that fabricates a customer's booking is the worst thing this lane could do.**
- **A DERIVED CHECK CANNOT BE DERIVED FROM THE THING BEING MUTATED.** Every layer guard loops over `EDIT_LAYERS`, so DELETING a name from that list shrinks what they check: removing `"rules"` survived all 2153 tests with the whole layer unreachable. The new guard reads the `eLayer === "…"` branches the Worker really has and requires the router to offer each — the tenth recorded instance of a guard watching the layer below the break.
- **THE OTHER TWO GAPS CLOSED THE SAME DAY, and both were the same shape one layer over.** The addon lane concatenated `[...prior, ...designed]` into `normalizeSchema`, so on an existing table `payment` and `publicView` were dropped silently — `mergeAddonSchema` keeps the columns-only behaviour that was right and adds exactly those two, the ones that need a PAGE as well as a schema change. **A COMPELLED FIELD IS NOT A STATEMENT OF INTENT**: `design_schema` requires `["name","access","columns"]`, so a designer asked to make `bookings` payable MUST answer `access`, and trusting that answer would publish every customer's phone number — `access`/`read`/`write` are therefore ignored on a table that already exists, and moving them is the `rules` layer, whose tool leaves them optional. The same route also passed `{tables}` alone, so **`functions`, `apis` and `jobs` never reached `normalizeSchema` at all** — the whole "the model writes the backend" tier unreachable after a first build (no inbound webhook handler, no confirmation computed by SQL, no third-party read). Not the worse failure it looked like: `applySiteSchema` already seeds `_meta` from the stored spec, so an addon never DESTROYED an existing function.
- **`builder/site-picture.mjs` IS THE SEVENTH LAYER, and the ALT TEXT is the handle.** A generated page already writes `alt="The row of chairs, Saturday morning"` for a screen reader, which is exactly the sentence to match *"the one of the chairs"* against — nothing new is stored to address a picture, and the swap is a string replacement at a known offset, so there is no page model call. **THE FREE HALF IS THE USEFUL HALF TODAY**: every `SafeImage` on every published site is drawing its placeholder because the image balance has never been funded, so the owner's OWN uploads are what fills them and the tool is told to prefer one. A mapped picture is SKIPPED — `<SafeImage alt={s.alt}/>` inside a map is one element rendering many, with no single span and no sentence to match. A slot is matched on page AND description, never description alone: two pages legitimately carry the same picture, and matching on the alt would change whichever the scan found first, on a page nobody mentioned. A picture that cannot be made leaves its slot and the others still change.
- **THE OVERLAPPING-WINDOW BUG, for the third time.** Two guards sliced a branch to a NAMED neighbour (`rules` → `text`, `data` → `text`); inserting the `picture` branch between them put a recompiling layer inside both windows and turned two correct changes red. The window is derived from whichever branch comes next now, so an eighth layer cannot repeat it.
- **STILL MISSING FROM EDIT:** per-page share previews, which are site-level only.
- 2156 tests, **25/25 mutations from a verified-green baseline**. Two survivors on the first sweep were both real: the `EDIT_LAYERS` one above, and a no-match that answered with no sentence at all — the path where the refusal reason is the only thing the customer gets. **NOT PROVEN LIVE** — the Anthropic account is empty.

## Read and write are two axes, not five bundles (2026-08-10, owner's call)

**Asked to fix the design step generally rather than patch the marketplace case, and the general fault is the vocabulary.** `display` does not mean "public read" — it means "public read AND no visitor writes". `collect` does not mean "anyone writes" — it means "anyone writes AND nobody reads". They are bundles, not axes, so the five levels are five points in a 4×4 grid and **any cell nobody pre-bundled is not merely missing, it is inexpressible.**

- **The commonest missing cell is "anyone reads, members write their own"** — listings, classifieds, a job board, a directory, public reviews, a community wall. A marketplace built the same night had no browsable page for exactly this reason: the designer read *"anything a visitor keeps as theirs"*, correctly chose `user`, and produced a site whose every listing was invisible to the visitors it existed for.
- **THE SAFETY ARGUMENT IS THAT THE PRESETS EMIT WHAT THEY ALWAYS DID.** This rewrote the security layer of every published site, so the five names were diffed against the previous implementation before the change was kept: **grants byte-identical across all 20 cases, policies differing only by inert parentheses on `feed`.** Proven by diff first, asserted as properties after — a captured string would stop meaning anything the next time the SQL legitimately changes.
- **`read: "none"` EMITS NO SELECT POLICY AND NO SELECT GRANT**, which is the write-only guarantee promoted from a property of the NAME `collect` to a property of the axis. The protection is the ABSENCE of the statement, so no filter in the wrong clause can weaken it, and `write: "anyone"` still gets INSERT and nothing else on every pair — a cell that opens the reads cannot quietly open the edits with them.
- **Four bugs found by checks rather than by reading, three of them mine, one pre-existing.** The marketplace cell granted SELECT only to `authenticated` — the member branch returned early and discarded the anonymous grant, so the one cell the whole change exists for published a table the public still could not read. `read: "own"` with `write: "none"` named `owner_id` in a policy the engine stamped only for own-scoped WRITES, so it referenced a column that did not exist, failed to CREATE, and left the table invisible. The normaliser dropped `read`/`write` silently — the allow-list trap that left `teamScope` dead for months. And **`access: ["display"]` was accepted**, because `String(["display"])` is `"display"` — the coercion bug already recorded for `normalizeRole`, on the field deciding who may read a business's customer data.
- **BOTH HALVES OR IT IS TIER B.** The policy layer can express all sixteen cells; without a slot in `design_schema` none of it could ever be asked for, which is precisely the state 14 other schema features are in — fully built, enforced, reachable by nothing. The tool takes `read`/`write`, the digest states the PAIR (naming the preset beside it when there is one), and the case with no shorthand is pointed at explicitly, because the five presets were each described accurately and the missing combination was described nowhere — the same failure as `publicView`, whose description named only the booking slot.
- **Two existing guards were pinned to the old spelling and went red on a correct change.** The `teamScope` one is re-anchored as what its own comment says it protects: **an agreement between the policy and the column**, derived from both files rather than pinned in either — and paired with a check that both are still narrow, since two conditions can agree perfectly and both be wrong.
- 1913 tests, **16/16 mutations caught** from a verified-green baseline. **Not proven live** — no site has been built on a pair yet.

**FOUR OF OUR OWN FEATURES WERE UNREACHABLE, AND FIVE MORE WERE DEAD (2026-08-10).** None of this comes with Postgres — every one is SQL this engine writes out of primitives, so nothing else will ever fix it. Audited before exposing, which is the part worth keeping:

- **EXPOSED, because they work end to end:** `oncePerUser` (a real unique index — one review per customer), `enforceRefs` (a real trigger — no booking for a deleted event), `expires`, `scheduled`.
- **DELIBERATELY NOT EXPOSED, because they are not reachable:** `sequence` creates a column and **no trigger stamps it**; `checks` is copied into `_meta` and emits **no DDL at all** (an earlier 900-character scan false-positived on a neighbouring statement — the count of "14 built and enforced" was too high); `audit` and `history` build tables the Worker reads in **zero** places; `version` is a lock `rows.ts` **never sends**. Offering any of them is the dead-feature trap one layer up — a slot that looks like a capability and does nothing. Asserted as an ABSENCE, so adding one later has to come with the wiring.
- **`expires` AND `scheduled` WERE HALF-DELETED BY THE NEON MIGRATION, and their own comments still claimed otherwise** — *"reads hide rows past it"*, *"hidden until this time"*. That filtering lived in the Worker's read path, deleted 2026-07-30; the columns stayed and the `WHERE` did not. So an expiring offer never expired and a scheduled post was public the moment it was written.
- **FIXED IN THE POLICY, NOT IN THE PROMPT** — the `safe-image` argument applied to data. A rule the generator must remember on every list of every page is one it will eventually forget, and forgetting means a shop advertising last month's price. A policy cannot be forgotten, applies to the owner's dashboard and the public API alike, and is one clause instead of a paragraph.
- **`IS NULL OR` IS THE CLAUSE THAT DECIDES WHETHER THIS IS USEFUL OR CATASTROPHIC.** `expires_at > now` is NULL for an unset column, which is not true — so without it every row that never had a date set would vanish. Mutation-checked in exactly that form.
- **Filters ride the READ policy only.** An owner must still be able to correct a scheduled post before it appears, and edit a listing that has expired.
- 1916 tests, **5/5 mutations caught**.

**AND THE ONE FACT NO PER-TABLE LINE COULD STATE (2026-08-10).** `schemaDigest` described every table accurately and never said the thing that decided the build: **whether a signed-out visitor can read anything at all.** On the marketplace that failed, `events` was private per member, `bookings` write-only and `reviews` members-only — three correct descriptions adding up to a site with no page worth opening, and the generator returned nothing rather than something.

- **A STATEMENT OF FACT, NOT A RULE, which is what makes it safe to add.** It cannot false-alarm: an internal tool legitimately has no public tables and the sentence is right for that site too — build the sign-in first. Refusing the schema instead would be wrong for every such site, and this file already records that a check wrong a third of the time is worse than no check.
- **`publicView` COUNTS AS READABLE**, or a correctly-built marketplace would be told it cannot be built — the one instruction guaranteed to stop it working. Asserted, along with the pair-declared form, so neither route to a public table is missed.
- **Also settled, and it corrects an earlier suspicion of mine:** the customer's raw brief — clarify answers folded in — DOES reach page generation (`pagesPrompt` opens with `"BRIEF\n" + brief`). So *"Trendy and modern"* is not consumed by the designer and discarded; the step that draws reads the same words. The thin frontend vocabulary is not the gap it looked like.
- 1917 tests, **4/4 mutations caught**.
